# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2007

## Introduction by Dravida dāsa

This year, as I gathered the homages that would appear in the Vyāsa-pūjā book, I realized that the yearly sacrifice is like an expanded *guru-pūjā*. What is the daily *guru-pūjā* but a formal offering of thanks, praise, and prayer to Śrīla Prabhupāda? And what is the yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book but an expanded offering of precisely these sentiments? Especially I thought that the two lines at the end of the second stanza of Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura’s profound song, sung daily for Śrīla Prabhupāda in ISKCON centers worldwide, form the perennial basis of the homages in the book:

*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei sei uttama-gati,*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

**Attachment to the guru’s lotus feet is the best way to make spiritual advancement. His mercy fulfills all our spiritual desires.**

The wonderful thing about these truths is that they are absolute — they are as true for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s granddisciples as they are for his direct disciples, and will be true for the many generations of his sincere followers yet to come. There is no consideration of time, place, circumstance, or candidate. And indeed we see the deep love for Śrīla Prabhupāda that empowers steady devotional service, as well as the heartfelt appreciation for his mercy as a necessity in one’s daily spiritual life and preaching efforts, expressed equally by old and new disciples alike. After all, every sincere member of ISKCON is a disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda: “disciple: a person who is an adherent of the doctrines of another.”

But this dry dictionary definition of *disciple* doesn’t begin to convey the true relationship between Śrīla Prabhupāda and his followers. The stark fact is that Śrīla Prabhupāda dedicated his life to giving us the most valuable thing there could ever be — Kṛṣṇa. For those who appreciate that invaluable gift, even slightly, the floodgates of gratitude and love for Śrīla Prabhupāda must open. These sentiments find expression in the homages printed here.

Some highlights:

Recalling an incident from almost thirty-five years ago, Śivarāma Swami remembers an ecstatic experience occasioned by hearing Śrīla Prabhupāda sing *VibhāvarīŚeṣa:*

Then you repeat, once, twice, thrice, *jāmuna-jīvana, keli-parāyaṇa,* and tears glide uncontrollably down my cheeks. I sob, I shiver, my heart cannot contain what it feels.

Is it bliss?

More than bliss, it is bliss induced by Kṛṣṇa’s presence. Kṛṣṇa is present by your voice. Kṛṣṇa is in the van, or wsherever it is that I am.

It can’t be. But it is. And at the moment it is an indisputable reality. Forget that I joined only months ago, forget that only the realized see Kṛṣṇa, forget any arguments — Kṛṣṇa is here.

And then, when the song is over, you slowly, purposefully explain every word, every line. But your words are not a purport. They are the incantations of a transcendental magician, who with each letter, each syllable, each word, manifests Kṛṣṇa and Vṛndāvana before me.

Dānavīr Goswami gives an encouraging report from India:

ISKCON flourishes in India. Your followers widely preach in universities throughout the country. Several thousand students attend an annual three-day ISKCON seminar with leading devotees. The *brahmacārī āśrama* is alive and well in your Indian centers, insuring extensive preaching for the present and fine spiritual leadership for the future.

It was so enlivening to see thousands of visitors coming to the ISKCON centers daily. Indeed, on one regular Sunday, 135,000 visitors came to the ISKCON Juhu temple. In December, the Delhi and Juhu temples distributed 112,000 and 150,000 hardback *Gītās,* respectively. The standards of cleanliness, management, Deity worship, and preaching are superb, yet the leader of one such temple humbly asked how granddisciples could ever perform substantial service to the Lord.

Gorgeous new temples such as Ujjain and Tirupati open regularly and immediately become the cynosures of their cities. Not only one temple per city, but many ISKCON temples flourishing in Delhi and Mumbai demonstrate a “big-ISKCON” vision of cooperation and expansion.

I heard one of your disciples who is a temple president in India deliver a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class that was so śāstrically astute that I could not help thinking how pleased you would be to hear it. All in all, it seems that ISKCON India has set the standard for our movement, and thus naturally other parts of the world should emulate its success.

The devotees in Brisbane, Australia, made an appeal to Śrīla Prabhupāda that typified many of the offerings:

We at the Brisbane *yātrā* ask you for the following blessings:

- That we seriously study your books and distribute them profusely.
- That we care about each other and inspire one another to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.
- That we ourselves become pure by attentively chanting the holy names and distributing this reservoir of pleasure to everyone.
- That we carry out all your orders co-operatively and in the right spirit.

Above all, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that we can follow your very high standards in a co-operative mood, so that our children, their children, and the generations to come will get the nectar of your pure loving nature.

And finally, this little gem from Vijaya Dāsa, the minister of book distribution:

One disciple of yours has been in a wheelchair for years with no legs, but he has never stopped distributing your books. Recently, while I was at the San Diego temple distributing books with a nice young *bhakta* named Bronson, I asked him how he had come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He said he had received a book from the devotee in a wheelchair. His name is Rādhānātha Dāsa.

A few drops from the nectar-ocean of this year’s Vyasa-puja book. *Śrī-guru-caraṇe ati, ei sei uttama-gati, ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā . .* .

Draviḍa Dāsa  
Vyāsa-pūjā book editor

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Viśākhā Devī Dāsī

Bona fide followers revere their bona fide spiritual master every day of every year. Yet the anniversary of the spiritual master’s appearance in this world — Vyāsa-pūjā— is the extraordinary day when his followers fully express their debt to him and fully celebrate their honor of him. “Vyāsa” refers to Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the original compiler of the *Vedas,* and “Pūjā” is the worship offered to Vyāsadeva’s pure representative, that person who is part of the disciplic succession — a long, continuous chain of spiritual teachers. By holding fast to the end of this chain, each of us can be pulled out of this world, where time slowly destroys all that we see.

With pride we acknowledge that our spiritual master, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, is our indispensable guide to everything everywhere and at all times. He uproots the so-called truths a mundane society firmly entrenched in us, and as we patiently hear from him, he gradually frees us from all sorts of false notions. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s perception is so clear, so unflinchingly based on the words of Vyāsadeva, so outrageously defiant of the prosaic life we would otherwise be immersed in, that with enormous pleasure we offer *pūjā* to him with all the humility we can muster and follow him according to the purity of our intent. We worship him for fearlessly saying what is shocking — what a nondevotee would never say — and yet what is unquestionably true and what refines our awareness. Eagerly we receive the revolutionary knowledge Śrīla Prabhupāda transmits from the transcendent world he lives in. To appreciate Śrīla Prabhupāda’s selfless efforts to give us what his spiritual master gave him, to attend to the purposes, values, and meanings he advances, to remember the heritage he bequeathed us, which will guide generations to come, and to attentively do the simplest yet hardest thing of all — chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy — is to understand the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā. Our homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda is our entrance into the spiritual culture he kindly offers us all, without distinction.

However, Vyāsa-pūjā is also something more. It is yet another chance for us to be released from what Śrīla Prabhupāda says is “our present causeless unwillingness for serving the Absolute and all souls.”

Our unwillingness to yield ourselves fully — mind, words, and body — to the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and the *jīvātmās* has no substantial philosophical, rational, religious, or even righteous cause. Śrī Yāmunācārya reflects on our wretched, stupefying unwillingness:

O Hari! I have no faith in religious principles, and I have no knowledge of the soul. Furthermore, I have no devotion to Your lotus feet. I am utterly destitute, a rogue with no idea of the goal of life. In this way I always cheat myself. \[*Stotra-ratna* 8\]

The unparalleled annual celebration of Vyāsa-pūjā can rekindle the submissive service attitude essential to spiritual progress. If we hear his transcendental vibrations without unnecessary opposition, Śrīla Prabhupāda will surely have mercy upon us. Awakened to his sublime wisdom, to his transcendental qualities, and to how much he cares about us, we are captivated; we long to be with him. As our love for him grows, we willingly follow his directives. He, in turn, strikes our spark of love for Kṛṣṇa.

Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa says, “You should accept a highly elevated *paramahaṁsa,* a spiritually advanced spiritual master. In this way, you should place your faith and love in Me, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.5.10) Kṛṣṇa’s grace can shine upon those who love His servants and Him. Just as fire in the stomach digests all that we eat, so sincere devotional service and the causeless grace of Kṛṣṇa dissolve causeless unwillingness. Quietly we discover the exalted nature of selfless service and find stalwart, irresistible reasons to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Their servants. Even more, we don’t need reasons; with joy we serve because there is absolutely nothing else worthwhile, because we cannot do otherwise.

Śrīla Prabhupāda writes:

It is a basic principle that one must accept a bona fide spiritual master in order to achieve the highest perfection of life, love of God. I thank all of you very much for accepting me as your spiritual master, and I promise that I will take you back to home, back to Godhead. I ask you all to promise me to always chant at least 16 rounds, follow the regulative principles, read our books and try to preach this Krishna Consciousness Movement all over the world. So far my qualifications are concerned, I am simply trying to carry out the order of my Guru Maharaja. \[Letter to Nityānanda, 12 November 1971\]

The meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā is to simply try, with love and devotion, to carry out the order of Śrīla Prabhupāda on this day and forever more.

Viśākhā Devī Dāsī

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

The story is told in your *Līlāmṛta* how you went with a few early disciples and followers to the Pacific Ocean for an evening beach party, complete with bonfire, baked potatoes, and *kīrtana*. The devotees present, as well as those who hear your pastimes today, were moved at how such a great spiritual master and emissary of God could find time and patience to indulge his students with such an affectionate, and seemingly frivolous, activity.

But you had fun with your spiritual children, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and they loved you all the more for it.

In this and a million other ways, you showed us how the full range of emotions and human interactions can be spiritualized and become sources of joy in this otherwise dreary and deceptive world. Our commands from you are thus threefold: 1) to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, 2) to preach enthusiastically, and 3) to love, and exemplify love, among ourselves and toward other souls lost in forgetfulness.

This year in Śrīdāma Māyāpur, your GBC members carved out special time for the third of these principles. As on your trip to the beach, we played and had fun together. We spent a day in mock debates, we acted out *līlās* of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya, we chanted, we laughed, we feasted, and we embraced.

We had fun together, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we loved you all the more because of it.

Some in your society, Prabhupāda, are trying hard to be Kṛṣṇa conscious, but don’t have much joy in their lives. Some work exhaustively to preach, but aren’t developing much love. Some seek frivolity and occasional chanting, but don’t take your mission very seriously.

To become pure, and to become the force in this world that you want us to be, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need balance in our lives and maturity in our hearts. We need to understand you, and the example you set for us, with broad vision. We need faith, but we must not be rigid. We need austerity, but we also need compassion. We need enthusiasm, but we must not lose patience. You have shown us how such greatness is possible. You have not only shown us, but you have ordered us to strive for it in our daily lives.

If we wish to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we must become a society where *gṛhasthas, sannyāsīs,* and *brahmacārīs,* Indians and Westerners, men and women, and rich and poor alike all respect, appreciate, and care for one another. We are only the first of many generations of Vaiṣṇavas to serve and expand ISKCON. We may not transform the entire world, but we can, if we work together, please you by building a strong foundation for the future.

To do so we need to feel genuine pain in our hearts when we see the sufferings and struggles of others in this world, as do you. We need to love others and be willing to give our lives to help them, as do you. We need to feel Kṛṣṇa’s presence at every moment and see His influence in the hearts of all living beings, as do you.

It is only by remembering you, by hearing from you, and by serving you and your ISKCON society that we can make progress. You are our founder and our *ācārya*. As long as we remember you, we will be safe, happy, and effective. And we will very soon rejoin the Supreme Lord, whom you have kindly revealed to us as Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord of all playfulness and love.

Your servant,  
Anuttama Dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We have just concluded the annual Śrīla Prabhupāda Memorial Festival in New Dvārakā. I mentioned to one of the organizers what a blissful event it is, and he replied, “That is because we have the best theme — glorifying Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

One of the guests at the festival, a well-known Hollywood actor (now brought to reflection by the fading of his fame), asked me, “What struck you most about Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

Growing up as a boy, I devoured books filled with tales of great yogīs, pilgrimages to Tibet, mystics of medieval Europe, and descriptions of elevated states of consciousness. I imagined what it would be like to meet someone free from lust, greed, self-righteous anger, someone completely satisfied personally and who only wanted to help others. But it all remained theoretical, distant, and unobtainable for me — until I met you.

With your consciousness fixed in transcendence yet with both feet firmly on the ground, you manifested all the above, wrapped in warmth, humility, and a keen wit. Your very presence bridged the gap between pure love of God and the conditioned soul’s state of petty and paltry existence. You were filled with the happiness that comes from remembrance of Kṛṣṇa at every moment, and, amazingly, you assured us that we could be too if we simply followed your words and example.

I have never met anyone else like you, before or since. Having left your spiritual home of Vṛndāvana (you reflected once in a morning class: “You cannot know what happiness I was experiencing living there in those days”), you sailed out on the *Jaladuta*, facing an unknown future and precarious health, simply to save us.

How can I ever repay you? All I can do is try my best to dedicate what remains of my life to you. Even in this effort, I have to appeal to you for your mercy. Please guide, chastise, correct, and inspire me so that I may someday become a worthy son of such a glorious father.

Only by your grace and tolerance, I remain  
Your servant,  
Badrinārāyaṇ Dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and your mission of delivering all the fallen souls of Kali-yuga.

Recently your disciple Kṛṣṇa Dāsa Swami Mahārāja kindly presented me with a copy of the *Kalki Purāṇa,* and I’ve been reading it and noting the graphic descriptions of the further developments of Kali-yuga. You came to us as the representative of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and you have saved us from those horrors. If it were not for you, we would have been first-class candidates for the mercy of Lord Kalki and His sword, but unfortunately that is not as sweet as the mercy of Lord Caitanya, which you have given us.

Of course, much of what is found in the *Kalki Purāṇa* is told in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which begins describing Kali-yuga as follows:

Then, O King, religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, tolerance, mercy, duration of life, physical strength, and memory will all diminish day by day because of the powerful influence of the Age of Kali. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.2.1\]

Some of these things we are already observing on a daily basis. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (12.2.7–8) says,

As the earth thus becomes crowded with a corrupt population, whoever among any of the social classes shows himself to be the strongest will gain political power. The citizens, having lost their wives and property to such avaricious and merciless rulers, who will behave no better than ordinary thieves, will flee to the mountains and forests.

We all know how your followers in Kazakhstan have been harassed by the local government to the point where your movement there has been forced almost to the point of having to go underground again, as happened in the Communist period in that part of the world.

Then *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (12.2.10) says, “The citizens will suffer greatly from cold, wind, heat, rain, and snow.”

The sinful activities of the population have made global warming a reality. This phenomenon is now transforming the world despite the efforts of some governments to deny the obvious, since doing so would necessitate policies that would infringe on their efforts to further industrialize and create more sense gratification.

Then *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (12.2.11) says, “The maximum duration of life for human beings in Kali-yuga will become fifty years.”

Here in South Africa, the average life expectancy is now less than forty-five years, so this place epitomizes Kali-yuga. What has caused this disaster? AIDS, or in other words, illicit sex.

These are a few of the many glimpses into Kali-yuga given in the Twelfth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Elsewhere, however, the *Bhāgavatam* offers hope:

*nādhi-vyādhi-jarā-glāni- duḥkha-śoka-bhaya-klamāḥ*  
*mṛtyuś cānicchatāṁ nāsīd rāme rājany adhokṣaje*

**When Lord Rāmacandra, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, was the king of this world, all bodily and mental suffering, disease, old age, bereavement, lamentation, distress, fear, and fatigue were completely absent. There was even no death for those who did not want it. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **9.10.53\]**

In your purport to this verse, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you present the real solution:

All these facilities existed because of Lord Rāmacandra’s presence as the King of the entire world. A similar situation could be introduced immediately, even in this age called Kali, the worst of all ages. It is said, *kali-kāle nāma-rūpe kṛṣṇa-avatāra:* Kṛṣṇa descends in this Kali-yuga in the form of His holy name — Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rāma. If we chant offenselessly, Rāma and Kṛṣṇa are still present in this age. The kingdom of Rāma was immensely popular and beneficial, and the spreading of this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement can immediately introduce a similar situation, even in this Kali-yuga.

In a purport two verses before this you say,

Even if one is sinful and cannot give up sinful life immediately, if he chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* with devotion and faith he will certainly be freed from all sinful activities, and his life will be successful. *Paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam.* This is the blessing of Lord Rāmacandra, who has appeared in this Age of Kali as Lord Gaurasundara.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have access to the mercy of Lord Caitanya. Let us take this opportunity and dedicate our lives at your lotus feet, and let us try to help others get Lord Caitanya’s mercy also. The alternative — waiting for the mercy of Lord Kalki — is not so attractive.

Your humble servant eternally,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

How do I start to express your glory? I had no personal time with you, but somehow through your flock of devotees I was able to access some of your mercy. There cannot be a better expression of your love than these devotees — my godbrothers and -sisters. How would I describe them? When I first met these great souls, I saw them as people rich with reason and people rich with rhythm. They were bright, philosophical, and highly enthusiastic and artistic. I wanted to spend the rest of my life with persons of such caliber. I saw that they were mirroring your optimism. You were spreading something contagious, a genuine love for life, nothing artificial but something so practical and otherworldly at the same time.

You offered a complete wholesome culture of constant music, exotic edibles, colorful divine images, and great books and social intercourse. How is this gift of yours to be repaid?

Moreover, your gift of strength constantly finds its way to me. My greatest moments of weakness come when I feel an inability to cope with negativity, which sometimes originates with sectors of that original flock. You always maintained optimism. You were and are the most upbeat person I have ever known, and your optimism is what sustains me in the midst of occasional cynicism.

It would be wrong to say that in our guru-disciple relationship my end of the job is done, that I have said all I possibly could in regards to your kindness. Rather, I beg for your continuous assistance. I can see that my dependence on you will be a perpetual reality.

I always remain your insignificant servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am one of your granddisciples, always waiting for your causeless mercy. I have never seen you personally, but I always see you through your ISKCON, which is nondifferent from you. I am trying my best to serve Your Divine Grace by serving your society, ISKCON.

We are facing many problems. But you are our eternal inspiration, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because even though you faced so many more problems than we do, still you overcame all of them simply by depending on the mercy of your Guru Mahārāja. Similarly, we hope that by your causeless mercy we will overcome all the problems and succeed in doing something substantial to please you.

By your mercy, ISKCON Māyāpur, your world headquarters, is expanding despite so many hindrances. Please bless me and keep me on the proper track so that I can serve you better and better under the guidance of your disciples, who were so nicely trained by Your Divine Grace. Bless us to lead your ISKCON nicely, year after year.

Thank you very much.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā celebration!

I am eternally indebted to Your Divine Grace for saving me from the all-devouring jaws of materialistic civilization, which keeps everyone in darkness concerning their eternal relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Nowadays, masses of people are increasingly enamored of numerous technological playthings, which are perfect tools for forgetting their real self-interest. This morning we discussed your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.2.2. In that purport you perfectly describe such futile attempts of the living entity to find happiness by materialistic progress:

Conditioned souls are strictly under the laws of fruitive activities, and as such they sometimes go up to Brahmaloka and again come down to Pātālaloka, as if they were unintelligent children on a merry-go-round.

This merry-go-around example can also be applied to the economic, scientific, and technological advancement of a materialistic civilization. These are all childish attempts at finding happiness in a place where happiness can never be found.

Of course, technology is not inherently bad. You taught us that utility is the principle in our attempt to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and many devotees are using technological devices in their preaching work. The Internet world is increasing, and many devotees take advantage of it to distribute devotional items to thousands of people. When I asked one devotee in the country where I live what item is being ordered in large quantity, he replied that your books are being ordered the most.

Kindly bless us to be able to distribute your books and to continue spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in ever-new ways to ever-new people in ever-new places. In this way we will always have your association and be protected from straying from your lotus feet.

Your insignificant humble servant,  
A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your two lotus feet were firmly rooted in Goloka Vṛndāvana, even as you focused your vision on this world, fixed in service to your Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

With that vision, which was directly opposed to the vision of those of this world, you again and again boldly declared that all those who failed to surrender to Kṛṣṇa were crazy, blind fools and rascals.

People are already mad, and the so-called rascal leaders making them more and more mad. That’s all. *Andhā yathāndhair upanīyamānās*. So-called scientists, so-called religious leaders, all rascals. If we say that all rascals, we are only intelligent, \[laughter\] they will not be very happy. But that will be fact, try to understand it. \[Room Conversation, 10 March 1972, Vṛndāvana\]

This radical opinion, which you confidently and often proclaimed, was not your own. You were simply and faithfully repeating the viewpoint of Kṛṣṇa Himself:

Those miscreants who are grossly foolish, who are lowest among mankind, whose knowledge is stolen by illusion, and who partake of the atheistic nature of demons do not surrender unto Me. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 7.15\]

But indeed, you did boldly proclaim Kṛṣṇa’s conclusion, and indeed, you did use this criteria when judging a man’s worth:

So you make one test, whether he has surrendered to Kṛṣṇa. If he has not done, then he is in these four categories. What are they? *Duṣkṛtina,* great sinful; *mūḍha,* rascal; *narādhama,* lowest of the mankind. And if you say, “Oh, these are so big, big learned scholar, now *māyayāpahṛta-jñāna*? Why?”*Āsuraṁ bhāvam, asura*. *Asura’s* business is to deny Kṛṣṇa and to kill Kṛṣṇa. \[Morning Walk, 25 December 1976, Bombay\]

Yet the *Bhagavad-gītā* also denotes a fifth type of unsurrendered soul, a category that may cut through the other four divisions:

Again there are those who, although not conversant in spiritual knowledge, begin to worship the Supreme Person upon hearing about Him from others. Because of their tendency to hear from authorities, they also transcend the path of birth and death. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 13.26\]

Your purport details the deliverance of such souls::

This verse is particularly applicable to modern society because in modern society there is practically no education in spiritual matters. Some of the people may appear to be atheistic or agnostic or philosophical, but actually there is no knowledge of philosophy. As for the common man, if he is a good soul, then there is a chance for advancement by hearing. This hearing process is very important. Lord Caitanya, who preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the modern world, gave great stress to hearing because if the common man simply hears from authoritative sources he can progress, especially, according to Lord Caitanya, if he hears the transcendental vibration Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. It is stated, therefore, that all men should take advantage of hearing from realized souls and gradually become able to understand everything. . . . If one is fortunate enough to take shelter of a pure devotee, hear from him about self-realization, and follow in his footsteps, one will be gradually elevated to the position of a pure devotee. . . . Although the common man is often not as capable as so-called philosophers, faithful hearing from an authoritative person will help one transcend this material existence and go back to Godhead, back to home.

Your words above seem to describe main mission-services of ISKCON and ISKCON’s devotees: (1) To bestow fortune by allowing others to hear about Kṛṣṇa, and (2) To become authorized, learned, realized devotees.

To the extent that we, your followers, carry forward this dual mission of preaching and becoming pure, to that extent we can successfully assist you in fulfilling your mission.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thākuka, in his *Śaraṇāgati* songbook, describes the exalted longings of a servant-preacher able to fulfill your desires. He has placed this song alone, within its own category, termed “Vijñapti, Spiritual Request.”

1\. When, oh when will that day be mine? When my offenses ceasing, taste for the name increasing, when in my heart will your mercy shine? When, oh when will that day be mine?

2\. Lower than a blade of grass, more tolerant than a tree — when will my mind attain this quality? Respectful to all, not expecting their honor — then shall I taste the name’s nectar sublime. When, oh when will that day be mine?

3\. Great wealth or followers, feminine beauty — I won’t care for them or the comforts of my body. Birth after birth give me, O Lord Caitanya, causeless devotion to Your feet divine. When, oh when will that day be mine?

4\. When will I utter “Krsna, Krsna, Krsna” with words choked up and shivering body? When will I tremble all over and lose bodily color, tears pouring from my eyes? When, oh when will that day be mine?

5\. When in Navadvīpa along the Ganges bank, shouting “Gaura-Nityānanda!” as a surrendered soul, dancing, chanting, running everywhere, when will I become half mad of mind? When, oh when will that day be mine?

6\. When will Lord Nityānanda show mercy to me? When will I reject the world of Māyā? Bestow unto me the shade of Your lotus feet. Let the right to preach the name be mine. When, oh when will that day be mine?

7\. I will beg, borrow, or steal the nectar of the name. By the name’s effect I will feel paralyzed. O enjoyer of the nectar of the name, when will I touch your lotus feet till the end of time? When, oh when will that day be mine?

8\. When kindness to all beings will be appearing, with free heart forget myself comforting, Bhaktivinoda in all humility prays, “Now I will set out to preach Your order sublime.” When, oh when will that day be mine?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my consciousness is vastly distant from the consciousness you and your predecessors have prescribed. And it is also so far away from attachment to the required practices that would lead me to the cherished position of truly being your servant.

I am deeply embarrassed by this. What to do? I am already sixty years old, and there’s little hope of a drastic change within me. As your servant, I keep trying as I await your mercy. My only hope is your causeless mercy.

Hoping to remain at your feet,  
Bhūrijana Dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Many devotees ask me about my first meeting with and impression of Your Divine Grace.

The first time I met Your Divine Grace in this lifetime was in Gainesville, Florida, in 1971, during your visit to our small center. My dear godbrother Amarendra Prabhu had arranged for the university to sponsor your trip.

When I saw you exit from the vehicle at the temple, I immediately fell to the ground and offered my prostrated obeisances at your feet. Looking afterward at your visage, I experienced a wave of unlimited compassion emanating from you. I tried to hold back my tears as I experienced the deepest caring and love that I had ever encountered. Your desire to help us was without any personal motive. I then realized beyond a shadow of a doubt that you were my eternal spiritual father, who had come to this world for the sole purpose of rescuing souls like myself, who had forgotten their spiritual identities.

The above realization convinced me to join your transcendental army as a full-time soldier. My reluctance to serve faded away completely, and I became determined to serve your lotus feet eternally.

Now, thirty-six years after this life-changing event, I am still overwhelmed with the realization of your love and compassion, and this realization continues to inspire me to deepen my relationship with Your Divine Grace and to please you by reflecting some of that compassion toward those whom I come in contact with.

My understanding is that the more real empathy I feel for others, and the more I manifest this in practical action, the more you will be pleased with me.

Of course, the experience of real empathy is difficult to obtain. Empathy is more than sympathy, which is simply feeling bad that someone else is suffering. Empathy is to actually take others’ suffering as one’s own, and to be absorbed in alleviating that suffering. Vāsudeva Datta’s mood toward the conditioned souls is a perfect example of this compassion. He was willing to suffer here in this world so that others would be saved.

You are also a perfect example of this compassion. You are an ocean of compassion. The spiritual ocean of your compassion has no limits to it.

I am praying that more and more of this compassion will manifest in my heart. I beg Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and your divine self to grant me this gift of empathy. When I see the desire trees in Vṛndāvana, I tightly embrace them, asking for this boon.

This is the blessing I am imploring you to grant me. Please let me serve you in this way. Please let me offer you this mood of compassion for others from my heart. Please help me develop this mood.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servant,  
Bīr Krishna Dāsa Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Whenever I survey the gruesome wreckage of just this one birth, before I met you, my heart grieves. Then, to think that for countless births I’ve drowned in nescience, forgetting Prāṇanātha, the Lord of our life, the river of my torment flows. How many births, in how many species, on how many planets have I ignored Kṛṣṇa and His devotees?

We have to learn how to act only for Kṛṣṇa, how to love only Kṛṣṇa. Then our life is successful. And the human life . . . Because we have also come down from Vaikuṇṭha some millions and millions of years ago. *Anādi karama-phale. Anādi* means “before the creation” . . .

So this chance is given. So if this chance is misused, this life, human form of life, it is very, very risky. Again we will have to accept the cycle of birth and death. And not only that, if we do not fulfill the mission of life, then again there will be annihilation of the whole creation and we will have to stay within the body of Viṣṇu for millions and trillions of years. Again we will have to come. So therefore it is called *anādi karama-phale. Anādi* means “before the creation.” \[Lecture in London, 6 August 1973\]

Birth and death disconnected from devotional service? Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your illuminating grace, now I can conceive of no greater pain. Birth and death wholly in your service? Now, by your affectionate training, I’d accept that — just as long as upon my head rest only a few particles of dust from your divine lotus feet.

Sometimes I try to fathom the depths of your pure love for Kṛṣṇa. Then the reality of your mercy — how you put up with us creatures — takes over the meditation. Allow me to offer back to you your own words from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Let the whole world consider what kind of devotee writes these words of pure ecstasy, bathing in *caraṇāmṛta* the generations to follow:

Out of humility, a devotee considers himself unfit to be transferred to the spiritual world. He always thinks himself contaminated by the modes of material nature. Nor is there any need for a devotee to ask to be freed from the modes of material nature. Devotional service itself is in the transcendental position; therefore there is no question of asking for this special facility. The conclusion is that a pure devotee is not anxious to stop the repetition of birth and death, but is always eager to associate with other devotees who are engaged in chanting and hearing about the glories of the Lord. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.30.34, purport\]

Enshrining these words in my heart, through all the typical phases and tumults of life in this world, may I always cling to devotional service — the irreplaceable activity that renders even our requesting freedom from *triguṇa-mayī* irrelevant.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the opportunity to render selfless service is the greatest benediction of devotional service within your ISKCON. While coping with the ocean of ISKCON’s diversity, our leaders must work hard to keep this special facility — selfless service — alive and available, so that at least somewhere in the ISKCON marketplace there is always demand for it. After all, you did demonstrate how to make life a success — “how to get ahead”— by vigorously investing the self into the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya.

The *gopīs* were captivated by Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa was a young boy, very beautiful, and the *gopīs* were young girls. That is the superficial . . . Actually, the *gopīs* are eternal associates of Kṛṣṇa. *Ānanda-cinmaya-rasa-pratibhāvitābhiḥ*. They are expansions of Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure potency expansion. They are meant for Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure. They are not ordinary women. But superficially, just to teach us how to love Kṛṣṇa at the risk of anything . . . Therefore *gopīs,* when they were attracted by Kṛṣṇa at midnight . . . Kṛṣṇa was playing flute, and they became attracted and they left home. Some of them were locked up. They gave up their life even. They were so much attracted. \[Lecture in London, 6 August 1973, continued\]

Venerable Vaiṣṇavas may talk about this most exalted level of spiritual attainment, but you daily lived it — in plain view. For you such selfless service — loving Kṛṣṇa at any risk — was as routine as *dāl,* rice, and chapatis. And then you have the greatest compassion and expertise to properly teach your followers this zenith of *bhakti,* as you so carefully and lovingly lead us into this most rarified atmosphere.

Aspiring to be your unconditional servant,  
Devāmrita Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Today, as I meditate on you and your teachings, a verse comes to mind:

*tat te ’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo*  
*bhuñjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam*  
*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk*

**My dear Lord, one who earnestly waits for You to bestow Your causeless mercy upon him, all the while patiently suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds and offering You respectful obeisances with his heart, words, and body, is surely eligible for liberation, for it has become his rightful claim. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.14.8\]**

From the purport:

This unhappiness suffered by a sincere devotee is not technically a karmic reaction; it is rather the Lord’s special mercy for inducing His devotee to completely let go of the material world and return home, back to Godhead.

A sincere devotee earnestly desires to go back to the Lord’s abode. Therefore he willingly accepts the Lord’s merciful punishment and continues offering respects and obeisances to the Lord with his heart, words, and body. Such a bona fide servant of the Lord, considering all hardship a small price to pay for gaining the personal association of the Lord, certainly becomes a legitimate son of God, as indicated here by the words *dāya-bhāk*. Just as one cannot approach the sun without becoming fire, one cannot approach the supreme pure, Lord Kṛṣṇa, without undergoing a rigid purificatory process, which may appear like suffering but which is in fact a curative treatment administered by the personal hand of the Lord.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have often quoted this verse to explain the mind of such exalted devotees as Ajāmila, Vṛtrāsura, Citraketu, and Gajendra. Through the pastimes of these great souls and others, you have shown us the consciousness we will need as we meet the challenges in our devotional life. Now your disciples are getting older and facing disease and other difficulties. And finally, of course, we will have to leave our bodies. It is happening to us all. No one will be left out.

When I was young, your glorious departure was my only real-life example of chanting the holy name while leaving this world and returning to Goloka Vṛndāvana. But in recent years, I have also seen disciples and granddisciples of yours leave this world in spiritual consciousness. Some left suddenly, others endured illness first, but nearly all heard the holy name during their last moments, by their own chanting or by the chanting of others.

The mercy of the Lord always follows the mercy of His devotee, so I know that these disciples have pleased you by their many years of selfless service. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your most worshipable Lordships have made special arrangements for your followers to return home, back to Godhead. Those of us who have seen the departures of such elevated souls are gaining the courage, strength, and determination to face our own last moment as well.

Now my prayer is that by the mercy of these departed souls I may obtain the mercy of Your Divine Grace, followed by the mercy of the Divine Couple. In this way, I hope to finally understand the meaning of *dāsa-dāsa-anudāsa*.

Your humble servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

Of Whom Shall I Take Shelter But You?

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I shall begin with a verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that especially reminds me of you. It is the twenty-third verse of the Third Canto’s second chapter, “Remembrance of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

*aho bakī yaṁ stana-kāla-kūṭaṁ*  
*jighāṁsayāpāyayad apy asādhvī*  
*lebhe gatiṁ dhātry-ucitāṁ tato 'nyaṁ*  
*kaṁ vā dayāluṁśaraṇaṁ vrajema*

**Alas, how shall I take shelter of one more merciful than He who granted the position of mother to a she-demon \[Pūtanā\] although she was unfaithful and she prepared deadly poison to be sucked from her breast?**

\[Purport by Your Divine Grace:\] Here is an example of the extreme mercy of the Lord, even to His enemy. It is said that a noble man accepts the good qualities of a person of doubtful character, just as one accepts nectar from a stock of poison. In His babyhood, He was administered deadly poison by Pūtanā, a she-demon who tried to kill the wonderful baby. And because she was a demon, it was impossible for her to know that the Supreme Lord, even though playing the part of a baby, was no one less than the same Supreme Personality of Godhead. His value as the Supreme Lord did not diminish upon His becoming a baby to please His devotee Yaśodā. The Lord may assume the form of a baby or a shape other than that of a human being, but it doesn’t make the slightest difference: He is always the same Supreme. A living creature, however powerful he may become by dint of severe penance, can never become equal to the Supreme Lord. Lord Kṛṣṇa accepted the motherhood of Pūtanā because she pretended to be an affectionate mother, allowing Kṛṣṇa to suck her breast. The Lord accepts the least qualification of the living entity and awards him the highest reward. That is the standard of His character. Therefore, who but the Lord can be the ultimate shelter?

I thought of this verse because it glorifies your characteristic of being merciful, of seeing the good in others even in the midst of so much other stuff. And it expresses the way we feel: How can we take shelter of anyone else — how could we find one more merciful than you?

We all — at least I speak for myself — come with so much poison in us, and it is your glory and mercy that you could suck up the poison without being affected, and at the same time purify us. The poison is envy. Everyone in the material world is full of envy. We are here in the material world because of envy. Only a pure devotee such as you is free from this poison. In fact, as you told us, if one is free from envy he is liberated, because only a liberated soul can be completely free from envy.

So, we act out of envy (*jighāṁsayā*), and we are unfaithful (*asādhvī*). Unfaithful: We pretend, like Pūtanā, to be selfless servants, but in our own way we are unfaithful. We waver between our determination to serve and our desire to enjoy and exploit, between our determination to help others and our tendency to be selfish and neglect others or even harm them.

There are many similarities between Pūtanā and us, but although Pūtanā was envious and unfaithful and came to poison Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa did not consider the dark side. He saw the bright side, that she came to render service. “She came to suckle Me just like a mother.” So He took her as His mother and elevated her to that position in the spiritual world; she became His nurse, like a mother, one of the seven kinds of mothers. In the same way, you took the good side of whatever we did and magnified it.

You have explained that there are different grades of devotees. The lowest grade will find a little fault and make a big thing out of it. The next grade will see the good and the bad in a person but give more emphasis to the bad. And the next grade will see the good and the bad and give equal importance to both. But the more advanced devotee, although he or she sees both the good and the bad in a person, gives more emphasis to the good. And the most advanced will find a little good and make a big thing out of it. And that is the way you were. You could see in us that spark of desire to serve, and you would fan it. You were very expert and would protect the little spark. Sometimes if a flame is weak it can be extinguished. If it is dampened it may go out. And if it is blown upon too strongly it can go out. You were very expert at preserving that little spark and fanning it in just the right way to make it grow. And you promised and guaranteed that if we just give this life to Kṛṣṇa, we will be successful and go back home, back to Godhead.

You always directed us to Kṛṣṇa. You told us that a spiritual master does not say, “Surrender to me; I am God.” Rather, he says, “Surrender to Kṛṣṇa. I am an insignificant servant of God.” So you were unique, at least among the swamis and yogīs who came to the West before you or at about the same time. You were unique in that you were always directing us to Kṛṣṇa: “Do not surrender to me; surrender to Kṛṣṇa.” Once, one of your disciples, Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Prabhu, said to you, “I trust you and can surrender to you, but I do not trust anyone else. I cannot surrender to anyone else — any representative of yours — because I fear they may cheat me.” You replied, “Do not surrender to me. I will also cheat you. Just surrender to Kṛṣṇa.” Because you were fully surrendered to your spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa and always directed us to surrender to Kṛṣṇa, you actually were a perfect servant of Kṛṣṇa and a transparent medium. Therefore we had faith that we could surrender to you and that surrender to you was tantamount to surrender to Kṛṣṇa.

The same quality of Kṛṣṇa’s that is exalted in the verse from the *Bhāgavatam* quoted above — His extreme mercy — was fully manifest in Your Divine Grace. You were truly a transparent medium because you saw the good in us and appealed to the good. And the good in us is in fact us, because as souls, as parts and parcels of Kṛṣṇa, we are pure. It is just because we are covered by the material body and mind that there is impurity. So you would actually see our true natures as servants of Kṛṣṇa, eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa, and you would relate to us as eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa. And that inspired us to act as servants of Kṛṣṇa.

And often, your faith in a person or mercy toward a person brought great results. There was a young man who came to the Boston temple when I was there in 1970. His name was Peter, and Peter was eccentric — to say the least. In the old Boston temple, the front door opened into a hallway and a stairway up to the temple room. When new persons came, practically all they would see was the hallway, unless someone greeted them and directed them to the stairs up to the temple room. So, Peter made his home in the front hallway. He would keep all his books and personal paraphernalia scattered on the floor of the hallway. He would rest his back against the wall and stretch his legs out, completely blocking the hallway. And sometimes he would lie down and sleep in the hallway. He did not respond to normal instructions and thus gained the nickname “Crazy Peter.”

Still, we all knew your mood: you were most merciful. And especially in the early days of the movement in America, we considered that any soul who came to the temple was sent by Kṛṣṇa and was not ordinary, and we would really do everything we could to encourage the person. But with Peter things came to the stage where we thought that he was causing such a disruption that new people who came would be discouraged from Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We thought that maybe for the larger interest, for the greater good, we should just ask him to go. Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, the temple president, wrote to you and asked if we could ask Peter to leave. And you replied, “What’s the matter, can’t you tolerate?”

Thereafter, you returned to India, and you asked each temple to send one devotee to help you develop the movement there. I was chosen from Boston, and so I went. For years I never heard about Peter, nor did I think much about him. Then, after being in India for many years, I came back to visit the United States. And there, at the Los Angeles temple, during *guru-pūjā,* I saw Peter. I could not believe it. So many years had passed — you had left the planet — and there was Crazy Peter right in the temple room in Los Angeles.

I asked a nearby devotee, “Who is that?” He answered, “Oh, that is Kuśakratha Prabhu.” I thought, “Kuśakratha Prabhu, the Sanskrit genius who is producing translation after translation of Vedic scriptures and the works of the Gosvāmīs and other *ācāryas*?” We were reading them and reciting prayers from them. His translations were an essential part of our spiritual lives. I thought, “O my God, that is Kuśakratha Prabhu! Crazy Peter has become Kuśakratha Prabhu, the Sanskrit translator!” And then I thought of your mercy. You had protected him. Somehow you wanted to give him a chance to render service, to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, and he did both.

Another aspect of Your Divine Grace’s faith in us (and in the process of devotional service) was how much responsibility you gave us “young boys and girls.” I joined at the age of twenty-one, and I was already a little older than most. Satsvarūpa Mahārāja was twenty-nine, and to us he seemed like an old man. He even had a job! I hardly knew any peers who had a job. But he had a job and went to work, and he got a paycheck. So, we were really young and inexperienced in the world, yet you gave us tremendous responsibility. Practically any devotee who wanted to go to a new place and open a center, you would encourage, “Go. Do it.” If a householder couple wanted to preach, you would tell them, “Go together and preach. Open a center.” That was your mood.

Your trust inspired us to want to fulfill your trust, to prove that your faith in us was justified. And very few devotees betrayed your trust. One who did was your disciple Gaurasundara Prabhu. Somehow he sold the Hawaii temple and kept the money and disappeared for awhile. But even then, you wanted to bring him back. Once, you were speaking of Gaurasundara’s misdeeds: “He did this; he did that. He was such a rascal—such a rascal. But I like that rascal!” You are so merciful. *Kaṁ vā dayāluṁśaraṇaṁ vrajema:* “Of whom should I take shelter but him, who is most merciful?” I cannot imagine anyone more merciful than you.

So we take shelter of Your Divine Grace, and we want to take more and more shelter of you. We want to encourage more and more people to take shelter of you, and we want them to take more and more shelter of you.

And how do we take shelter of you? By taking shelter of your instructions, which you have given to us out of your immense love and compassion for us — and out of your deep, personal realization of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, *kṛṣṇa-bhakti-rasa*. In one of your early letters to me, you wrote:

Now please increase your enthusiasm for pushing on this Krsna consciousness movement and Krsna will inspire you with all intelligence for serving Him, as much as you engage yourself with sincerity and devotion. Therefore, to keep advancing in your spiritual power, always be sure to chant at least the prescribed sixteen rounds of beads daily. This is the fundamental program for advancing more and more in Krsna consciousness.

Today I read your words “more and more,” and I believe that you are saying that our sixteen rounds of *japa* will carry us step by step to further and further stages of advancement: *sādhana-bhakti*, *bhāva-bhakti*, and *prema-bhakti*, the ultimate goal (*śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati*).

And how do we chant? In your very first letter to me, my initiation letter, you wrote, “With my blessings I am sending herewith your beads, duly chanted upon by me. You should avoid the ten offenses as far as possible, and follow the four regulative principles.” (You also wrote that my beads are my “direct link with Kṛṣṇa.”)

Now, forty years later, I am struggling to chant without offense, to chant with attention. On my own strength, by my own efforts alone, I am sure to fail. I can succeed only by your causeless mercy. And so I pray to you to help me.

*ekakīāmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

**On my own, I find I have no strength to chant the holy name of Lord Hari. Please be merciful by awarding me a particle of faith and giving me the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. \[Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura,** ***Śaraṇāgati,*** **“Ohe! Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura”\]**

Only by your mercy will I be able to fulfill your desire for us — that we taste Kṛṣṇa consciousness and distribute it.

And you have given us your mercy. You have given us the process, the instruction to chant and hear, and you have given us the association of your sincere disciples who have faith in and taste for the holy names, and in whose association we can feel inspired to actually chant and hear.

Still, I pray:

*kabe ha’be bolo se-dina āmār*  
*(āmār) aparādha ghuci’, śuddha nāme ruci,*  
*kṛpā-bale ha’be hṛdoye sañcār*

**Please tell me, When will that day be mine — when my offenses will end and a taste for the pure holy name will be infused within my heart by the power of divine grace?**

*kabe jībe doyā, hoibe udoya,*  
*nija-sukha bhuli’ sudīna-hṛdoya*  
*bhakativinoda, koriyā binoya,*  
*śrī-ājñā-ṭahala koribe pracār*

**When will there be an awakening of compassion for all fallen souls, and when will this Bhaktivinoda, forgetting his own happiness, with a meek heart set out to propagate by humble entreaty the sacred order of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu? \[Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura,** ***Śaraṇāgati,*** **“Kabe Ha’be Bolo”\]**

And I am confident that your mercy will prevail. *Kaṁ vā dayāluṁśaraṇaṁ vrajema.* There is no one more merciful than you.

Your eternal, fallen, crazy servant,  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, I beg to offer my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

In your dealings you manifested all the qualities of an ideal Vaiṣṇava *ācārya*. Your every thought, word, and example was based on the *śāstras*. Despite thousands of followers, comfortable living conditions, copious donations, etc., you always remained a humble devotee of the Supreme Lord. Even a poor man could easily approach you for guidance.

You did not let obstacles obstruct your determination to serve your spiritual master. Even old age and sickness did not slow down your preaching and translation activities. When the world slept, you got up to dictate the Bhaktivedanta purports. Your books have guided thousands from the path of illusion to the path of devotional service. Your Bhaktivedanta purports will continue to guide humanity for the next ten thousand years. Your determination to preach is comparable to the mood of Grandfather Bhīṣma, who, while pierced by many arrows, remained in perfect meditation and instructed King Yudhiṣṭhira for many days.

The devotees of Lord Caitanya are likened to clouds pouring water that nourishes the fruit of love of God. As a sincere follower of Lord Caitanya, you have instructed the members of ISKCON to increase the number of Vaiṣṇavas. The following appears in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā*7.92):

\[Īśvara Purī to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu:\] My dear child, continue dancing, chanting, and performing *saṅkīrtana* in association with devotees. Furthermore, go out and preach the value of chanting *kṛṣṇa-nāma,* for by this process You will be able to deliver all fallen souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the above is also your desire. On this auspicious day, I beg for your causeless mercy so that I may be a tiny instrument in expanding Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, which is the only hope for suffering humanity.

I feel very fortunate to have the opportunity to glorify you in the association of other Vaiṣṇavas. Kindly excuse me for this poor offering, which I am writing in Moscow. After a long struggle we have finally obtained land in this very important city. Please bless us so that we can build a nice temple and a cultural center for your pleasure.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your divine saving grace, which inundates the world with the nectarean waters of *kīrtana-rasa*.

I wish to continue my humble effort in explaining, to the minute degree of which my limited perception is able, the Vaiṣṇava qualities manifested in their fullest measure by you. The next quality is *śuci* — cleanliness, purity.

Sometimes *śuci* means *brāhmaṇa*. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the *dvijottama, dvija-śreṣṭha,* or *dvija-vāraṇa* is Śukadeva, Kardama Muni, the Kumāras, etc. You are the personification of their qualities — presenting the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the benefit of the world, showing perfect conduct and responsibility in marriage and renunciation, and being a perfect *brāhmaṇa* admired by kings, leaders, and qualified persons. Certainly you are the best of the *brāhmaṇas*.

Your example of cleanliness was always perfect. You established Deities all over the world to purify our eyes, minds, and senses. You made *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas* whose Deity worship surpassed that of many life-long *brāhmaṇas*. Your desk and room were always immaculate. You taught all of us the habits of brahminical consciousness, even to the last detail of noting disorder in the most obscure places, like a bird’s nest in a fan cage.

You are the modern Śuci-śravā, Vyāsadeva, whose compassion is cleansing the world. Purity, or cleanliness, is one of the pillars of human life. Your preaching challenges its antithesis, the impurity popularly manifested as illicit sex life. While many other religious leaders have bent to the whims of the public, your preaching stands unaltered on this principle, opposing the modern psychological trend. Therefore, you are the true person *bhāgavata,* and I pray that you allow me to engage in *bhāgavata-sevā,* service to your lotus feet, birth after birth, to purify my tainted consciousness by the words emanating from your lotus mouth.

Your most fallen servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My simple prayer is that Lord Kṛṣṇa bless me to be an enthusiastic member of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *saṅkīrtana* party. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pure loving service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu inspired the Lord to reveal Himself in the most powerful and enchanting way. We can all participate in that unique revelation by enthusiastically assisting Śrīla Prabhupāda in his unique service to the Lord.

Sincere service to Śrīla Prabhupāda in his *saṅkīrtana* activities quickly and easily elevates us beyond the predictable misery of selfishness. Service to Śrīla Prabhupāda is the best way to water the root of our existence, and thus achieve eternal peace and happiness. Let us try to put his pleasure above our own, for only thus will we be happy in this world and the next.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pleasure is sublime and selfless: he seeks the eternal happiness of all living beings. Trusting in his mercy and that of Lord Kṛṣṇa, let us rededicate ourselves to his mission, which is identical to that of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

From one who aspires to serve him,  
Hridayānanda Dāsa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrate respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy that you have lifted me up from the ocean of birth and death and engaged me in Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s devotional service. It is your causeless mercy that you left the comfortable spiritual environment of Vṛndāvana and took so many risks to carry out the order of your spiritual master and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western world. If it weren’t for your mercy, where would we be now? Who would have given us Kṛṣṇa consciousness? We are eternally indebted to Your Divine Grace.

On your sacred Vyāsa-pūjā day we remember all the things we are indebted to you for and want to reconfirm our commitment to serving Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet. We don’t have any other purpose in life.

We remember the song of Vāsudeva Ghoṣa Ṭhākura in which he sings that if Lord Caitanya hadn’t come to give Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *prema* and the *saṅkīrtana* movement, then what would have happened? Who would have done it? In the same way, we are feeling that if it weren’t for your causeless mercy, we wouldn’t have gotten the holy names of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, which are such living transcendental nectar; we wouldn’t have gotten the sacred Gāyatrī mantras, which give such inspiration; and we wouldn’t have learned about devotional service and be able to serve the lotus feet of Guru and Gaurāṅga.

If it weren’t for your causeless mercy, we wouldn’t have been able to reside in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur and Navadvīpa *dhāma* and serve the *dhāma* and its pilgrims.

If it weren’t for your causeless mercy, we wouldn’t have been able to read such transcendental literary works as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Bhagavad-gītā,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

If it weren’t for your coming to the West, we wouldn’t have known about Deity worship or gotten the shelter of serving Śrī Pañca-tattva, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and Their Aṣṭa-sakhīs, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha-Baladeva-Subhadrā, Śrī Śrī Narasiṁha-Prahlāda, and so many other wonderful Deities in ISKCON.

If it weren’t for your sacrifices, we wouldn’t have gotten the inspiration to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, following in your divine footsteps, rather than simply practicing in seclusion.

If it weren’t for your causeless mercy, we wouldn’t have learned about and been connected to the *guru-paramparā*.

It goes on and on. Your Divine Grace has given us so much that we cannot even fully describe how much causeless mercy you have given. I have only touched the tip of the iceberg. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my obeisances again and again at your lotus feet.

ISKCON and its devotees are constantly dependent on your causeless mercy, protection, and guidance. The GBC is trying to fulfill your desires and is taking up new initiatives. We need your mercy for these efforts to expand the *saṅkīrtana* movement and care for the devotees.

You have instructed us that we can demonstrate how much we love you by how well we cooperate. I pray for your blessings to always cooperate with the devotees and fulfill your desires in this regard. We have many challenges facing us, and only by Your Divine Grace’s mercy can we overcome them. So I take this opportunity to re-affirm my commitment to serving Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

Your humble and insignificant spiritual son,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.**

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?**

Each year, when it comes time to write an official offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā day and I start to think of all your transcendental qualities and accomplishments, I become bewildered and do not know where to start. When I think of how my godbrothers and -sisters are glorifying you not only by eloquent words but by awesome deeds, I become more bewildered. This year for many days I was not able to start writing.

At the time, I was reading the biography of the your dear disciple Bhakti Tīrtha Swami. Reading about the deep religiousness of his childhood and his later ecstatic devotion to your service made me even more hesitant to write, since my life before you rescued me was devoid of religion, and since joining your movement I have simply been swept along by the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas.

Finally, I spent many hours reading your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and in this way I got over my fear of not writing nicely.

I read about Dhruva Mahārāja when he saw the Supreme Lord (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.9.4):

\[Translation:\] Although Dhruva Mahārāja was a small boy, he wanted to offer prayers to the Supreme Personality of Godhead in suitable language. But because he was inexperienced, he could not adjust himself immediately. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, being situated in everyone’s heart, could understand Dhruva Mahārāja’s awkward position. Out of His causeless mercy He touched His conchshell to the forehead of Dhruva Mahārāja, who stood before Him with folded hands.

\[Purport:\] Every devotee wants to chant the transcendental qualities of the Lord. Devotees are always interested in hearing about the Lord’s transcendental qualities, and they are always eager to glorify these qualities, but sometimes they feel inconvenienced by humbleness. The Personality of Godhead, being situated in everyone’s heart, specifically gives a devotee intelligence to describe Him. It is therefore understood that when a devotee writes or speaks about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, his words are dictated by the Lord from within. This is confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā,* Tenth Chapter: to those who constantly engage in the transcendental loving service of the Lord, the Lord, from within, dictates what to do next in order to serve Him. When Dhruva Mahārāja felt hesitant, not knowing how to describe the Lord for want of sufficient experience, the Lord, out of His causeless mercy, touched His conchshell to Dhruva’s forehead, and he was transcendentally inspired. This transcendental inspiration is called *brahma-maya* because when one is thus inspired, the sound he produces exactly corresponds to the sound vibration of the *Vedas*. This is not the ordinary sound vibration of this material world. Therefore the sound vibration of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* although presented in the ordinary alphabet, should not be taken as mundane or material.

Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja had a similar experience (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.5–6):

\[Translation:\] When Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva saw the small boy Prahlāda Mahārāja prostrated at the soles of His lotus feet, He became most ecstatic in affection toward His devotee. Raising Prahlāda, the Lord placed His lotus hand upon the boy’s head because His hand is always ready to create fearlessness in all of His devotees. By the touch of Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva’s hand on Prahlāda Mahārāja’s head, Prahlāda was completely freed of all material contaminations and desires, as if he had been thoroughly cleansed. Therefore he at once became transcendentally situated, and all the symptoms of ecstasy became manifest in his body. His heart filled with love, and his eyes with tears, and thus he was able to completely capture the lotus feet of the Lord within the core of his heart.

From reading these passages I realized that I am fully dependent upon your mercy, without which I am deaf, dumb, and blind. I know that you are giving your mercy freely, but I need special mercy in order to take it.

Simply by the causeless mercy of Patita-pāvana Gaura-Nitāi, I was able to become your direct disciple, and because, to encourage me to continue serving, you have given me numerous titles like “*sannyāsī,*”“GBC,” and “guru,” many devotees, out of their humility, offer me respect. I need the mercy of Nityānanda Balarāma, which you have arranged for us, so that I may have the strength to reciprocate and serve with compassion. Without that mercy, I will think I deserve to be honored and will become an offender. I try to remember Lord Brahmās prayers in the third canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

The Supreme Lord, the Personality of Godhead, is always the benefactor of the surrendered souls. His activities are always enacted through His internal potency, Ramā, or the goddess of fortune. I pray only to engage in His service in the creation of the material world, and I pray that I not be materially affected by my works, for thus I may be able to give up the false prestige of being the creator. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.23\]

. . . I therefore pray that in the course of my material activities I may not be deviated from the vibration of the Vedic hymns. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.24\]

O my Lord, the people of the world are embarrassed by unlimited material anxieties — they are always afraid. They always try to protect wealth, body, and friends, they are filled with lamentation and unlawful desires and paraphernalia, and they avariciously base their undertakings on the perishable conceptions of “my” and “mine.” As long as they do not take shelter of Your safe lotus feet, they are full of such anxieties. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.6\]

O my Lord, persons who are bereft of the all-auspicious performance of chanting and hearing about Your transcendental activities are certainly unfortunate and are also bereft of good sense. They engage in inauspicious activities, enjoying sense gratification for a very little while. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.7\]

O great actor, my Lord, all these poor creatures are constantly perplexed by hunger, thirst, severe cold, secretion, and bile, attacked by coughing winter, blasting summer, rains, and many other disturbing elements, and overwhelmed by strong sex urges and indefatigable anger. I take pity on them, and I am very much aggrieved for them.” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.8\]

So I wonder when I will become a real Vaiṣṇava and take the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu seriously. When you gave me my initiated name you said, “Kavicandra was a great devotee of Lord Nityānanda.” At that time I thought I might do book distribution always. I have not done much, but I pray to keep trying. I know that if I can give up the false ego I can follow in the footsteps of the real Vaiṣṇavas.

*kabe jībe doyā, hoibe udoya,*  
*nija-sukha bhuli’ sudīna-hṛdoya*  
*bhakativinoda, koriyā binoya,*  
*śrī-ājñā-ṭahala koribe pracār*

**When kindness to all beings will be appearing, with free heart forget myself comforting, Bhaktivinoda in all humility prays, “Now I will set out to preach Your order sublime.” When, oh when will that day be mine? \[*****Śaraṇāgati*****\]**

You did everything perfectly. Everything that I do is full of mistakes, but you tolerate me like a loving father. I know that it is not possible to repay the debt to the spiritual master, but I pray that I will never stop trying, even though the debt is constantly increasing.

The numbers of devotees is always increasing, and they are very kind to keep me in their association. I pray that I will be able to take up Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions and be the servant of the devotees. I take inspiration from the following passage in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 11.28–30):

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**“\[Lord Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna:\] ‘Those who are My direct devotees are actually not My devotees, but those who are the devotees of My servant are factually My devotees.’**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu quotes this verse from the *Ādi Purāṇa*. The verse is also included in the *Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta* (2.6).

*\[Texts 29–30\]*  
*ādaraḥ paricaryāyāṁ sarvāṅgair abhivandanam*  
*mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā sarva-bhūteṣu man-matiḥ*  
*mad-artheṣv aṅga-ceṣṭā ca vacasā mad-guṇeraṇam*  
*mayy arpaṇaṁ ca manasaḥ sarva-kāma-vivarjanam*

**“My devotees take great care and respect in rendering Me service. They offer obeisances to Me with all their bodily limbs. They worship other devotees and find all living entities related to Me. For Me they engage the entire energy of their bodies. They engage the power of speech in the glorification of My qualities and form. They also dedicate their minds unto Me and try to give up all kinds of material desires. Thus My devotees are characterized.’**

These two verses are quoted from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.19.21–22). They were spoken by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who was answering Uddhava’s inquiry about devotional service.

I am not talented at anything. My knowledge of the scriptures is not great. Basically I have no good qualities, but I do have faith that if I can stay in the association of your followers and try to serve them, your mercy will act. By your words and deeds you perfectly taught the essence of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s teachings.

*prabhu kahe, — “vaiṣṇava-sevā, nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*dui kara, śīghra pābe śrī-kṛṣṇa-caraṇa”*

**The Lord replied, “You should engage yourself in the service of the servants of Kṛṣṇa and always chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. If you do these two things, you will very soon attain shelter at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.” \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*****16.70\]**

Thank you for giving us the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, the *mahā-mantra*. The holy name is Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is everything, so you have given us everything. To list all the gifts would make this essay too voluminous, so I will just say thank you for giving Kṛṣṇa to the world. Without Kṛṣṇa, everyone is suffering, trying to enjoy the inferior energy of the Lord. I pray that I will always remember your unlimited compassion and try to give as many people as possible the *mahā-mantra*,

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ*  
*pūrṇaḥśuddho nitya-mukto ’bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ*

The holy name of Kṛṣṇa is transcendentally blissful. It bestows all spiritual benedictions, for it is Kṛṣṇa Himself, the reservoir of all pleasure. Kṛṣṇa’s name is complete, and it is the form of all transcendental mellows. It is not a material name under any condition, and it is no less powerful than Kṛṣṇa Himself. Since Kṛṣṇa’s name is not contaminated by the material qualities, there is no question of its being involved with *māyā*. Kṛṣṇa’s name is always liberated and spiritual; it is never conditioned by the laws of material nature. This is because the name of Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa Himself are identical. \[*Padma Purāṇa*, as cited in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 17.133\]

Your aspiring servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Though I am most unqualified, I offer these few verses in praise of you.

**Attached to You**

Living inside plastic bodies,  
Spending years caring for them,  
Calling them “family” and “kinsmen,”  
We’re gliding with them down to hell.  
Morbid senses with no mercy  
Keep us stuck as if in glue.  
Only way to be protected —  
To become attached to you.  
Hosts of maniacs called “researchers”  
Scan this matter in and out,  
Search for cosmic mozzarellas,  
Soups, black holes, or primal life.  
Foolish humans wallow, thinking  
No reactions will come through.  
Only way to be protected —  
To become attached to you.  
So-called spiritualist figures  
Show compassion for the poor.  
Others talk of a dimension  
Where we all are absolute.  
Such a waste of time and money,  
And of Kṛṣṇa not a clue.  
Only way to be protected —  
To become attached to you.  
When we think we’ve made some progress,  
How complacent we become!  
Profit, fame, and adoration  
Make us think we are advanced.  
Going so far to even judge you,  
We become the lowest fools.  
Only way to be protected —  
To become attached to you.
