Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though I don’t deserve it and my desire is not as strong as it should be, I am humbly asking for an attachment to you that may carry me across this life, through death, and finally unto your lotus feet. Please protect me.

Your foolish servant,  
Madhusevita Dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most holy master and beloved friend and parent Śrīla Prabhupāda,

May I remain perpetually falling at your lotus feet like grains of sand through an hourglass, over and over again. How much you are missed! Not only by those who sought and received your soothing shelter during the time you graced this earth with your godliness and message of hope in the form of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*, but by those wonderful devotees who have since joined your mission as dedicated volunteers in the real war on terror — the struggle against Māyā. It is my good fortune to gain your continued association through these wonderful souls, who are currently surrendering to you by following your teachings, feeling solace in their hearts at having found the perfect process and the perfect master. In your physical absence they have your books and your current representatives, who, with enthusiasm, perseverance, and patience, deliver the message of *bhakti* to unlimited suffering *jīvas*. The continuing endeavor of these devotees who joined your movement after your departure shines further glory on you as our source of enlightenment and refuge, our only shelter during the countless, unintelligible, tumultuous Kali-yuga days — each one described in startling detail in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

In every instance, just when everything seems about to fall into total despair and disintegration, Your Divine Grace emerges from the depths of our hearts to remind us of our ultimate gain and glory and to renew our desire to continue our search for perfection in the form of the holy name, to fight the battle against Māyā with arrows of loving truth and knowledge, and to look for a three-curved blue boy with a flute in His hand and a peacock feather on His curly-haired, helmeted head. We are blessed with the gracious *saṅga* of illuminated revered senior Vaiṣṇavas who have truly taken to your teachings and mission, and who share their realizations and affection with great eagerness. My only lament is that I have failed to cultivate the ability to serve and appreciate these beacons of your success. Perhaps, if I am fortunate enough to become sober, Kṛṣṇa will allow me this mercy and I too may join your eternal entourage. Meanwhile, by Kṛṣṇa’s inconceivable plan, one by one these revered Vaiṣṇavas are departing to join you in your ongoing *līlās*.

Please help me develop gratitude, my dear master, so that I may serve you and yours. I beg your mercy so that a particle of humility will touch my calloused heart, rendering it fit for transcendental devotional service to flourish.

Eagerly awaiting the dawn of mercy at your lotus feet, I remain  
Your eternal servant,  
Mālatī Dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

In addition to your many other characteristics, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you often displayed a sense of humor. I found thousands of instances in the VedaBase in which the word “laughter” occurs, referring to your mostly public and devotee audiences. Rather than try to explain what a sense of humor is, I thought I would cite a few examples of your public statements that caused your audience to laugh. In one sense, your laughter-eliciting statements were entertaining; in another sense, such commentary brought out the raw truth and was unintentionally funny. You sometimes transformed language in order to bring out truth.

It’s been said that laughter is a type of therapy for the ills that beset these material bodies. But how does humor come into a philosophy that’s serious, a philosophy that says that we’re all subject to birth, disease, old age, and death, that this world is a place of misery, that pleasure is by and large illusory, that fun is fantasy, that the only true parties are *saṅkīrtana* parties, that play is for children, and that people lead lives of “quiet desperation”?

Well, one answer is that in the spiritual world one can find laughter, fun, and pleasure. Another is that spiritual life is not a somber, lugubrious affair, nor is Kṛṣṇa, in your words, “a stereotyped, stagnant block” but is “a dynamic force,” a charming young boy who is alive and happy, who jokes with intimate disciples and plays childhood pranks on His parents and young friends.

Once Harikeśa Prabhu told me he was sitting next to you on an Air India flight when rousing classical sitar music began playing over the public address system. When Harikeśa began bobbing his head to the beat, you admonished him by sternly saying, “This is Māyā.” Harikeśa said he instantly stopped bobbing his head, sat straight, and assumed a grave countenance. But then you turned to him and, smiling impishly, said, “But it is very *nice* Māyā.”

Another one-liner: When asked about the movement’s attitude toward health, you responded by saying, “No one is healthy; everyone is dying.”

How can dying be a laughing matter? Maybe laughter can also be a kind of acknowledgement of what is said, rather than an endorsement of something humorous (funny peculiar, not funny ha-ha). There is and often was a kind of ironic laughter that meant an important truth had been spoken in a creative way, sort of like a headline or poetic phrase that tells a whole story in a few words.

Early on, you adopted informal American language, using words like “wildfire” and “hippy,” among other colloquialisms. During a Kolkata lecture, you even told the now-famous story of Gopal Bon when illustrating the point of how intimacy can include insults that the insulted person enjoys. The king had employed Gopal as his court jester, and one day the king asked him, “Gopal, what is the difference between you and an ass?” You said that Gopal immediately measured the distance between him and the king and said, “Just three feet,” and that the king enjoyed this answer. Once in Kenya, after a lecture, an Indian guest asked you to display a many-armed form, saying that you must have some power. You replied, “I am not so powerful that I can show the *viśva-rūpa*. Kṛṣṇa has given me this power, that ‘You go and speak to the foolish rascals that I am Bhagavān.’ That much power. Yes. That is my power. I can say to you that ‘Kṛṣṇa is Bhagavān.’ That’s all.”

Humor — sometimes intentional, sometimes not — is just one of the many features that endeared you to thousands.

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Niranjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of SarasvatīGosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I wish to thank you from the core of my heart for having blessed me with the true understanding of the word “life.” Had you not rescued me, I am convinced I would have never known what it means to live. Though I have existed since time immemorial, my life did not begin until you infused this lost soul with faith in the existence of Guru, Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa’s pure holy name. Without this faith, there can never be life — or, at best, there can be a life no more meaningful than that possessed by the bellows, which only breathes. Having had this faith awakened in me, my will to “live” is becoming stronger and stronger, and at the same time the desire to increase this faith continues to grow. I’ve come to realize that it is *this* desire which is life itself.

Desire is the symptom of my, my life. How can you stop it? It is not possible. \[From a lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.55–56, given in New York on 19 April 1966\]

“Through my practice of *sādhana-bhakti* I will one day attain the stage of *bhāva*”— it is highly commendable for a devotee to maintain such a desire, but apart from this desire all other types of desires \[desires separate from the desire to please Kṛṣṇa\] are fit to be rejected. \[Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s *Tattva-viveka,* Chapter One\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you created my faith, you nourish my faith, and by your causeless mercy you want nothing less than my unflinching faith.

One who has unflinching devotion for the Supreme Lord and is directed by the spiritual master, in whom he has similar unflinching faith, can see the Supreme Personality of Godhead by revelation. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 11.54, purport\]

You often quote these words of your Guru Mahārāja: *prāṇa āche yāṅra, sei hetu pracāra.* “One can preach who has got life.’’ Preaching is the real necessity of life. Although we often think of life’s necessities as food, water, clothing, and shelter, these are not the necessities of life but are meant for maintaining life in the body. “Life” has its own necessity, independent of the body, and this necessity has been given by you — to preach with unflinching faith in the order of guru! This was not only your order but your pure and ideal example as well.

As much as possible read, chant, and preach. This is our life and soul. \[Letter to Hridayānanda Dāsa Goswami, 6 January 1972\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I beg you to please allow me to use the remainder of my life in this body to serve your desires with this unflinching faith. You are the lord and master of my heart. Please do with me as you wish. My *life* is yours.

Your eternal servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master,

This year we will observe the thirtieth anniversary of your departure from this planet. On this auspicious anniversary of your appearance I am reflecting on your instructions concerning how ISKCON should to be organized, specifically on the pillars you established that support this Society: Purity is the force; Books are the basis; Utility is the principle; and Preaching is the essence.

Sadly, over these last thirty years we have committed many mistakes due to a lack of maturity and purity. These mistakes have seriously harmed ISKCON and consequently made you suffer very much. I am deeply meditating on how we can improve our working methods and training so that we can avoid the recurrence of such serious mistakes.

“Love me by staying together, and remain united under all circumstances” is your instruction. A look back into the past shows how difficult it is to follow this instruction. I am humbly taking some time during your auspicious appearance day to introspect, so I may understand the causes of various deviations.

Purity plays a critical role in this very complex context, and indeed the world is in great need of Śrī Caitanyadeva’s message. So you have marvelously adapted the Lord’s message in such way that people of the modern society can understand it. You wish that members of ISKCON continue to spread this message all over the world, both in Eastern and Western countries.

I pray to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to always remind me of the invaluable gifts you have bestowed upon mankind and to help me properly understand them. Please help me become your true servant, qualified enough to become your useful instrument.

Your eternal and disqualified servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This material world is a labyrinth of allurements and complications. We have been trapped here since time immemorial and risk remaining permanently if we do not seize the opportunity to escape this cycle of birth and death. It is extremely rare for a conditioned soul to get this opportunity. True, in the *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa gives the relevant instructions on how one can return to the spiritual world by following the path of *bhakti-yoga,* and the *Bhagavad-gītā* is certainly a very famous scripture around the world. However, despite its being available in many languages, it is usually not properly understood, and generally people are not able to understand how to actually apply Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions in their lives. We are therefore unlimitedly indebted to Your Divine Grace for showing us how to understand the message of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and how to practically follow the instructions of the Lord. Thus spiritual life becomes an experienced reality and not just a theory.

You have shown us in great detail how to practice all the important aspects of *bhakti-yoga* in the modern world, and have especially given great emphasis to the chanting of the holy names, as per the example and instruction of Lord Caitanya. The Lord inaugurated and preached the *saṅkīrtana* movement in India, and you, being empowered by him due to your dedication to your spiritual master, have inaugurated and developed the *saṅkīrtana* movement of the Lord all over the world. Thus we are getting the rare opportunity to become freed from the shackles of birth and death and return to the spiritual world. We are all eternally indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The only repayment you ask is that we continue to follow in your footsteps and spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement. May Your Divine Grace empower us to do so.

Your insignificant servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**\[Lord Kṛṣṇa said to Uddhava:\] One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27\]**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are definitely not an ordinary man.

Recently I visited the town of Porbandar, in Gujarat, India, which is the hometown of Kṛṣṇa’s *brāhmaṇa* school-friend Sudāmā. Porbandar is also famous for being the birthplace of Mahatma Gandhi. I visited his ancestral home and the Gandhi museum, where I read about his life and his assassination. I was reminded of a certain paragraph in a letter you wrote to him in 1947, a few months before his death:

I tell you as a sincere friend that you must immediately retire from active politics if you do not desire to die an inglorious death. You have 125 years to live as you have desired to live but if you die an inglorious death it is no worth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote this letter on July 12, 1947. Mahatma Gandhi was assassinated on January 30, 1948, a little more than six months later. It is said that a *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee has direct contact with the Supreme Lord and as such knows what others cannot. Great devotees are truly compassionate and continuously try to save people from the clutches of birth and death by bringing them to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In another letter from the late 1940s, sent to Dr. Sardar Patel, the Deputy Prime Minister of India, you wrote:

I wish to organize a spiritual society and therefore seek your active help and support for this. The immediate need is to start an organization centre in a suitable place preferably in New Delhi under your direct supervision.

In another letter written during this time, you stated your intention of sending your *Back to Godhead* papers to fifty-three countries: “Afghanistan 1,000, America 10,000, Argentina 500, Belgium 500, Brazil 500 . . .”

The *ācārya,* who is the representative of the Supreme Lord, has the same mission as the Lord, that is, to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to as many fallen souls as possible and take them back home, back to Godhead. You are an intimate associate of Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you were sent by Him to save us. Although you are always humble, on one occasion you confided to your servant: “Maybe my father knew.” Your servant understood what this meant. Those who are truly blessed and fortunate in this world can also understand.

Tirelessly you traveled the globe preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness and showing people the true nature of spiritual life. You exposed the illusion of material life, especially the promises of materialistic politicians and scientists. You challenged pseudo meditators by saying, “What is the object of their meditation? They meditate on nonsense for twenty minutes, and the rest of the day they are engaged in sense gratification.”

Many impersonalists around the world are alluring people by chanting *bhajans* and *kīrtanas* and then preaching Māyāvāda philosophy. You criticized this severely in Rishikesh by saying, “They are cheating, attracting people by deity worship, *ārati*, *kīrtana*, and then saying there is no God.” We personally go to these places and invite the participants to our centers so they can chant with us and learn about Kṛṣṇa. In this way we are trying to follow in your footsteps.

However, I am starting to realize that it is not totally possible to follow in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They are so great. You are such a bold preacher. I remember reading that you asked your godbrother Śrīla Śrīdhara Mahārāja to join you in preaching to Mahatma Gandhi. You saw no one as too big or too small, and nothing intimidated you. A few years ago Mahatma Gandhi was voted by *Time* magazine as Person of the Century, but *Time* magazine is so foolish, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You should have been named as Person of the Century because you are no ordinary man: you are the intimate associate and representative of Kṛṣṇa. As such, who can be greater than you?

Your insignificant servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are Kṛṣṇa’s mercy manifest, made active — *pro*active.

Once more I committed the crime of birth and this time made America my hideout: You sought me and found me.

It was my vanity, in those days, to think myself *disillusioned*. You knew I had plenty of illusions left, and you began relieving me of them, one after another.

Of illusions, I am a nested Russian doll: Under one painted shell there’s another, and another, and still another . . .

Who am I? Where does the be-littling end? (The vanishingly small unshelled self.)

You see me, and you seek me out, and I pray one day I become reduced enough for you to meet me.

As, by your mercy, I seem to become less, I become more: more myself, more enchanted by the Lord, more grateful to His agent, you.

I learn this truth: as we grow smaller, love of God grows greater. As love of the Lord discovers no upper limit, humility finds no lower. Great souls — you, your teachers — yearn to become less and less.

I fled from you, Prabhupāda, into this world, into this country, into this body, into this many-layered mind. Please don’t stop uncovering me — discovering me. Lift from me the intolerable, crushing weight of these useless simulacra of “I.”

Free me so I can finally serve you as you serve your master as he serves his as he serves his . . .

Longing to become *dāsānudāsānudāsānudāsa* . . .

Ravīndra Svarūpa Dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mukhaṁ karoti vācālaṁ panguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dearly beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Vyāsa-pūjā time is such a joyous time for focusing our full attention upon you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — all of us, all of your followers all over the world. Just after Kṛṣṇa-janmāṣṭamī, having just completed festive celebrations and all-day fasting on such a sacred day as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s divine appearance, we are now poised to honor with full attention the very person who introduced us to the treasure of pure devotional service and its sublime object, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

In very beautiful poetic language, Śukadeva Gosvāmī describes your role in introducing us to unalloyed devotional service.

*etāvān eva yajatām iha niḥśreyasodayaḥ*  
*bhagavaty acalo bhāvo yad bhāgavata-saṅgataḥ*

**All the different kinds of worshipers . . . can attain the highest perfectional benediction, which is spontaneous attraction unflinchingly fixed upon the Supreme Personality of Godhead, only by the association of the pure devotee of the Lord. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.3.11\]**

By your association, by *bhāgavata-saṅgata,* we have the opportunity to become like swans, although we are but crows. With utmost compassion you have extended yourself to us, knowing no limits to personal sacrifice, to provide us with your most-loving association. This association is absolutely transforming!

You are the very person *bhāgavata* who has introduced us to the book *Bhāgavata*, which reveals Kṛṣṇa to the world. Such a gift you have given us! We cannot even imagine how valuable such a gift is!

Meticulously and painstakingly — you planned it this way — you set up your Society so that even after your physical association would be withdrawn from this world, you would continue to offer your most precious association through your Bhaktivedanta purports. We have full access to your association though your divinely-inspired writings! Who will not take advantage?

*jñānaṁ yad āpratinivṛtta-guṇormi-cakram*  
*ātma-prasāda uta yatra guṇeṣv asaṅgaḥ*  
*kaivalya-sammata-pathas tv atha bhakti-yogaḥ*  
*ko nirvṛto hari-kathāsu ratiṁ na kuryāt*

**Transcendental knowledge in relation with the Supreme Lord Hari is knowledge resulting in the complete suspension of the waves and whirlpools of the material modes. Such knowledge is self-satisfying due to its being free from material attachment, and being transcendental it is approved by authorities. Who could fail to be attracted? \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.3.12\]**

Full access, however, has its obstacle. You taught us that our hearing of your divine teachings is to be done with rapt attention, *niviṣṭa,* and then we can receive Kṛṣṇa fully.

By rapt attention fixed upon Kṛṣṇa, one is purified, and thus one is delivered from material life. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.1.26, purport\]

But . . .

Simple hearing is not all; one must realize the text with proper attention. The word *niviṣṭa* means that Sūta Gosvāmī drank the juice of *Bhāgavatam* through his ears. That is the real process of receiving *Bhāgavatam*. One should hear with rapt attention from the real person, and then he can at once realize the presence of Lord Kṛṣṇa in every page. The secret of knowing *Bhāgavatam* is mentioned here. No one can give rapt attention who is not pure in mind. No one can be pure in mind who is not pure in action. No one can be pure in action who is not pure in eating, sleeping, fearing, and mating. But somehow or other if someone hears with rapt attention from the right person, at the very beginning one can assuredly see Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in person in the pages of *Bhāgavatam*. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.3.44\]

Accessing rapt attention is not easy to accomplish, even when strictly observing the pure living habits you gave us.

How then to attain rapt attention, despite our deficiencies? We need your special mercy!

One of the most dynamic and exciting developments in your Society these days is a swelling interest to go more deeply into the holy name by a number of devotees taking part in *japa* retreats, organized by the Spiritual Care initiative of the strategic planning group. I have attended two of them, and they have been most inspirational. I am sure you are pleased by this effort; at the same time, the vast gap between absorption in the holy name and where we are now is acutely more obvious as a result.

In April this year I attended a *japa* retreat in a very beautiful setting, a mountainous area in Ojai, California. The vista at the retreat center was spectacular! Tall mountains on three sides, a deep valley and the ocean off in the distance on the other side, beautiful gardens all around, tiny hummingbirds, varieties of chirping birds all around, cool breezes in the warm California sun — ideal for *japa* absorption!

One of the visual scenes was the aerial performance of two large hawks. They would daily glide about in the air, serenely and gracefully and effortlessly floating amongst the gusts of wind coming off the mountainside, circling around, soaring higher and higher into the air. It was awesome!

Unfortunately, on the 64-round day these two hawks were in a particular mood of lofty performance, and it was most distracting from absorption in chanting. Their soaring motion, although beautiful to behold, was like the mind flying about here and there instead of focusing upon the holy name.

This aerial dance had been going on for some time when its purpose was abruptly disclosed: suddenly the hawks tucked their wings against their bodies and, like dive bombers, plunged within a second and a half to the ground, where one of them grabbed their prey with its talons. Then they flew off again, with the small creature twitching and struggling with all its might to get release from the hawk’s grasp.

Another retreat participant saw the same sight I did, and our realization was identical: urgency to reach the goal of rapt attention *now,* because at any moment death may come. The mesmerizing show of the illusory energy is in fact the very instrument of our demise.

There must be a sense of urgency to effectively focus the mind on the holy name, or upon the message of pure unalloyed devotion. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and the holy name are your gifts, by which you are directly delivering Kṛṣṇa to us; we would be most ungrateful if we were not to make a full-hearted effort to strive to receive the fullness of these two gifts. Urgency to enthusiastically strive for this mercy is the call of the hour.

My prayer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on your sacred Vyāsa-pūjā day, is that you mercifully provide me with the strength of devotion and the love within my heart to make all efforts to manifest the fullness of these sublime gifts, as an offering of my unlimited gratitude to Your Divine Grace.

Kindly accept my sincere and humble prayer at your divine lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

I offer my obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who arrived as the first *ācārya* to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the West. He was ordered to do so by his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, early in life, and he led a lifetime in preparation before he was able to carry out the sacred order. He had to overcome the obstacles of family life and business failures, and then he made his resolution to dedicate himself to the renounced order of life. He led a life of poverty, attempting to raise money to print volumes of his *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* translations and commentaries. He begged for money and permission to travel to preach in America, and finally he was granted a free passage on a steamship and a three-month stay with a family of Indians living in America.

This is the beginning of the well-known story worth telling and retelling to generations. The crossing of the Atlantic on the *Jaladuta* despite heart attacks, living virtually alone in New York City, gradually meeting some boys from the Lower East side . . . 26 Second Avenue.

The life story is so important; it is like the epics *Mahābhārata* and *Rāmāyaṇa,* like Kṛṣṇa’s stories and Lord Caitanya’s life. And yet in another sense Prabhupāda’s story is not bigger than life: it is a story of a human spiritual master. It is told in the *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* it is told in the multi-volumes of Hari Śauri Prabhu’s diaries, and it is told in many excellent single-volume memoirs such as Śrutakīrti Prabhu’s *What Is the Difficulty?* All of these memoirs are important. Also important are the DVD series by Siddhānta Prabhu and Yadubara Prabhu. In addition to filmed fresh interviews, devotees are meeting together informally every day and holding sessions of talks about Prabhupāda.

Aside from memories, we have to *work* for Śrīla Prabhupāda, carrying out his living mission. These are the teachings of Lord Caitanya. We have to live the lives of exemplary devotees as he taught us, attaining the twenty-six qualities of a devotee. Not only read about his *life* but read his *books*.

He wants us to live with devotees and engage in the loving exchanges and be friendly with all living beings. He wants us to follow the exemplary instructions he taught us, and as we follow them we please him and we please Kṛṣṇa. This is the way to glorify him in a Vyāsa-pūjā homage.

The mission of Lord Caitanya, which Śrīla Prabhupāda is following, is nothing less than the attempt to save all fallen souls, all those engaged in meat-eating, illicit sex, intoxication, and gambling. He has done great work in this effort with his International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Prabhupāda’s books have been translated into dozens of languages and distributed in many millions of volumes, and this has changed many thousands of people from sinful to purified. Such is the power of his spiritual *śakti*. Prabhupāda is a most wonderful force for good against evil in the world. And it is most fitting to offer him the highest homages again and again.

Satsvarūpa Dāsa Goswami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

I am sitting in Vṛndāvana writing another book. To my left, the spire of your *samādhi mandir* is just visible. To neutralize the sounds of construction, barking dogs, fighting monkeys, and amplified *kīrtanas,* I listen to your *bhajanas* through my headphones. Your voice is the only relief.

*Bhaja Hūre Mana* ends and the next song begins: *Vibhāvarī-śeṣa* — my favorite. I lean back and close my eyes.

It is evening time in 1973; I am alone in the *saṅkīrtana* van near Plattsburgh, New York, while Śukra Prabhu is buying groceries from a supermarket. Tired, I listen as your sonorous voice rings through the air:

*vibhāvarīśeṣa, āloka-praveśa,*  
*nidrā chādi’ uṭho jīva.*

It is one of those unrehearsed mystical moments. Suddenly there are only the names of Kṛṣṇa — Kṛṣṇa Himself — and nothing else.

*nṛsiṁha vāmana, śrī-madhusūdana,*  
*brajendra-nandana śyāma.*

There is no Plattsburgh, no parking lot, no van — only Kṛṣṇa’s names like whitecaps riding the waves of your soul-stirring and timelessly familiar voice.

Then you repeat, once, twice, thrice, *jāmuna-jīvana, keli-parāyaṇa,* and tears glide uncontrollably down my cheeks. I sob, I shiver, my heart cannot contain what it feels.

Is it bliss?

More than bliss, it is bliss induced by Kṛṣṇa’s presence. Kṛṣṇa is present by your voice. Kṛṣṇa is in the van, or wherever it is that I am.

It can’t be. But it is. And at the moment it is an indisputable reality. Forget that I joined only months ago, forget that only the realized see Kṛṣṇa, forget any arguments — Kṛṣṇa is here.

And then, when the song is over, you slowly, purposefully explain every word, every line. But your words are not a purport. They are the incantations of a transcendental magician, who with each letter, each syllable, each word, manifests Kṛṣṇa and Vṛndāvana before me.

I can see, feel, touch, and smell Kṛṣṇa and His abode — all through my ears. And while you continue to speak, I remain immersed in a long-forgotten reality that completely suffuses my being with unending happiness and complete satisfaction.

Śyāma, His bodily hue is blackish, therefore He is called Śyāmasundara . . . *Navīna nīrada, nīrada* means cloud, His complexion is just like new cloud. New cloud, blackish, *rūpa*. Still He is so beautiful. Generally black is not considered very beautiful within this material world, but because His body is transcendental, even He is blackish, He is the universally attractive. *Rūpa manohara*. *Mohana-vaṁśī-vihārī*, simply when He stands with His flute, He, even though He is blackish, He becomes so attractive to everyone.

So beautiful! So attractive!

To hear those words is to taste a beauty that is the essence of all beautiful things. In the presence of that beauty everything and everyone pales.

Then you conclude, “Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura is requesting everyone, ‘Now you chant all these different names of the Lord, and save me.’ *Rākho vacana mano*: ‘My dear mind, please keep my word. Don’t refuse, go on chanting all these holy names of Kṛṣṇa.’”

Silence.

But the echo of your song, your voice, Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa-the-beautiful, resonates in that place where I am, for what seems like a few moments of eternity.

Then “click,” and Śukra enters with an armful of groceries.

It was not my first transcendental experience, nor the last. But like all others, it was the outcome of your grace. As Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura says, “It is the primary duty of the spiritual master to produce feelings of ecstasy within the hearts of his disciples.” And like other feelings of ecstasy, this one came to me, as it does to all your followers, by contact with your words. For your words contain the extraordinary potency to awaken souls to their eternal identity and bring them face to face with Kṛṣṇa. This phenomenon is described with incomparable beauty and clarity by Pṛthu Mahārāja, and by your purport to his words.

My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification of Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You. Devotees thus gradually come to the right conclusion about the value of life. My dear Lord, I therefore do not need any other benediction but the opportunity to hear from the mouth of Your pure devotee. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.20.25\]

And, from your purport:

A pure devotee always engages in the service of the Lord, taking shelter of His lotus feet, and therefore he has a direct connection with the saffron mercy-particles that are strewn over the lotus feet of the Lord. Although when a pure devotee speaks the articulation of his voice may resemble the sound of this material sky, the voice is spiritually very powerful because it touches the particles of saffron dust on the lotus feet of the Lord. As soon as a sleeping living entity hears the powerful voice emanating from the mouth of a pure devotee, he immediately remembers his eternal relationship with the Lord, although up until that moment he had forgotten everything.

Somehow, amongst the countless *jīvas* wandering aimlessly, I had the good fortune to be blessed by the saffron particles emanating from your voice. And in the same way as those particles give occasional and unexpected glimpses into the spiritual world, they are also the key for perpetual residence there.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura concludes the statement quoted above: “But, without *sādhana,* such feelings \[of ecstasy\] cannot remain a permanent reality. If a disciple wants to feel spiritual ecstasy, day and night, he must follow a strict *sādhana*.” (*Devadāsa,* Chapter 12, page 66)

The full meaning of “strict *sādhana*” is strictly following all the teachings and orders given by the spiritual master.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have a golden container in which I have been collecting one by one, year after year, the saffron particles you gift. Due to foolishness or negligence I occasionally miss some. Nonetheless, those that I have are my greatest asset, my greatest treasure. Sometimes I open the container, taste a few particles, rub them over my body. Again it is as in Plattsburgh, but more. Again it is that same Kṛṣṇa, but even more beautiful. So beautiful!

One day I will douse myself in the contents of my box and go to that place from where you are broadcasting the beauty and message of Vṛndāvana. Then perhaps I can accompany you as you sing

*yaśodā-nandana, kaṁsa-nisūdana,*  
*nikuñja-rāsa-vilāsī.*

Until that day I hope to live close to your words. So close that I never miss one saffron particle that emanates from your lotus lips. Especially when you say “Śyāma.”

Your servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. I offer my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and your devotees!

Your lotus feet are the only shelter for this foolish *jīva* and for all fallen *jīvas*. By your selfless service to your Guru Mahārāja and the fallen souls, the great *saṅkīrtana* fire is spreading everywhere, to all living entities. You came, you saw, you conquered.

I see so many souls happy when they chant the holy names, even though they don’t know anything of Kṛṣṇa or the philosophy. Just by hearing the holy name and chanting it, they become happy. This is so wonderful to see! You gave the world this simple process of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. You took that priceless gift and distributed it everywhere. You are the transcendental touchstone of mercy. You are Lord Nityānanda’s mercy. The true *rūpānuga,* the real representative of Lord Gaura and your Guru Mahārāja.

Your father wanted you to be a devotee of ŚrīmatīRādhārāṇī. This great desire was no doubt completely fulfilled. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most munificent incarnation of lord Gaura’s mercy! You are His mercy personified. I thank you for holding on to this retarded fool. Please continue to look favorably upon me. I want to be of some service and some use in your *saṅkīrtana* party.

All glories to you, my spiritual master, my father, my deliverer, my shelter — everyone’s shelter.

Please forgive my numberless offenses once again.

Thank you for everything.

Your insignificant servant, the worthless retard,  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.**

**When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?**

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, whose 111thappearance anniversary we are pleased and fortunate to celebrate this year.

I am writing my Vyāsa-pūjā offering from Phnom Penh, the capital of the Kingdom of Cambodia. It has been quite an experience since first visiting this somewhat remote and forgotten tract of land in Southeast Asia last June. I have since returned five times. From my very first *darśana* of the Mekong River, which flows from the sacred Himalayan Mountains, I have felt an irresistible attraction to this land, very much sensing that I am certainly living again within Bhāratavarṣa.

The cause of *varṇāśrama* is still very much a need of the hour. This forsaken Kingdom of Cambodia, with its rich cultural background and long-standing traditions, can give us a clue to re-establishing the mission of *varṇāśrama*. The Khmer civilization will surely benefit from Lord Caitanya’s teachings, and its people will again be able to contribute toward bringing the world closer to India’s forgotten Vedic culture. Amazingly, without the intervention or involvement of even one single ISKCON devotee, Yogeśvara Kṛṣṇa, the mystic of all mystics, is luring millions of foreign tourists from all over the world to take *darśana* of the famed Angkor Wat complex, where one finds still intact today the largest Viṣṇu temple the world has ever seen. Surely those people are receiving immense benefit by setting foot in the holy shrines of those historical monuments, where thousands of Khmer devotees would daily serve the Lord with all pomp and reverence.

By your kind mercy, soon after visiting Cambodia and after thirteen years in exile, I have finally been able to again enter the sacred land of India. I was blessed to again visit your place of worship, Śrīdhāma Māyāpur; your place of work, Juhu in Mumbai; and your place of residence, Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making this dream come true.

Every day we chant the following prayer:

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

We tend to confine the concepts of impersonalism and voidism to the Western countries. While such concepts are certainly present in the West, I had not realized until stepping foot in the Kingdom of Cambodia how this also very much applies to these Eastern countries as well.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have preached very boldly the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. In your first meeting with your Guru Mahārāja, you opined that since India was ruled by the British, what benefit would it serve to preach the message of Lord Caitanya under such conditions? You relate how your Guru Mahārāja expertly responded by explaining that political conditions will always tend to fluctuate in this material world whereas the eternal teachings and the eternal message of the Lord will always remain pure and intact. The following quote by your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda, further elaborates on this point:

There has not been, there will not be, such benefactors of the highest merit as \[Caitanya\] Mahāprabhu and His devotees have been. The offer of other benefits is only a deception; it is rather a great harm, whereas the benefit done by Him and His followers is the truest and greatest eternal benefit. . . . This benefit is not for one particular country causing mischief to another; but it benefits the whole universe. . . . The kindness that Śrī Chaitanya Mahāprabhu has shown to *jīvas* absolves them eternally from all wants, from all inconveniences, and from all the distresses. . . . That kindness does not produce any evil, and the *jīvas* who have it will not be the victims of the evils of the world. \[*Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* Chapter 3, “A Very Nice Saintly Person”\]

Such realizations from such great personalities as you and your Guru Mahārāja must somehow become my driving force in wanting to do something of significance here in the Kindgom of Cambodia. From a purely material point of view, it seems like an insurmountable task. We have yet to have a proper book printed in the Khmer language, we have yet to make any devotees, and we have barely opened a fledging preaching center. However, the few devotees who are involved are sincere at heart and long-standing, dedicated front-line soldiers.

Please bless those devotees who are endeavoring to preach in this remote part of the planet so that they may have the needed strength, courage, and determination, qualities you displayed when going to the Western countries. Unless we constantly remind ourselves of Your Divine Grace’s hard struggle to introduce and push on Kṛṣṇa consciousness against all odds, in a way similar to what the Pāṇḍavas did in the Battle of Kurukṣetra, and in a way similar to how so many of your valiant soldiers are pushing on Kṛṣṇa consciousness in other distant corners of the world, we will not be able, by our meager attempts, to succeed.

I therefore pray for your kind blessings on this auspicious day on which we celebrate the 111thanniversary of your appearance. We need no less than a miracle to turn the tide toward Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whether here in the Kingdom of Cambodia or in any other of today’s materially engrossed countries. Please continue to shower your kind mercy upon all your sincere preachers. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your ever-inspiring example and your ever-fresh instructions. Please forgive me for all my shortcomings, mistakes, and offenses. Help me become an actual devotee.

May your name and fame be spread far and wide all over the three worlds.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, my eternal divine spiritual preceptor!

Lord Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, the reservoir of all knowledge, exhibited Their perfect understanding of all the arts and sciences mentioned above. Then They offered to serve Their teacher by awarding him anything he desired. This offering by the student to the teacher or spiritual master is called *guru-dakṣiṇā*. It is essential that a student satisfy the teacher in return for any learning received, either material or spiritual. \[Chapter 45 of *Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead*\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us countless gifts, such as your books, your letters, and your conversations, all of which are full of truth and wisdom. You sacrificed your life to establish the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and you traveled worldwide to see that it was maintained properly. I am certainly indebted to you, my dear master, and it is my duty to satisfy you. But I also know that my debt to you is not an easy debt to repay.

As you write in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.47:

A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a devotee. The best friend and benefactor of all people is one who awakens humanity to its original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You are actually my best friend and benefactor. You saved me and thousands of other conditioned souls also. You continued traveling and preaching despite your advanced age. You knew that we needed you and would easily fall victim to *māyā* without your guidance. Your mood of compassion and mercy is described by Vidura to the sage Maitreya at *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.5.3:

O my lord, great philanthropic souls travel on the earth on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead to show compassion to the fallen souls who are averse to the sense of subordination to the Lord.

You kindly came into our lives and taught the real meaning of spirituality. You opened our eyes and showed us the futility of material life and the eternal beauty of the Supreme Lord. You never offered us salaries for our service, but you still wanted us to double our book distribution scores. We slept on the temple floor and ate our breakfast cereal with chickpeas and ginger off greaseproof paper while sitting on the floor. Our days were spent chanting, dancing, and distributing books on crowded streets. People saw us everywhere and thought we must be numbering in the millions, while we were actually just a few. We shaved our heads and wore robes even in the winter. We did all this because we knew you wanted us to do it and we wanted to please you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I still want to please you. I would like to please you more and more. If you are pleased, then only does my life have some meaning, then only can I begin to repay my debt to you. Please keep me engaged in your service eternally.

Your son who has no other shelter,  
Tridaṇḍi Bhikṣu Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were personally manifest in our presence, it was clear to most of your disciples that even though some were relatively senior and advanced, in comparison to you we were all micromidgets. Your position was exceptional, even among your godbrothers.

Nothing has changed. You are simply the best. There is no comparison with what you have done and how you continue to profoundly influence innumerable people. Without disrespect to the many sincere souls practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness and trying to follow in your footsteps, your extraordinariness remains axiomatically unique for those sane enough to acknowledge it.

Although it is impossible to fully understand or describe what makes you so outstanding even among saints, one major aspect is your intense desire to please guru and Kṛṣṇa, which manifests through your unflagging attempts to awaken every living being to his real identity as an eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa and to cultivate even the slightest inclination he might have toward acting in that capacity. Moreover, despite being so far above all, you consider yourself not master but servant of everyone. You are totally selfless. You have given yourself to guru and Kṛṣṇa and are one with their desire to give Kṛṣṇa to every *jīva* in the universe. You are certainly in a category apart from those who foolishly advertise or imply themselves to be more realized or intelligent than you.

As a fully surrendered servant of the *paramparā,* you never compromise for the sake of pleasing the public or gaining cheap fame or popularity. Nor do you exploit disciples to fulfill personal desires. Nor do you act the role of what others think a sādhu or *premī-bhakta* should be, but are always naturally and unaffectedly absorbed in serving Kṛṣṇa. For these and innumerable other reasons, I feel so fortunate and grateful that Kṛṣṇa sent you, and no one else, to be my guru.

To earn your blessings and properly glorify you requires imbibing your quality of wholly guileless sincerity. Neither flattery, sentimental or fanatical adulation, nor formal praise can fool or satisfy you. It seems impossible to attain the standard of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you have exemplified, but it is easy because you make it easy; that is your mercy. And that we are entirely dependent on your mercy is not simply a platitude, as evidenced by the various litters of tigers-by-your-mercy now reconverted into mice.

You are a genuine *mahā-bhāgavata,* an empowered representative of Kṛṣṇa fully capable of bestowing His mercy upon all conditioned souls and thus delivering them back to His lotus feet. Even though I am one of the most severely contaminated persons upon whom your merciful glance has fallen, you are so kind that you engage me in the exalted service of delivering your message so that others may attain the divine grace you so mercifully distribute.

I pray to always treasure these simple, quintessential, and powerful truths, upon which all our spiritual endeavors depend. Remembering your warning to protect the valuable jewel of spiritual love from thieves and burglars, and being increasingly surrounded by pseudo intellectuals, diluters, and committed doubters, I cling to the lifeline of my connection with you, without which whatever little Kṛṣṇa consciousness I have imbibed is doomed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, to aspire to be your servant is no small thing. Even great demigods and the inhabitants of Vaikuṇṭha can hardly recognize the value of such a position or become eligible for it. Yet you have planted in this otherwise thoroughly disqualified person the desire for eternal service at your lotus feet. Indeed, only you and Kṛṣṇa know how severely disqualified I am for such a boon; and only you and Kṛṣṇa are merciful and powerful enough to nonetheless make me worthy and fulfill my aspiration.

Bhakti Vikāsa Swami

## Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this all-auspicious anniversary of your appearance, during this year of the thirtieth anniversary of your divine disappearance pastime, please accept my fallen respectful obeisances again and again at the dust of your lotus feet.

*vande śrī-gurudevaṁ taṁ karuṇā-varuṇālayam*  
*yat-kṛpā-lava-leśena pāmaro ’py amarāyate*

**“I bow unto the bona fide spiritual master, that veritable ocean of compassion, a tiny fraction of whose mercy can immortalize even a vile outcaste.”**

This year we are meditating on the following theme, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*(*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.87.24)

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, who happily sport in the company of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma! Although you sport so carefree in Goloka, you also still guide and instruct us, your servants, who somehow still live while being separated from you in this morbid world.

You mentioned once that on the appearance day of the previous *ācāryas* one can directly go to them and pray for their special mercy. May I take this opportunity to pray for your special mercy today? Although that mercy is always there for those who are greedy for it, I see that only the most desperately greedy actually get that mercy.

Your mercy comes in some astonishing ways. One day, your mercy came to me in the form of the above-quoted verse (*vande śrī-gurudevam*), given to me by your surrendered disciple Mukunda Datta Dāsa. I happily offer the verse to you and to those devotees near me, who increasingly represent your mercy to me.

Thus the verse spoke out to me:

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

I, along with all the other devotees, pray to your lotus feet daily for guidance, strength, and shelter. I meditate on your divine form in my daily manasic *pūjā*. I see your smiling form come to take the offering I somehow or other arrange to bring to you so you may offer it to the Lords and Their associates.

I cry out, will you again come across the path of my eyes?

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

Oh, my master! Because I am still conditioned by this temporary material aging body, and because I am splashed by miseries pertaining to it, and thus weakened by it, I fumble while attempting to somehow continue in your footsteps, stumble around as I travel from temple to temple and country to country. By reading your pastimes, however, I see how you remained always blissful, even while suffering bodily pains on account of your mercifully accepting sinful us as your disciples. Thus you taught us then and especially now, as your disciples enter the early twilight stages of their lives. Your example rings out loud and clear:

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

I occasionally find myself grumbling when I make firm travel arrangements for preaching in your mission, only to have to subsequently change my airline itineraries. I am thus forced to tolerate, as you tolerated all of us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Yet all the while I still mumble to myself this verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (12.2.1) exposing the difficulty of this age:

*tataś cānu-dinaṁ dharmaḥ satyaṁśaucaṁ kṣamā dayā*  
*kālena balinā rājan naṅkṣyaty āyur balaṁ smṛtiḥ*

**“Then, O King, religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, tolerance, mercy, duration of life, physical strength, and memory will all diminish day by day because of the powerful influence of the Age of Kali.”**

During the present age, Kali-yuga, practically all desirable qualities will gradually diminish, as described in this verse. For example, *dharma,* which indicates a respect for higher authority that leads one to obey religious principles, will diminish.

The degradation of the age is due to the low-class birth and habits of the fallen *śūdras* who make up the vast majority of the population. We belonged to this population, Śrīla Prabhupāda, mistakenly thinking that our lower-than-*śūdra* lifestyle was normal, when it was actually abnormal and even insane! Yet if we follow your instructions, we will be lifted out of our morass.

As you teach us in your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 16.29:

Similarly, in the *Padma Purāṇa* it is said:

*na śūdrā bhagavad-bhaktās te tu bhāgavatā matāḥ*  
*sarva-varṇeṣu te śūdrā ye na bhaktā janārdane*

**“A devotee should never be considered a** ***śūdra*****. All the devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead should be recognized as** ***bhāgavatas*****. If one is not a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa, however, even if born in a** ***brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya,*** **or** ***vaiśya*** **family, he should be considered a** ***śūdra*****.”**

In the *Padma Purāṇa* it is also said:

*śva-pākam iva nekṣeta loke vipram avaiṣṇavam*  
*vaiṣṇavo varṇo-bāhyo ’pi punāti bhuvana-trayam*

**“If a person born in a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **family is an** ***avaiṣṇava,*** **a nondevotee, one should not see his face, exactly as one should not look upon the face of a** ***caṇḍāla*****, or dog-eater. However, a Vaiṣṇava found in** ***varṇas*** **other than** ***brāhmaṇa*** **can purify all the three worlds.”**

The *Padma Purāṇa* further says:

*śūdraṁ vā bhagavad-bhaktaṁ niṣādaṁśva-pacaṁ tathā*  
*vīkṣate jāti-sāmānyāt sa yāti narakaṁ dhruvam*

**“One who considers a devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead who was born in a family of** ***śūdras, niṣādas,*** **or** ***caṇḍālas*** **to belong to that particular caste certainly goes to hell.”**

Although you floated among the fallen-*śūdra* classes, by the grace of Kṛṣṇa and Gaurāṅga only very rarely did anyone disrespect you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were a follower of the Six Gosvāmīs, being dear to both the gentle and the ruffians. You charmed them all, Prabhupāda. But we see that now we have a harder time charming the opposition then you did.

You taught us to purify ourselves of our *śūdra* habits by cleansing ourselves externally with three baths a day, and internally by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra. But Śrīla Jagadānanda Paṇḍita has written, *nāmākṣara bāhirāya baṭe nāma kabhu naya:* just because the external syllables of the holy name are sounded doesn’t mean that the actual name is there.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will your mercy shine on us so that we will chant the holy names purely, with a clean mind?

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] *Satyam,* truthfulness, is also diminishing, simply because people do not know what the truth is. Without knowing the Absolute Truth, one cannot clearly understand the real significance or purpose of life merely by amassing huge quantities of relative or hypothetical truths.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave all of us the truth that in this wicked Age of Kali only the chanting of the holy name can counteract all the faults so prevalent now. But when will you bestow on us the increasing taste and ability to chant these names offenselessly?

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] *Kṣamā,* tolerance or forgiveness, is diminishing as well, because there is no practical method by which people can purify themselves and thus become free of envy. Unless one is purified by chanting the holy names of the Lord in an authorized program of spiritual improvement, the mind will be overwhelmed by anger, envy, and all sorts of small-mindedness. Thus *dayā,* mercy, is also decreasing.

When will mercifulness to all, as described below at Cc *Madhya* 15.104, increase on this planet for your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You once said that devotees are increased by three things: *harināma-saṅkīrtana*, distribution of your books, and distribution of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to one and all.

*prabhu kahena,—’kṛṣṇa-sevā, ‘vaiṣṇava-sevana’*  
*‘nirantara kara kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana’*

**\[Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied:\] Without cessation continue chanting the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Whenever possible, serve Him and His devotees, the Vaiṣṇavas.**

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] All living beings are eternally connected by their common participation in the divine existence of God. When this existential oneness is obscured through atheism and agnosticism, people are not inclined to be merciful to one another; they cannot recognize their self-interest in promoting the welfare of other living beings. In fact, people are no longer even merciful to themselves: they systematically destroy themselves through liquor, drugs, tobacco, meat-eating, sexual promiscuity, and whatever other cheap gratificatory processes are available to them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as time removes our youthfulness, sapping our strength, we marvel at your endurance in your seventies. Simultaneously we lament our former youthful foolishness in the 1970s, which you so succinctly revealed when you told one of your godbrothers in India, “All my children eat five times what they require.”

Although *prasādam* was one of the anchors that helped steady our otherwise wayward minds and keep us in your movement, you told us in your purport to *Upadeśāmṛta* 2 not to overeat in the name of *prasādaṁ seva*.

I remember how in my youth in America I joined some of your other disciples in recklessly overeating *prasādam*. In retrospect, we see how foolish that was, for many of your senior men are now bedridden, enfeebled and humbled by diseases only in middle age. We all see now how our former reckless habits have undone our health. We must act properly now to turn the tide as far as possible. Increasingly, as the years go by I see in Vṛndāvana and elsewhere more and more of your senior saintly preachers spending their last days in the *dhāmas,* leaving their bodies, which have been ravaged by cancer. It all rings out, loud and clear:

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

Forgive us for our lack of sense control, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Give us the forbearance to lead regulated lives and teach the same by example. As Kṛṣṇa teaches us at *Bhagavad-gītā* 6.17:

*yuktāhāra-vihārasya yukta-ceṣṭasya karmasu*  
*yukta-svapnāvabodhasya yogo bhavati duḥkha-hā*

**He who is regulated in his habits of eating, sleeping, recreation, and work can mitigate all material pains by practicing the yoga system.**

The tongue has two businesses, to vibrate and to eat, so rather athan eat the holy name while drinking the elixir of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, we eat other items. In the 1970s I often found that the verses all rolled off my tongue as fast as I ravenously ate *prasādam,* but when oh when will I get your mercy to genuinely understand how to follow this and the other verses you loved to quote, not only in word but in deed? *Bhagavad-gītā* 6.17 is a million-dollar verse for regulation and sense control.

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] Because of all these self-destructive practices and the powerful influence of time, the average life span (*āyur*) is decreasing.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.2.1 and its purport ring out:

*Work now to leave a lasting legacy to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

Daily I’m happy to remember you, but the memories are increasingly fading, along with the physical presence of those who were directly touched by you. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī is quoted as having said, “Kṛṣṇa takes away from our midst those that are the most dear to Him.”

Recently He took away from us, rotting here in this world in separation, four more of your disciples, leaving us to lament our losses and rejoice in their spiritual gain. And day by day your dear friend Lord Kṛṣṇa uses His infallible, unassailable time factor to take more of your and His dear servants.

By this He teaches all of us who remain here, “Serve your guru by contributing a lasting legacy, for soon, legacy or not, you must leave your body!”

*Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] *Balam,* bodily strength, is also decreasing. The Vedic literature states that five thousand years ago, in the previous age, human beings — and even animals and plants — were larger and stronger. With the progress of the Age of Kali, physical stature and strength will gradually diminish.

In 1997, in His Holiness Bhakti Caitanya Swami’s room in Durban, at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha temple, we were all reading Kuśakratha Prabhu’s translation of Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī’s *Bhakti-sandarbha*. The gist of *anucchedas* 299 and 300 has been glued to my brain ever since:

One performs Ekādaśī by eating only once on Daśamī, refraining from eating at sunset, then remaining awake the entire night while hearing and chanting the glories of the Lord. At dawn on Ekādaśī one worships the Lord, remaining awake, totally fasting from food and water the whole day and night. Before the dawn of Dvādaśī one worships the *śrī-vigraha* of the Lord again. Then at dawn on Dvādaśī one drinks a little *caraṇāmṛta* to break the fast and continues to hear and chant and worship the Lord until one’s usual lunchtime.

These words of Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmīs shook our minds! Wow, how did they do that?

But *anuccheda* 299 was not as astonishing as 300: “One who cannot do that is not a substantial Vaiṣṇava!”

Wow! How the residents of Kali-yuga have fallen from the standards of four hundred years ago! The austerities and standards of Vaiṣṇava behavior on Ekādaśī and other auspicious days, which our previous *ācāryas* observed somewhat matter-of-factly with the local village devotees, we fallen Western Vaiṣṇavas, and even those of India, can hardly dream of observing.

How did they do it, Śrīla Prabhupāda? I am begging at your lotus feet to answer this question. Very few records of the lives of the Gosvāmīs talk about the step-by-step procedures of how they performed their austerities. Those details are obscured by the passage of time. When will you be pleased by my calling out to you? When will you kindly reveal that information to lowly me?

Obviously, the humble *ācāryas* considered such talk of the details of their practices *prajalpa*, but as a result hundreds of years down the line we have no detailed road map of how to follow step-by-step in their footsteps.

Still, you instructed us to sing the *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭakam* daily, explaining that although we can’t imitate the Six Gosvāmīs of Vraja, they nonetheless remain the standard-bearers.

Oh Prabhupāda, before the last Śiva-rātri festival, I asked your venerable ninety-year-old godbrother Madana Mohana Dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja how to get the merciful benediction to be able to stay up all night and write, as you did. He said such empowerment was the extraordinary mercy of the Lord and the *bhāgavatas*! He quoted a verse from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* as *pramāṇa* to guide me, but unfortunately I could not properly understand which verse he was citing. At the time of this writing I am still bereft of it.

So now, in my early old age, I admit at your lotus feet that practically on a daily basis I am awed at the abilities you demonstrated when you physically blessed this earthly planet. For all of us are weaklings, and with each passing generation we become more and more feeble. Yet we’re proud of mundane industrial accomplishments.

No! We are all dependent perpetually on all your mercies. Sometimes I wonder if the fortunate day will ever arrive when I can get that merciful benediction to even approach your standards.

You traveled, apparently unexhausted, without being much affected by jet lag. You compassionately preached to the multitudes with open doors until 10:30 p.m. and later, yet you arose often as early as 1:00 a.m. to wash your face and translate your books, which remain the inspiration for countless devotees, the glue that holds your ISKCON society together.

How did you get the mercy to do all you did to please your Guru Mahārāja? How can we get your mercy to understand how to follow in your footsteps so we can leave a legacy worthy of you?

*Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

\[Purport to 12.2.1, continued:\] Certainly *smṛti,* memory, is weakening. In former ages human beings possessed superior memory, and they also did not encumber themselves with a terrible bureaucratic and technical society, as we have done. Thus essential information and abiding wisdom were preserved without recourse to writing. Of course, in the Age of Kali things are dramatically different.

We can’t even remember what we did at the same time yesterday. How will we remember the essential points of *Gītā* and *Bhāgavatam*?

Only if we smear ourselves with the dust of your lotus feet in the form of your instructions can we hope to be successful to even the smallest degree.

*By this you teach all who remain here, Serve your guru by contributing a lasting legacy, for soon, legacy or not, you must leave your body! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you once stated that the world is so sinful that the demigods wouldn’t even come here to answer the call of nature, we who were born in this sinful world were all attracted to and purified by you, who purified all the major countries by visiting them, touching them with your lotus feet and sending your purified representatives to help us remember you and Kṛṣṇa.

Regarding the development of *bhakti,* Lord Kṛṣṇa . . . states in the *Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad* (Uttara 4), *apūtaḥ pūto bhavati yaṁ māṁ smṛtvā, avratī vratī bhavati yaṁ māṁ smṛtvā, niṣkāmaḥ sa-kāmo bhavati yaṁ māṁ smṛtvā, aśrotrīśrotrī bhavati yaṁ māṁ smṛtvā:* “By remembering Me, one who is impure becomes pure. By remembering Me, one who follows no vows becomes a strict follower of vows. By remembering Me, one who is desireless develops desires \[to serve Me\]. By remembering Me, one who has studied no Vedic mantras becomes an expert knower of the *Vedas*.” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.87.23, purport\]

O Prabhupāda, I pray at your lotus feet for the strength to follow in your footsteps in all respects. It is by your mercy only that we can clearly understand this injunction . . .

*Serve your guru by contributing a lasting legacy, for soon, legacy or not, you must leave your body! Ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layaḥ: “Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die.”*

By your grace only are we allowed the *darśana* of our Lords Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma in the places where you inspired Their service, such as this *dhāma* of the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma farm in Mauritius, which I am presently visiting by your mercy. May your blessings continue to shine so we may always remember Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma and Their holy names wherever we go.

In closing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, while I travel here and there to help you spread your mission, I remember how you exhorted us to never ever be attracted to the illusory energy, nor to go where your devotees, who speak Kṛṣṇa’s glories, don’t reside. Yet on occasion we are forced to visit such sinful places and also to transit airports where nondevotees drone on about their wasted lives. As the *Gītā* certifies, this world is *duḥkhālayam* and *aśāśvatam*,

It is simply *andhakāra*. The whole world is . . . This is called darkness. We can . . . We experience every moment. If there was no sun, then what is the value of this world? We have got good experience. In the Western countries where there is no sun, it is hell, simply hell, simply hell without sun. All the condemned countries are devoid of sunlight. This morning we were speaking that London, it is without sunlight practically throughout the whole year. Long ago, in 1969, in the television, the television man asked me that “Where is hell?” and “It is here in London.” \[laughter\] That was published in the paper. There is such a . . . And always dark and always moist and always so cold. So this is hell. Why you have to search out hell? Here is hell. \[Class on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.28.21, Nairobi, 1 November 1975\]

Protect us, oh Prabhupāda, where your devotees are not! I pray at your lotus feet to bless me so that I may strictly follow the instruction in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.19.24:

*na yatra vaikuṇṭha-kathā-sudhāpagā*  
*na sādhavo bhāgavatās tadāśrayāḥ*  
*na yatra yajñeśa-makhā mahotsavāḥ*  
*sureśa-loko ’pi na vai sa sevyatām*

**An intelligent person does not take interest in a place, even in the topmost planetary system, if the pure Ganges of topics concerning the Supreme Lord’s activities does not flow there, if there are not devotees engaged in service on the banks of such a river of piety, or if there are no festivals of** ***sankirtana-yajna*** **to satisfy the Lord \[especially since** ***sankirtana-yajna*** **is recommended in this age\].**

May your blessings from the previous *ācāryas* be our strength to guide us in this dark Age of Kali!

Your insignificant mendicant,  
Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As we were unaware of what lay just ahead,  
The savage result lay ahead as well, but not ostentatious, rather concealed.  
The darkened half of the world could not know  
That items regarded as simple and common could be factually such fault.  
The sleep of moral ignorance was pervasive,  
Merely astride in the mode of the day,  
Merely taken by the fashions of cruelty,  
Willing subscribers to haphazard misshapen tradition,  
A nation of renegades with slight pretense of noble history  
And every reason to obscure the past in carefully crafted euphemisms,  
Schooled in the intellectual poverty and narrow-minded oppressive presumption,  
Mistaking slogans for philosophy, dogma for rationale, and fanaticism for fervor.

\* \* \*

And now happy to cast off these winter straight-laced boots of a cumbersome past,  
To embrace the springtime reckless haste of scarcely considered hedonism,  
From smug stifling provinciality to Flower-Power abandon,  
An event in modern Cosmic Time, for certain,  
A generation struggled, as all generations do,  
With issues of relevance, and issues of complacency,  
With issues of freedom, and issues of public consensus.  
As a generation and a nation we had “our sages” and “our prophets,”  
But these words “our sages” and “our prophets” demand the literary device of quotation marks.  
A generation with its famous profligates and abject failures,  
As well as once-hopeful wreckages smoldering at the borderline of popular relevance.

. . .

An arrogant experiment that forgot what it had set to prove.  
But a generation too large to conveniently sweep away its earlier loudness  
Quickly into the dustbin, to escape embarrassing questions from future descendants.  
Oddly, some became Hare Krishnas.

\* \* \*

The young find it hard to think about the future, and yet they claim everything in its name,  
While the old find it hard to make us believe in the past, and yet they claim everything in its name.  
At least the old cannot make us “believe that they themselves believe.”  
A rare swan, however, can bring out the timeless from the past  
And leave aside the aggregating blunders.  
While young, we still lack things;  
While old, we lose track of things.  
The wheel spins; in any case we are incomplete.

\* \* \*

Do we still believe that miracles can happen?  
Right here in River City?  
Just by the invocation of the Abode and Ultimate Refuge?  
Sociopolitical trends are vast, and we, tiny.  
The matrix of interaction is dizzyingly complex.  
Are we dizzy too? . . . Maybe only tired.

. . .

Away with appearances: often they lie.  
Our master has called.  
Our master has called, and each hears his voice, in a different tune.  
Our master has called, and each hears his voice, to a different task.  
The world is now, and will forever be, a curious admixture of knowledge and pipe dreams,  
And the absurd segues of popular culture will no doubt march on,  
Churning out more curiosities than the Milk Ocean —  
Certainly some poisons, too.

\* \* \*

Some will say that there is serious error,  
Some will decry the dearth of virtue and facility,  
Some will vituperate, calling our attention to improper method:  
“Cast out, castigate, look — all must see!”  
Tell me who has followed all you have said, my master? Show me that person.  
Shall we wait, then, until all can be rendered without any omission in any category — and in full measure?  
Should we not be glad for what has been fashioned, though omissive and short of plenum?  
Some simply prefer nostalgia or, we can say, somewhat fictional nostalgia.  
I prefer to think of the cup as half full.  
Rose glasses aside, I am sure we are able to build and fix.  
Seems that *bhakti*-philosophy tells us that with Kṛṣṇa, all can be made well.  
With the right demeanor, what cannot be overcome?  
Does realism really require from us a dour face?

\* \* \*

No, but it does require that I should preach.  
The world will not ask me for my opinion  
Nor invite me to come out on the street, either,  
As the world did not ask you, Srila Prabhupada, to come.  
Saints like you do something amazing,  
Quite beyond our bounds.  
Now, will I pray for their deliverance — the deliverance of the fallen?  
Will I pray for their deliverance with sincere feeling —  
Those who *now* follow “unaware of what lies just ahead”?

\* \* \*

Yet save me from hunger for personal acclaim.  
How many times has wrong ambition, disguised, laid waste such a vast scene?  
Reduce my lassitude and let me not mire in ambivalence,
