Nor allow my minor resolve to extinguish, snuffed beneath technicalities.  
Arise; go to the lectern; speak  
An improbable message to an unlikely nation.  
But someone else has dared the same and gone before.

\* \* \*

Another year waits as I write;  
Another year will have passed as I read.  
I am sure that progress will attend, just I keep the simple vigil of daily practice,  
That the name of God will carry and preserve — I believe  
Progress will attend.  
Success will be the outcome, and I wish to be instrumental  
Someway, somehow, it matters not how.  
It matters not how.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear beloved Guru Mahārāja, Your Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I think the best way for me to glorify you and hopefully please you, on this most auspicious day of your appearance-day anniversary, is to give a short description of what I observed this past year while living in India.

With the aim of fulfilling your desire for the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium, I had the exceptionally good fortune of assisting your devotees in understanding the cosmological science of the universe. By your causeless mercy, the following works were translated from Sanskrit and Bengali into English and published: 1) the commentaries of ten Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* Fifth Canto cosmology (chapters 16–26), 2) cosmological sections from the *Vāyu Purāṇa, Viṣṇu Purāṇa, Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa*, and *Liṅga Purāṇa,* 3) the *Sūrya-siddhānta* and *Siddhānta-śiromaṇi* (the works of Śrīla Bīmala Prasāda Siddhānta Sarasvatī), 4) Bhūgola Bhāgola Viṣayā— a summary of Vaiṣṇava astronomer Śrī Thiruvenkata Jeeyar Swami’s writings and diagrams, etc.

These works corroborate your own writings about the structure of the universe. Furthermore, your Vedic planetarium concept has captured the appreciation of all schools of Vedic thought — including scholars from the Mādhva-sampradāya, the Śrī-sampradāya, the Siddhāntikas, the followers of Śaṅkarācārya, etc. This planetarium will also open the minds of Westerners who are eager to learn the truth about the universe and its original intelligent designer.

ISKCON flourishes in India. Your followers widely preach in universities throughout the country. Several thousand students attend an annual three-day ISKCON seminar with leading devotees. The *brahmacārī āśrama* is alive and well in your Indian centers, insuring extensive preaching for the present and fine spiritual leadership for the future.

It was so enlivening to see thousands of visitors coming to the ISKCON centers daily. Indeed, on one regular Sunday, 135,000 visitors came to the ISKCON Juhu temple. In December, the Delhi and Juhu temples distributed 112,000 and 150,000 hardback *Gītās,* respectively. The standards of cleanliness, management, Deity worship, and preaching are superb, yet the leader of one such temple humbly asked how granddisciples could ever perform substantial service to the Lord.

Gorgeous new temples such as Ujjain and Tirupati open regularly and immediately become the cynosures of their cities. Not only one temple per city, but many ISKCON temples flourishing in Delhi and Mumbai demonstrate a “big-ISKCON” vision of cooperation and expansion.

I heard one of your disciples who is a temple president in India deliver a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class that was so śāstrically astute that I could not help thinking how pleased you would be to hear it. All in all, it seems that ISKCON India has set the standard for our movement, and thus naturally other parts of the world should emulate its success.

Kindly forgive my extreme slowness in learning and applying your sublime instructions. To become a genuine devotee and please you is my dominant desire. Therefore I bow down at your feet and beg for the opportunity to serve you perpetually.

Your insignificant servant,  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This year finds me one notch closer to the end of the limb that is my fragile life. In this condition, I am remembering all the exciting years of traveling *saṅkīrtana* that you mercifully engaged me in. Indeed, my coming to the shelter of your lotus feet and subsequent engagement in your preaching campaign are the only worthwhile memories of my life. I remain hungry for the experience of being used as an instrument to connect sincere seekers with our Lord, and I must confess that attachment to this endeavor still shadows my attachment to Kṛṣṇa, or to entering His abode. Thus, my only recourse is to humbly petition Your Divine Grace to afford me the opportunity to recommence my meager preaching efforts, either in a revitalized body or another of the Lord’s choice. Thank you for your kind consideration.

Aspiring for more of your service,  
Gaṇapati Dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

It has been accepted among the body of Vaiṣṇavas that you have accomplished in this world what only a *śakty-āveśa avatāra* could accomplish. Indeed, millions of people know of your unparalleled preaching and writing, and even those who are not *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas* are appreciating all that you have done. However, when your followers meditate on your glories, it is often not these great accomplishments that come to mind, but rather it is your simple lovable qualities and intimate pastimes with us that most fascinate and endear us to you.

Today I am meditating on one of those lovable qualities: your transcendental smile. You have blessed me with it on some very special occasions, and it has become an integral aspect of my meditation.

When I was serving in Los Angeles in 1973, I was so fortunate to get your *darśana* and hear your classes every day. The temple atmosphere was surcharged with your presence. I remember how every morning at 6:00 a.m., just before you would go out to take your walk, all the devotees would line up at the bottom of the stairs that led to your apartment. We all chanted in great anticipation, waiting for that supremely relishable treat of having the day’s first *darśana* of you. When the door to your quarters finally opened, there you would be, standing looking down at us with that incomparable smile that always lit up our hearts and our lives as nothing had ever done before. It filled us with your love and your approval. It told us that you were happy to be with us. And it gave us the security that in spite of all our offenses you had accepted us unconditionally and eternally as your sons and daughters.

I also meditate on a famous photo of Your Divine Grace performing the first *ārati* for Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma at the grand opening in Vṛndāvana in 1975. I had the great fortune to be present for that precious event. The temple room was completely packed with devotees and guests that day, all of us preparing to witness the momentous occasion of Their Lordships’ first *ārati*. Right at noon I had somehow been shoved right in front of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma’s altar. Soon the conch was blown, and then came the breathtaking *darśana* of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma along with their *pūjārī,* who, to my surprise, was none other than your divine self. For me this was the benediction of a lifetime and the ultimate perfection of the eyes. Never before had I seen such a transcendental combination. And to make this perfectly complete sight even more complete, when you turned to offer the peacock fan to the devotees, you were glowing with happiness, your transcendental smile once again lighting up the world. That was the very moment when that famous photo of you was taken, perfectly commemorating that wonderful day and encapsulating your ecstatic mood in history. Finally you had achieved your long-cherished goal of establishing the service of your dear Lords in Their eternal home of Vṛndāvana and providing all of your followers direct access to Their divine lotus feet. After years of intense devotion and hard work, you had seen your Lords come in all Their splendor to personally receive your love. Your smile was the embodiment of that love and represented the fruits of your countless endeavors to please Them.

As I was offering breakfast to a picture of you this morning, I relished yet another one of your all-attractive smiles and was reminded of how dear this photo is to me. It was taken in 1975 outdoors as you sat on your *vyāsāsana,* when you blessed the San Diego *yātrā* with your presence at our Spring Festival in Balboa Park. It had been a marathon, organizing both your visit and the festival simultaneously. And because I was in charge of the temple, it was a particularly challenging time for me — so much so that during your stay I could hardly keep track of all the offenses I was committing. Feeling ashamed of my incompetence, every time I looked at you I read nothing but displeasure on your face. Soon afterwards I was shown the photos of the event, and I was amazed at what I saw: In every one of them you were smiling with great satisfaction. To me it was as if they were photos of a different festival, one in which I did not have the opportunity to let you down. I realized then how merciful, tolerant, and transcendental you were — how, in spite of so much ineptitude, and contrary to my mistaken vision of how you had been seeing things, you were able to truly appreciate all our faulty efforts and relish with great pleasure what meager service we offered you. Once again your smile said it all, and it lives with me daily whenever I stumble and seek your encouragement and support.

I am also remembering with great happiness when at your *darśanas,* during your walks, and in your classes you would smile, make jokes, and laugh. At such times absolutely everyone in your presence would smile and laugh with you. Your humor was indeed always very funny, and moreover, your way of expressing it communicated another type of joy, an inner joy, and also a fearlessness and understanding of life that drew us in to you and enabled us to transcend the suffering of this miserable and dangerous world. Your realized smile enlightened us, protected us, and comforted us. It is just what we needed and still need to carry on.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.28.32) Lord Kapiladeva says:

A devotee should meditate on the most benevolent smile of the Lord, a smile which, for those who bow to Him, dries away the ocean of tears caused by intense grief.

I pray that I can bow lower and lower to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, life after life. Your benevolent smile, just like the Lord’s, is miraculously drying up my ocean of tears brought about by lifetimes of sin. I had no hope for deliverance, and certainly did not deserve it, but you suddenly and causelessly swooped me up from my misery and showered all good fortune upon me. Your joyful association has proven contagious, because for the first time since I came to this material world I am learning what it actually means to smile, and there is certainly no boon greater than this. You are uncovering your devotees’ eternally blissful nature. You have set us free to chant and dance in transcendental happiness all over the planet. In fact, you are setting the entire planet free by your ecstatic *saṅkīrtana* of the holy names. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! May your smile forever light up the world!

Your insignificant, unworthy servant,  
Guṇagrāhi Dāsa Goswami

## Janānanda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This is my first written offering in many years. Thank you for bestowing upon me the order of *sannyāsa* through His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja, which has afforded me this opportunity to write. As I reflect on what to write, my memory goes back to Ratha-yātrā day, 1972, in London . . .

It’s a long story, but after the festival in Trafalgar Square, I had to go back alone to the temple in Bury Place to receive initiation from you there while practically everyone else remained at the festival. Alone, I nervously entered your room and paid obeisances. Then, after the usual questions, you handed me the beads. I again bent down to offer you obeisances. “Put your beads down and offer obeisances properly,” you instructed me. “Put both hands on the floor. Offer obeisances properly.”

I often reflect on that simple instruction and realize that even to this day I am not following it even mechanically. Offer properly! Both hands on the floor means much more than meets the eye. Both hands tightly holding your lotus feet. Smearing the dust of your lotus feet all over my body — not just the physical frame but the subtle mind and intellect. Making the order of the spiritual master my life and soul. I question myself. What conceptions do I have when “offering obeisances”— am I really offering or offending? Do I mean it, or am I mean and miserly — holding on to my plans and thoughts? Am I surrendering consciously at your lotus feet? I hope that having accepted *sannyāsa* this year by your mercy, I will now make conscious service to you a real priority and not let my service be just a show.

“Both hands on the floor.” Oh! Now that appears even more difficult, at least externally. You said a *sannyāsī* should always be with two things — his beads and the *daṇḍa*. Now — two hands full. “Put the beads down and offer your obeisances properly, with both hands on the floor.” The deeper meaning of “properly”— with surrender. Put everything at the disposal of the spiritual master. Have nothing in those hands. A clean slate to fill. No hidden agendas now. Depend on nothing else. Ultimately we can depend on nothing but the mercy of the spiritual master. It is all that we are made of. Everything else is fallible (fall-able). We can’t go back to Godhead with a bead bag and *daṇḍa*. We have to fall flat at the lotus feet of Śrī Gurudeva in all humility.

In your room at initiation you also explained the meaning of the name you gave me. “Servant of the one who brings *ānanda,* or pleasure, to others.” Another meditation . . . How can I follow that? Ah! Here maybe:

\[20 Feb 1970, in LA. Śrīla Prabhupāda speaks to Viṣṇujana Prabhu at his *sannyāsa* initiation:\]

Now chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Dance. So, this is now formal accepting of *sannyāsa*, but real *sannyāsa* purpose will be fulfilled when you’ll be able to induce the people of the world dancing like you. That is real *sannyāsa*. This formal dress is not *sannyāsa*. Real *sannyāsa* is when you can induce other people to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and they dance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Okay, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I will try! “No!” you reply, “don’t try — Just do it!” Be fearless. Bold like in days of old! Just go everywhere chanting and dancing. Is that enough?

\[From an April 1976 morning walk in Vṛndāvana:\]

So if you are engaged, *satatam,* always, in *kīrtana,* then you are safe. *Māyā* . . . will not be able to touch.

\[And from *The Path of Perfection,* compiled from your “Yoga System” tape series:\]

When chanting the *mahā-mantra,* we are completely safe, even in this most dangerous position.

Dance and chant! It sounds easy. It’s the real medicine to cure our disease. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu was criticized by the Māyāvādīs for doing it, but He converted them all and they all become intoxicated dancers and chanters. You stated, “So this movement is so important. Somehow or other if you can engage them in chanting and dancing, the whole world will be united.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, people on this planet are really appreciating the chanting and dancing now more than ever before. They are so attracted to happy, sincere devotees. They are devoid of happiness, and when they see the devotees their faces light up. Many of them dance, chant, and laugh. I get the realization that first we just have to go out and chant and be happy. Become absorbed in chanting and dancing. Other things, like dress and musical ability, come second. The most frequent question I hear is “Where have you guys been all these years?” In the early years of ISKCON so much energy was given to *saṅkīrtana*. Now it’s a luxury for some devotees to go onto the streets one hour a week. The *jīvas,* however, are looking for the “real” persons. We are supposed to be just that. There is such a great need to share the holy names with them. Happiness is the medium. I wish I really desired that they get the mercy, that they also become ecstatic chanters.

\[A conversation in Bombay, December 1976:\]

**Gopāla Kṛṣṇa:** We’ll make the *kīrtana* so ecstatic that they are jumping.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, he can dance. He can dance.

**Hari-śauri:** Then they jump and then —

**Prabhupāda**: Dance and chant. This is best exercise. We allow them to dance very . . . Yes. High jump. Actually that is exercise and at the same time ecstasy. If they dance and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, it is automatically a very big exercise and spiritual advancement. Yes. Yes. There is no doubt about it. If he chants and dances, immediately he becomes ecstatic.

\[And this from your purport to the song *Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nityānander Dayā,* by Locana Dāsa Ṭhākura:\]

But Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda introduced a process that is simply joyful — simply chant and dance. . . . Don’t think that this chanting and dancing will not lead to the desired goal. It will. It is the assurance of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu that one will get all perfection by this process. Therefore one must chant with firm faith and conviction.

\[And also this, from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 9.50:\]

\[Translation:\] When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the great gardener, sees that people are chanting, dancing, and laughing and that some of them are rolling on the floor and some are making loud humming sounds, He smiles with great pleasure.

\[Purport:\] This attitude of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is very important for persons engaged in the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In every center of our institution, ISKCON, we have arranged for a love feast every Sunday, and when we actually see people come to our center, chant, dance, take *prasādam,* become jubilant, and purchase books, we know that certainly Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is always present in such transcendental activities, and He is very pleased and satisfied. Therefore the members of ISKCON must increase this movement more and more, according to the principles that we are presently trying to execute. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, thus being pleased, will smilingly glance upon them, bestowing His favor, and the movement will be successful.

Ah! *Prasādam* — thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That’s the third thing that happened in your room that memorable day of initiation. With your own divine hand you gave me my then favorite sweet, an enormous, succulent “simply wonderful.” All of us greatly treasured any *prasādam* from your own hand, and a devotee eagerly waiting outside your room for some remnants forced me to share it with him.

Where would we be without this mercy? Would any of us be here? I certainly wouldn’t. *Catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda* . . . You are happy when we honor *bhagavat-prasādam*. I pray that I may actually learn to honor *bhagavat-prasādam* and enthuse others to also honor it. This alone is sufficient. The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is full of so many *prasādam* pastimes! I’ve become lax in this aspect of devotional service, too, and the deep absorption and meditation on the prayers before, during, and after honoring *prasādam* are well in the background. Sometimes I look at *prasādam* as food — something I like or dislike. I pray that I may develop the proper conceptions while performing this most important service.

That wasn’t all that happened at that memorable time in your room. The rest will manifest in due course. By your mercy alone I am still here — trying to offer obeisances, dance, chant, and honor *prasādam*. I may not be much use in most areas, but I pray, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you kindly bestow upon me your mercy so that I can serve the Vaiṣṇavas by chanting, dancing, and serving *prasādam*. Below is my prayer. Although perhaps I am unworthy to offer it or expect its fulfillment, I offer it nonetheless. Who else but you, my eternal father, can I appeal to?

Oh for the day when I can serve all of your followers *prasādam*! All the devotees sitting together, young and old, newcomers and seniors — everyone relishing *prasādam*. When will I be blessed with the opportunity to serve them all? In this life it may not be possible. Haridāsa Ṭhākura hankered for the opportunity to receive the remnants of the devotees’ *prasādam* lifetime after lifetime, and I hanker for the benediction to serve *prasādam*. Please give me the strength and mercy to serve *prasādam* to the devotees lifetime after lifetime. Perhaps it will be possible only in a spiritual body. I thus lament, since that is a long way off.

Oh for the day when I will be able to dance and chant tirelessly in loving ecstasy, forgetting all bodily concepts, with all of your followers, casting off all external concerns.

In November 1968 you wrote to Devānanda Prabhu:

Simply our prayer should be, “My dear Krishna, please remind me to always chant Your Holy Name, please do not put me into forgetfulness. You are sitting within me as Supersoul, so You can put me into forgetfulness or into remembering You. So please do not put me into forgetfulness. Please always remind me to chant, even You send me into the hell, it doesn’t matter, just so long as I can always chant Hare Krishna.”

I pray to quit this body anywhere, as long as I can be serving your devotees *prasādam* or be on *harināma*. I don’t care where it is, because I know I will be safe. I know these are at the moment external understandings. I pray that they become internal and pleasing to you. Not that only when the body is fit can I serve. The reality of this world is staring us in the face. Our godbrothers and -sisters are passing away only too quickly. This body is fallible. We cannot depend on it or anything of this world. Only your mercy is safe, and the mercy of the holy names.

My existence is only for your pleasure and the pleasure of the Vaiṣṇavas. I hope and pray that I will serve you eternally to your satisfaction.

The eternal servant of your servants,  
Janānanda Goswami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Poetic language and poetic girls,  
Poetic *māyā* — God! The subtlety  
Of pictures whispered by a voice within,  
Pretending to convey us to the truth  
And guide us safely. Damn our minds! We’re lost —  
Why lost? Why damned in mind? What benefit  
From listening to a tricky subtle voice  
When Kṛṣṇa’s message, powerful and clear,  
Can trash the whispers, soothe the troubled mind,  
Replace false pictures with reality?  
Let’s celebrate! Our Prabhupāda came  
To give that message, drive away the fog,  
To send illusion packing, rescue us,  
And bring us back to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.  
Our rescuer! Our Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
He spoke, he sang, he wrote, he sailed and risked,  
He gave himself, he stayed awake at night  
To publish Kṛṣṇa’s glories to the world  
And bring that cowherd, playing on His flute,  
To one forgotten soul — just even one.  
Now Kṛṣṇa’s dancing, here and everywhere.  
He’s dancing in the bead and on the tongue:  
Vṛndāvan, cows, and *gopīs* in our hearts  
And Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa-*līlā*, deep within,  
All manifest in Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.  
Our life’s for Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is our life.  
All by Śrīla Prabhupāda’s resolve  
To carry out the order he received  
And hand it on to us. May strength be ours  
To keep within our hearts and carry forth  
That sacred order. May our life be his.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Kadamba Kānana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your disciples, who have become wonderful instruments of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy!

With the passage of time, some of your disciples are leaving this world. We remember them for all the devotional service they did, out of love for you.

You conquered their hearts with your most magnanimous mood. You truly gave everything you had to make the whole world Kṛṣṇa conscious — or maybe the whole universe: *ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana. “*Your fame is spread all over the three worlds.” Or maybe you are now preaching in another universe. Who is with you now? Or are you in the spiritual world, surrounded by disciples and granddisciples in that other ISKCON, which you said we would have up there?

I am still here in this world, where in 1965 you crossed the Atlantic on the *Jaladuta* to start this movement and in the following twelve years circled the globe fourteen times. You are with me every day of my life, and now every day of the lives of many who never met you in person. You are the teacher by example. You have instilled faith in my heart that I may attain love of God one day. You are the kind and gentle, grandfatherly friend who is always there as my shelter. Yet simultaneously you are the stern, lionlike *ācārya* from whom I hide and who in one glance exposes my false ego. But above all, I implicitly trust you!

At first I tried to avoid your movement by avoiding your devotees and your books. But I got the *Gītā* as a gift. Then I could no longer ignore you, for you called out for complete surrender to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa on every page of that book and showed it by your own example. By my connection with you, I see a miracle happening within myself: even I am becoming a devotee of Kṛṣṇa.

In a *Bhāgavatam* lecture you spoke about miracles:

Sometimes, you know, people say that I have done miracles. They say everywhere. But I do not know anything, miracles or magic. If there is any miracle, that miracle is that we present things as they are. That’s all. Without any adulteration. So that should be the principle. Present as it is. It will be accepted. \[Vṛndāvana, 22 October 1972\]

All I can say in response to this is that it is a great relief to get the straight and simple truth in the mad and confused world we live in.

By your mercy I now so much relish chanting the holy name and distributing it all over the planet to people who are lost that it has become an addiction. I cannot stop. And yet another part of me wants to hide in a quiet corner and just read *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* without end. Now I am in love with Kṛṣṇa’s Deity form and have become a beggar begging for a little service to His lotus feet.

I fall at your lotus feet, overwhelmed with gratitude, and ask you and your servants what I can do to repay all this inconceivable mercy.

Your servant,  
Kadamba Kānana Swami

## Krishna dasa Swami

My dear most revered Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *koṭi koṭi daṇḍavat pranāms* at the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kali-yuga’s contamination is raining stronger each day. Severe climatic changes are being experienced around the globe, a sure sign of punishment. The dawn of an unhealthy future has come, and yet human beings do not change their evil ways. The general masses of people totally distrust their leaders, and the standard of life is deteriorating faster than ever before. In spite of all that, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy your dearly loved ISKCON is still going on and is attracting more and more innocent people. Thank you for giving us the *maṅgala-āratis*, the book distribution, and the chanting of the holy name, which is shielding us against Kali-yuga’s contamination. As Śrīla Vyāsadeva has said in the *Kali-santaraṇa Upaniṣad:*

*iti ṣoḍaśakaṁ nāmnāṁ*  
*kali-kalmaṣa-nāśanaṁ*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in Kali-yuga those who are sincerely trying to follow dharma are at a loss in finding the Absolute Truth, even though there are so many channels of information available, such as the Internet and TV. In the case of TV, there are so many religious channels supposedly trying to turn people’s minds to God, to the truth. This type of media has really become very popular in Bhārata, and many of the so-called speakers of dharma have become popular, as if they were experts on the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

However, just as you warned your followers in the introduction to the *Bhagavad-gītā,* the majority of such speakers are impersonalists and are using the *Gītā* for their own popularity. It is the blind leading the blind. These so-called experts are disseminating their own conclusions, sinking their followers further into darkness and confusion. As Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura said, so much wrong is going on in the name of religion! If it wasn’t for your translated scriptures, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there would be no hope for future generations.

Lord Caitanya’s magnanimous *audarya* (compassion) is working very nicely in making love of Godhead freely available for the sincere devotees and the innocent public at large. However, for the wicked and the selfish bigoted leaders and their supporters and followers, we need Śrī Kalki to come and clean up this earth, which is a beautiful gift of the Lord’s *sandhinīśakti* so we can all freely enjoy the promised golden period of *bhakti* promised to us.

When I was in Vṛndāvana this year for the *abhiṣeka* ceremony during the Gaura-pūrṇimā festival, I was delighted to receive an English translation of the *Kalki Purāṇa,* by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsadeva. It was translated by His Grace Bhūmipati Dāsa and dedicated to Your Divine Grace. I feel this should have been translated years ago so we could all pray to Śrī Kalki to come down now and stop the widespread corruption dominating the whole world, where life has no value and the modern way of life is the seed of discontentment and chaos.

I have been praying for the intervention of Śrī Kalki since I discovered that Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s cows are no longer sacred in Bhārata due to its huge export industry in cow flesh and leather. How painful it is for the sādhus and devotees to live with this sad fact! Śrī Kṛṣṇa is Gopāla, He is Govinda, and His place is Goloka, and yet we, His devotees, are helpless. The only option left is to pray to the Lord for His *avatāra* Śrī Kalki to come and solve this serious problem.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy I am still able to travel to distant lands and preach. I was therefore delighted to be involved in the opening of another ISKCON center in South Africa in February of this year, the ISKCON Ladysmith Preaching Center. Your granddaughter Patita-pāvanī Devī Dāsī was instrumental in the opening of this center, and I’m sure you would be very proud of her for this service to Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

I am like an old soldier reminiscing about the past pioneering days of our missionary work, when I had a lot of youthful energy. Now I am admiring the next wave of young devotees who are enamored of your books, your temples, and the process of *bhakti* you have instituted. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! May I never let you down in the quest to win people’s hearts and make them Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Your humble servant,  
Krishna Dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on the occasion of the 111th anniversary of your divine appearance day. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

**Full Prabhupāda Consciousness**

On 3 December 1975, as your Indian, Canadian, and French disciples sat before you, you conducted the fire *yajña* for the *sannyāsa* initiations of Lokanāth Dāsa, Śrīdhara Dāsa, and Pṛthu-putra Dāsa in the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple courtyard in Vṛndāvana.

On 3 March 2007 we conducted another *sannyāsa* ceremony in the same Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple courtyard, awarding *sannyāsa* to your dear disciple Janānanda Prabhu. We decided to conduct this ceremony to commemorate the *sannyāsa* ceremony you had conducted thirty-two years ago.

When it was time for Janānanda to change into his three-piece *sannyāsa* suit, we suggested he use the same room, at the left side of the temple entrance, that the three of us had once used to change into our *sannyāsa* robes.

Then when it was time to give a lecture, I decided not to manufacture a new lecture but to repeat your own words:

Preaching is also not very difficult because you haven’t got to manufacture anything. Everything is there, and it is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s order. Caitanya Mahāprabhu also took *sannyāsa* at a very early age, twenty-four years old only. So He has practically shown by His activities how to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. And He gives order to everyone, ‘*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa’*: “In whichever country you may live, it doesn’t matter. Try to deliver them by becoming their guru.”

If you follow it strictly, you also think of Kṛṣṇa yourself, and you teach others, “My dear sir, my only request is that you think of Kṛṣṇa,” nobody will kill you. If he doesn’t follow, he will appreciate you: “Oh, these *sannyāsīs* are very nice. They are advising to think of Kṛṣṇa.” Then you become guru. Simple thing.

It was a perfect lecture because it was your own lecture. After thirty-two years it was still the most relevant for the occasion.

After the ceremony in 1975 we had followed you into your living quarters, wanting to know our new names. You responded by saying “add Swami to your names.” Following in your footsteps, we named the candidate Janānanda Goswami, because generally if the candidate was married before you would give him the title Goswami.

In this way we spent the whole day in full Prabhupāda consciousness.

**Heaven on Earth**

Recently on Rāma Navamī day the grand opening of your Heaven on Earth project took place in Mumbai, fulfilling yet another of your dreams. It was a grand occasion, with thousands in attendance. On this occasion I was reminded of your inquiry regarding the Juhu temple guest attendance.

I was visiting Your Divine Grace at Kartikeya Mahadeviya’s place. You asked me how many visitors were attending the evening *ārati* and *kīrtana* at the Juhu temple. I replied, “These days a lot of visitors are coming, Prabhupāda.”

“Oh? So, how many?” you wanted to know.

I informed you that around fifty to sixty people came every evening.

“Oh, so many!” you exclaimed, expressing great satisfaction.

Today the Juhu property attracts thousands of visitors every day. At any given moment there must be fifty to sixty people taking *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārī. If you were pleased in 1973 with our tiny evening program attendance, you must be pleased hundreds of times over by the preaching going on at ISKCON’s Juhu property now. And what to speak of the additional numbers at the Rādhā-Gopīnātha temple in Chowpatty and at the Rādhā-Giridhārī temple on Mira Road?

**Kārttika Car Crash Conclusion**

One day last Kārttika we were driving along a lonely road toward Govardhana at 4:20 a.m. It being the early morning hours, the air was cool and misty. Suddenly the driver of the Tata Sumo spotted a tree lying across the road. He braked and swerved to miss the tree, and as a result the car tipped over and rolled.

I was caught by my left hand, which was stuck in the side of the seat. The weight of the car held me tightly. Although the rest of my body was free, I could not get out of the car, though I was desperate to do so.

There is only one other time I can recall being in a similar situation. One day fifteen to twenty years ago, my right hand was grabbed by our Padayātrā elephant, Lakṣmī. The elephant held me in its grip for about fifteen seconds. I was struggling, but there was no way I could free myself. Only by the elephant’s kindness was I released.

After the car crash, the other devotees in the vehicle jumped out, and realizing I was stuck, they tried to lift the car. After a few minutes I was pulled out, to my great relief.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as I contemplated my narrow escape, I began to reflect more deeply that, actually, I am caught tightly in the grip of the Lord’s illusory energy.

I feel as if my life has been extended. The accident has made me sober and has transformed me. It has brought me a little closer to the Lord, Your Divine Grace, and the devotees.

Time is limited. Time is Kṛṣṇa. It should be spent to gain Kṛṣṇa and nothing else. There is no time for anything else but Kṛṣṇa’s interest.

The situation reminds me of an analogy. A rich man doesn’t have to worry how often or how much he spends, but a poor man has to think twice before spending even a penny because he doesn’t have so many pennies at his disposal. I feel that I am becoming a bit elderly and don’t have so many “pennies” left, but that I just got a little extension of time here on this planet. In my situation I must be very frugal while spending that time.

Your humble servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, I want to thank you for allowing me to take part in pioneering Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Democratic Republic of Congo with my Congolese disciple Bāla Nitāi Dāsa Adhikārī and others. It will never be possible to thank you enough, of course, as it is the mercy of Lord Caitanya spreading causelessly. It is all because of your donated French BBT books that we are here at all in so-called “darkest Africa.” People here who study your books are very impressed that this is not just another dogmatic sectarian teaching but is a science of God for everyone, regardless of caste, color, or creed.

As you say in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.1:

In other words, the executive heads of all states are intended to spread the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that the people may take advantage of this great science and pursue a successful path, utilizing the opportunity of the human form of life.

After its first election, Congo is trying to reconstruct itself. We hope that through your books we can introduce the knowledge of *sanātana-dharma* into its veins, even into the remnants of the ancient royal tribal cultures that still exist. Over many centuries this area has been chaotic because of so much colonial and military interference, slave trading, and commercial exploitation. The good side is that this society’s traditional roots, which are similar to aspects of our Vedic culture, have not yet been obliterated by Western materialism. So there is a special chance here.

Some Africans ask, “Is this Hindu culture?” and we reply as you have taught us:

First of all we have to understand what we are. Am I this body or something else? This is the first question. I was trying to answer this, but some people in my audience thought it was a kind of Hindu culture. It is not Hindu culture. It is a scientific conception. You are a child for some time. Then you become a boy. Then you become a young man, and then you become an old man. In this way you are always changing bodies. This is a fact. It is not a Hindu conception of religion. It applies to everyone.

*dehino ’smin yathā dehe kaumāraṁ yauvanaṁ jarā*  
*tathā dehāntara-prāptir dhīras tatra na muhyati*

**“As the embodied soul continuously passes, in this body, from boyhood to youth to old age, the soul similarly passes into another body at death. A sober person is not bewildered by such a change.” \[*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.13\]**

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* everything is explained very logically, very scientifically. It is not a sentimental explanation. \[*The Journey of Self-Discovery*\]

Although for our protection, financial support, and practical social intercourse you sometimes evoked ISKCON’s cultural classification as a “Hindu organization,” from the devotional and philosophical point of view, you kept on and on, time and time again, trying hard to stop us from being identified as Hindus or anything else of this temporary world. For instance:

Unless we have got spiritual understanding, we’ll have to present ourself with this bodily designation. This is designation: “I am Hindu,”“I am Muslim,”“I am Christian,”“I am Bengali,”“I am Sindhi,”“I am Punjabi,”“I am American.” These are all bodily conception of life. And so long we are in the bodily concepts of our life, we are no better than the animals — cats and dogs. That is the statement of Vedic literature. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture in Hong Kong, 25 January 1975\]

If one is learned *paṇḍita,* he does not see Hindu, Muslim, Christian. I went to America. I did not go there to turn the Christian to become Hindu. No, I never said that. Did I say, any, anyone, that “You are Christian; you become a Hindu”? No, never I said. That is not my business. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, Nairobi, 27 October 1975\]

So this is the process. You cannot understand Kṛṣṇa, sitting in your parlor and speculating on your so-called educational qualification. That is not possible. That is not possible. You must surrender. You must surrender. *Sarva-dharmān parityajya mām* . . . You must give up all these idea that “I am Hindu,”“I am Muslim,”“I am Christian,”“I am Indian,”“I am American,”“I am white,”“I am black.” These designations, you have to give up. First qualification: *sarvopādhi-vinirmuktam*. *Upādhi,* these are all designations. Bhagavad-gita lecture, Bombay, 18 February 1974\]

In my travels in many African countries, I often take shelter of immigrant Indian Hindu families, and I am very much indebted to them for their kind friendship, hospitality, and support in our preaching efforts. Unfortunately, though, I can see that the native African people are generally suspicious and not very fond of Hindu Indians, because although the Hindus are good at making money and quite well behaved, they are a very introverted community that keeps its teachings and culture to itself. They do not encourage the locals to join their temple worship. They are unlike the Christians and Muslims, who like to boldly preach their gospel and find ways to expand their congregations. The Hindus make no attempt to teach even their own African drivers, cooks, pot-washers, gardeners, shop assistants, and other servants about their God or gods. So here in Congo and elsewhere Hinduism exists as a kind of expat Indian social club. Thus local Africans mistake the Hindu community for something like a secret society that, with their idols, may even do some kind of sinister magic.

As you said in a letter to your African preachers:

If you are living in an old Hindu Temple, that is very nice. Yes, actually this Hindu religion is a dead religion. In India, too, and in Africa, we have been offered many empty Hindu temples to take over and manage. This Hindu religion has no philosophy; therefore it has died because in this age people have become very much hardened by material living and they are not much interested in sentimental religions like Hinduism. Sentiments are temporary, and they always dry up. But what the people really want is a philosophy to give their life meaning and guide it under all changing circumstances — and the only philosophy available nowadays is profit, where is profit for sense gratification, or Marxism, or this -ism or that -ism. But none of these so-called philosophies have proven very successful in satisfying the people. Therefore, there is trouble all over the world, dissatisfaction, and people are taking to the only philosophy left, or hopelessness philosophy. But our Kṛṣṇa philosophy is bringing real meaning and hope to the modern people. We are opening up the dead churches and temples, so we may be certain that because our philosophy offers the substantial basis for everyone’s life and the solution to all kinds of problems of miserable material life, that very soon the prediction of Lord Caitanya will come true and all men of the world will find shelter at the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa. \[Letter to Vaikuṇṭhanātha and Patita Pāvana, 4 February 1972\]

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have delivered Lord Caitanya’s new life to the dead branches of religions, redeeming all of us with the universal teachings of *sanātana-dharma* you have received in disciplic succession. You are unifying the world with your books and your ISKCON.

Sometimes in ISKCON we may be foolish and take your words about faith, designations, and *sanātana-dharma* as if they were elementary teachings and thus overlook their profundity. We may proudly look for higher knowledge without having understood the basics. We may also get influenced by the Hindu designation while trying to do a balancing act, engaging the Hindu congregation in devotional service, *sanātana-dharma*. But your words coming in *guru-paramparā* remain stark truth, like great blazing beacons, which are the very foundation of knowledge for us humans struggling miserably with the modes of passion and ignorance. When will humankind and even we ourselves serving in ISKCON ever catch up with what you have clearly said?

Yet man professes to belong to a particular type of faith with reference to a particular time and circumstance and thus claims to be a Hindu, Muslim, Christian, Buddhist, or an adherent of any other sect. Such designations are non-*sanātana-dharma*. A Hindu may change his faith to become a Muslim, or a Muslim may change his faith to become a Hindu, or a Christian may change his faith and so on. But in all circumstances the change of religious faith does not affect the eternal occupation of rendering service to others. The Hindu, Muslim, or Christian in all circumstances is a servant of someone. Thus, to profess a particular type of faith is not to profess one’s *sanātana-dharma*. The rendering of service is *sanātana-dharma*.

Factually we are related to the Supreme Lord in service. The Supreme Lord is the supreme enjoyer, and we living entities are His servitors. We are created for His enjoyment, and if we participate in that eternal enjoyment with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, we become happy. We cannot become happy otherwise. \[Introduction to *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*\]

Here in Congo, far away from England and a long time since I joined your movement, your clarion words still repeatedly ring crystal clear. They are leading us and all human folks everywhere to surrender to Kṛṣṇa as He is revealed in *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and all your books. You are the real preacher, the deliverer of Kṛṣṇa’s universal message in your books. As you said portentously and prophetically:

They are now feeling the weight of this movement. Formerly they thought these people come and go, but now they see we are staying. Now we have set fire. It will go on, it cannot be stopped. You can bring big, big fire brigades but the fire will act. The brainwash books are already there. Even if they stop externally, internally it will go on. Our first-class campaign is book distribution. Go house to house. The real fighting is now. Kṛṣṇa will give you all protection. So, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and fight. \[Letter to Tamāl Krishna, 30 October 1976\]

Your words have given my life its purpose and fulfillment. I see hope and happiness all around in preaching Lord Caitanya’s movement. By our carrying the science explained in your books to every town and village of the world, all our hearts can be satisfied: *viśvaṁ pūrṇa-sukhāyate*.

It is exactly as you wrote in your diary so wonderfully in 1965 when you were crossing the Atlantic on your way to the USA:

*tāhale ānanda hoy tabe hoy digvijay*  
*caitanyer kṛpā atiśay*  
*māyā duṣṭa jata duḥkhī jagate sabāi sukhī*  
*vaiṣṇaver icchā pūrṇa hoy*

**As the vast mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu conquers all directions, a flood of transcendental ecstasy will certainly cover the land. When all the sinful, miserable living entities become happy, the Vaiṣṇavas’ desire is then fulfilled.**

It is actually true that I am still sinful and miserable. But as you have kindly given this rascal a special chance to preach for you and fulfill your desire, I am now happily engaged. You are the Vaiṣṇava, not me. May I help to fulfill your desire so that you become happy with my puny efforts. That is my only hope.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day please give me your blessings to be intelligent and kind enough to help deliver the people here in Congo by following in your footsteps with your message of the *paramparā*.

Your humble servant,  
Mahāvishnu Swami

## Navayogendra Swami

My dear gurudeva Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have appeared in order to deliver fallen souls like me!

Dear Gurudeva, today is the most auspicious day, your glorious appearance day. Śrīla Prabhupāda, what would have happened to me without you? It was the special mercy of Lord Gaurāṅga and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa that They sent you to save fallen souls like me. Dear Gurudeva, my only prayer unto your lotus feet is that you please purify me with the shade of your lotus feet.

**Ohe! Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura**

(from *Śaraṇāgati*)

*ohe! vaiṣṇaba ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse koruṇā kori’*  
*diyā pāda-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*  
*chaya bega domi’, chaya doṣa śodhi’,*  
*chaya guṇa deho’ dāse*  
*chaya sat-saṅga, deho’ he āmāre,*  
*boshechi saṅgera āśe*  
*ekākīāmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*  
*kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro,*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to’ kāṅgala, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’,*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

Gurudeva, you used to say that our only business is to purify ourselves. Gurudeva, I can’t purify myself; my purification is possible only by your causeless mercy. My prayer unto your lotus feet is that you may kindly bless me that I can head toward purity and at the end of my life remember your lotus feet and those of Lord Gaurāṅga and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me so that I can become a good instrument in your great ISKCON. Your ISKCON is the only hope for the whole world. Kindly bless your ISKCON devotees so that they all follow your instructions very strictly.

Thank you very much.

Your humble servant,  
Navayogendra Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda often advised his followers to read his books. As he wrote to Satsvarūpa Dāsa Goswami in 1976:

Yes, as a Sannyāsi and GBC your first duty is to read my books. Otherwise how will you preach? In order to remain steadily fixed in Krishna consciousness there must be a sound philosophical understanding. Otherwise it will become only sentiment. Whenever you find time please read my books.

Below I would like to offer three suggestions for reading Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books more effectively.

**The Proper Mood**

Before reading or studying Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, we should ensure that our mood is appropriate. What is the proper mood? For instance, it is very conducive to cultivate awe and reverence, to be respectful toward the books and their subject matters. It is also very good to approach them with inquisitiveness, enthusiasm, confidence, patience, and a prayerful attitude.

**Imagination**

Lord Kṛṣṇa appears in this world in many forms. As Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in chapter 22 of *Kṛṣṇa* book:

It is recommended in the Vedic scriptures that a deity may be made from different kinds of material elements: it can be painted, made of metal, made of jewels, made of wood, earth, or stone or can be conceived within the heart of the worshiper.

Therefore, one method of reading with attention and absorption is to use your imagination — in accordance with the words of the scriptures — to envision and experience within your heart what is being described. Read and experience, for instance, the following description from chapter 35 of *Kṛṣṇa* book:

When Kṛṣṇa walked on the bank of the Yamunā, He was seen nicely decorated with *tilaka* on His face. He was garlanded with different kinds of forest flowers, and His body was smeared with the pulp of sandalwood and *tulasī* leaves. The bumblebees became mad after the fragrance and sweetness of the atmosphere. Being pleased by the humming sound of the bees, Kṛṣṇa would play His flute, and together the sounds became so sweet to hear that aquatic birds like cranes, swans, and ducks were charmed. Instead of swimming or flying, they became stunned. They closed their eyes and entered a trance of meditation in worship of Kṛṣṇa.

By reading and visualizing such passages, we can remember the form of Lord Kṛṣṇa by expanding the verse and seeing Him as Lord Brahmā describes Him in the *Brahmā-saṁhitā:* dressed in a yellow *dhotī,* with a peacock feather in His hair, with eyes like lotus petals, carrying a flute, and wearing a *vaijayantī* garland. By using our imagination we can see many beautiful forest flowers of different colors, smell their fragrances, and also hear the jubilant bees humming around them. And if we pay close attention, within our minds we can hear the enchanting sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute and marvel at the intensity of love displayed by the birds who are worshiping Him in their trance of ecstasy. Painting spiritual pictures within our minds will make our minds spiritual.

**Self-recitation**

Probably one of the most important factors in reading is to examine how much we are absorbing what is being said. One way of doing this is to recite what we have read. Read a paragraph, close the book, and try to recite out loud (or sub vocally) what you have read. Self-recitation will reveal the blanks in our remembrance and knowledge and at the same time help us organize and consolidate the information in our minds.

Do not be discouraged if you cannot remember so much. Simply go back and read the paragraph again with the same mood of enthusiasm, confidence, and patience mentioned above. Again close the book and recite the paragraph or passage once more. Repeat this exercise until you can remember, understand, and perhaps even assimilate or experience what has been said. Studies have shown that self-recitation is particularly effective in helping one absorb and assimilate what one is studying.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Puruṣatraya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I always feel your constant presence in my life, especially due to the kind of pressure that comes from the responsibility of helping to carry on your glorious mission. This pressure of responsibility is actually your special mercy on us, your followers.

If I didn’t have such pressure, I would fatally act on my own, guided by my bewildered and limited mind, thinking “This is my mission,”“I’m the controller,”“I can do what I want,”“This is my opinion,” etc. In fact, such ideas would inevitably lead me to complete disgrace. Not being attached to you and not following your instructions, I would fall prey to my own false ego, and all kinds of *anarthas* would take over my consciousness. In that situation I would be lost and hopeless.

I am eternally indebted to you for having connected me to the glorious *paramparā* that comes from Kṛṣṇa Himself, and for having imparted unto me the highest knowledge through your books and other media. Now I have the responsibility that comes with belonging to such an important spiritual lineage, which passes through Lord Brahmā; Vedavyāsa; Śrīpāda Madhvācārya; the *yuga-avatāra,* Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu; and such exalted representatives of His as the great *ācāryas* Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, Jīva Gosvāmī, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the one who accomplished the herculean task of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the whole world. By propagating the *saṅkīrtana* movement around the world, you have given the opportunity to many to be free from materialistic tendencies and to get the highest boon, which is to serve and worship Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa with pure devotion. O Prabhupāda, somehow or other I got at least a fragment of this causeless mercy from you.

Once you said that to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness one must accept obstacles — not flee from them but go after them. What we should not do is search for material facilities and always try to get a comfortable and leisurely life, thus assuring that we will be distracted with lots of entertainments. Rather, you want us to work hard continuously. In fact, by conquering each obstacle we gradually approach the ultimate goal of life.

O Prabhupāda, please keep me always engaged in your mission. Bless me by giving me more and more service.

Puruṣatraya Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are my real shelter!

Only three days back I had to undergo an unavoidable leg operation with full anaesthesia. While waiting for the anaesthesia to take effect, I prayed to you for protection of my consciousness. I had read in the *Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* that you had continued to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa under full anaesthesia when the doctors had treated you for a life-threatening heart attack during the early days of your preaching in America. I had also heard that you had continued to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa even under full narcosis.

After waking up with fourteen deep cuts in my leg, I took shelter in listening to your tapes.

One day I heard you pose a deep question:

“What do you do for eternity?”

At the time these words penetrated into my heart I was experiencing my darkest hours of pain and desperation. But suddenly I felt an unfamiliar strength enter into me. Although I was still in the same situation — a feverish hospital patient under emergency treatment — my life changed, and many sweet realizations in Kṛṣṇa consciousness came to me. Your tremendous spiritual strength saved me. It’s mystical, transcendental, and ultimately beyond my understanding.

Now I can say with full conviction:

The great wonder of your mercy can infuse spiritual life into the desert of my heart.

The great wonder of your mercy can connect me with Kṛṣṇa in love and devotion.

The great wonder of your mercy can obtain for me Kṛṣṇa’s sidelong and compassionate glance.

The great wonder of your mercy can carry me back to Godhead.

I will continue to try my best to show you that I wish to be your good servant. But you know me best — how unqualified I am. (This often makes me sad.)

Please know the wonder of your causeless mercy is my only hope, shelter, and solace.

May you be glorified by countless wonderful Vaiṣṇavas throughout the universe for the great wonder of your mercy!

Most merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Today I fall into the dust of your lotus feet with a feeling of deep gratitude and security.

Thank you so much.

Your humble servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Smita Krishna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is hard to write, it is a fight with all my might.  
Thank you for reminding me.  
Thank you for remembering me.  
May I always stay with thee.  
You are the true preacher.  
That is your feature.  
What will it be like in the future?  
What you started will prosper.  
A wonderful culture.  
May I be at your feet a creeper.  
Please look kindly upon me.

Your lowly servant,  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Ācārya* he was in every detail,  
A standard so high none could compare.  
How was it possible, one could ask?  
By pleasing Kṛṣṇa as his only task!  
Easy to say in a poem like this.  
To actually follow, easy to miss.  
Only by love is it conceivable.  
Not by mechanical means achievable.  
Yes! Undoubtedly love is the key.  
Even for one as fallen as me.  
But how can one change lust into love?  
For me, an impossible task, the above.  
Mercy, your mercy — no other way!  
Save me, a fallen — what else can I say!

Your lowly servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

**87 Million Billion Lives**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda has been telling me since the beginning that I am eternal, but only recently has it occurred to me that it might be true.

Of course, like everyone else, I sometimes wonder whether I will make it in this life, and if not, what will I be next time? A demigod? A rich, handsome movie star on earth? But now, another question has come to mind: If I were to stay in the material world, where would I be, who would I be, 87 million billion lives from now?

Of course, Śrīla Prabhupāda says that there is no point in trying for a better material birth. And in the *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa says that even the best places in the material world are places of suffering, with repeated birth and death.

But 87 million billion lives? And to think that it is only a flash in eternal time! Birth and death, birth and death — how long have I been going through it? How long will it continue? There is just no measure for it. How can you measure forever?

Will I become what I see in the news? A poor tribal woman in a war-torn country, walking miles across the desert to get help for my sick and hungry baby? Maybe I will be the baby. Or maybe a fuzzy little rabbit being cut and tortured in a laboratory. It is happening to others; it can happen to me.

I used to think this world was my friend, a fun place, but I was born in the darkest of ignorance and my spiritual master has now opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. It is only beginning to dawn on me what Śrīla Prabhupāda has saved me from.

I can see that I have never appreciated what Śrīla Prabhupāda has done for me. Even now, I can only understand a little crumb of it, for the horror of this world goes beyond the grasp of my tiny brain.

Will I make it home in this life? I hope so, but even if I have 87 million billion more lives to go, I will know that I am on the road back. I will see light at the end of the tunnel. And it is all by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

What’s more, if I follow Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can do for others what he has done for me. I can take others out of the material world and back home. As fallen as I am, I can save others. I just have to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda. How amazing!

All glories, all glories, all glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, 87 million billion times today and every day forevermore!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Today man is plagued with problems and troubled with fears of a magnitude and complexity that have never been seen before. While everyone unanimously agrees that there is some major imbalance causing these disturbances, debate rages on regarding the root of the problems.

In the past man turned to religion to resolve such problems. But as the age has progressed, many have found the resources of their religious institutions inadequate. When the very faiths that were originally harbingers of peace smolder with intercommoned and religious discord, where is a person to turn?

The bitter fruit of humanity’s misguided search for peace and happiness is the modern world. In the name of progress, man has resumed building the proverbial tower of Babel. Intoxicated by the imagined success of his technological and empiric mastery, he constantly invites the lightning bolt of his own transient achievements to bring down the very tower that stands for his vainglory.

Contemplating such affairs far in advance, Kṛṣṇa concluded, “The most serious malady requires the most potent remedy. Thus I will return to the earth, not bearing weapons or leading armies but bringing the holy name and accompanied by my pure devotees.”

During the luminous time of Lord Caitanya and His immediate associates, the sky of good fortune remained bright and clear. But as those *pāriṣada* returned one by one to Goloka, the dark clouds of the age, bearing ominous tidings of horrors yet to come, gathered again on the horizon, darkening the world’s prospects.

Still, humble, sincere seekers did not completely lose hope. They could feel in their hearts that the golden rays of the benediction moon rising in the East would cast her light into the Western world, causing the pure white lotus of auspiciousness to blossom in the midst of darkness. The merciful Lord had already set into motion a plan that would gradually unfold and reveal the first glimmer of the prophesized golden age.

Bringing the message of Godhead to Westerners was a challenge that even many merciful Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas considered impossible. The teachings of Lord Caitanya had first gone to the West in book form in 1896, the year of your appearance. For the message of Lord Caitanya to transform from just another of the many theories floating around the halls of academia to devotional realizations rooted in the heart required someone to inspire surrender and service. Such a person would have to be a master of both transcendental knowledge and the spiritual culture of loving relationships. It was in 1965 that you, Lord Caitanya’s ambassador, reached Boston harbor aboard the humble cargo ship *Jaladuta*. This “messenger from the waters” carried with her the essence of what the fluid and flowing metaphors of the highest spiritual literary works allude to – *rasa* — for the *Jaladuta* bore the book as well as the person *bhāgavata*.

Your humility, faith, and dedication were so pure that Kṛṣṇa personally and lovingly worked wonders through you. With unparalleled faith in the holy name, in the order of Śrī Guru, and in the universality of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you introduced the *mahā-mantra* with extraordinary effect.

The holy name is the panacea everyone is seeking, but no one would have understood that without a demonstration of its potency. To manifest the divine spiritual reality living within Śrī Nāma necessitates the personal dimension of a pure surrendered servant of the name.

You are that person. Śrīla Prabhupāda, in time you became our everything — guide, savior, guru, father, friend. You not only opened our eyes with the torchlight of knowledge; you further anointed them with the salve of love. And with the blessed eyes of devotion, we are able to behold what Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura had foretold — the pastimes of Lord Caitanya unfolding even today.

The treasure we inherited was not due to any qualifications of our own. You *created* our good fortune. In doing so you fulfilled and embellished the verse penned by Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī that glorifies Lord Caitanya for garlanding the entire earth with good fortune by weaving a wreath of *prema* out of the holy name.

It is customary to make offerings of flowers to signify the delicate assemblage of heartfelt emotions we pour out to the feet of our beloved Gurudeva on the day of his appearance. But no individual or collective Vyāsa-pūjā offering could compare with the gift of love you have bestowed upon us.

Your servant,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! On this occasion of celebrating your Vyāsa-pūjā, I pour out my prayers at your lotus feet so I can find security in their tender shade.

I know by your example that nothing can be accomplished without endeavors of sacrifice for the pleasure of Guru and Kṛṣṇa. So in those endeavors I am trying my best under the able guidance of some of my senior godbrothers. As Guru and Kṛṣṇa are supremely transcendental entities, I pray that my endeavors are honest and bona fide for their pleasure. I know that I should maintain brahminical qualities as I carry out these endeavors, so that the transcendence will be retained. This is the way to please the transcendental entities. This is explained by Pṛthu Mahārāja in his teachings in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed the way of sacrifice by personally taking every risk to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even at an advanced age and without any attachment, just to please Guru and Kṛṣṇa. You did it. You have created an everlasting impact in highly educated modern Western people and induced them to investigate the oldest wisdom. Even stubborn Māyāvādīs and impersonalists are so far refraining from envious criticism because they must have read your books. That they do not openly disagree means they must agree. No one is finding any fault with you; it is unheard of. Your teachings, your dealings, your plans for future success of the institution — all are glorified in the *śāstra*.

Therefore the conclusion is that any pinpricking that may be felt is due to our own deficiencies in rendering our services to your institution. There is no other explanation. One thing is sure: maintaining and developing ISKCON is our collective responsibility.

How may I serve you? I get only one answer, and that is to render service according to the advice of my senior godbrothers. I hope I am not incorrect, and I will try my best.

Your servant,  
Vedavyāsa priya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this glorious day of your appearance. Over five thousand years ago, on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra, Arjuna inquired thus (*Bhagavad-gītā* 8.1–2):

*arjuna uvāca*  
*kiṁ tad brahma kim adhyātmaṁ kiṁ karma puruṣottama*  
*adhibhūtaṁ ca kiṁ proktam adhidaivaṁ kim ucyate*

**Arjuna inquired: O my Lord, O Supreme Person, what is Brahman? What is the self? What are fruitive activities? What is this material manifestation? And what are the demigods? Please explain this to me.**

*adhiyajñaḥ kathaṁ ko ’tra dehe ’smin madhusūdana*  
*prayāṇa-kāle ca kathaṁ jñeyo ’si niyatātmabhiḥ*

**Who is the Lord of sacrifice, and how does He live in the body, O Madhusūdana? And how can those engaged in devotional service know You at the time of death?**

Over five thousand years later you came, delivering Kṛṣṇa’s answers for the benefit of all mankind.

The words of the Lord and the transcendental Bhaktivedanta purports are ours to take as a gift from you. We feel that we may never be able to fully appreciate this gift or make a scratch on the debt of gratitude and service owed to Your Divine Grace for this unconditional gift.

On this day, every one of us, your servants at Bhaktivedanta Āśrama, fall at your feet and pray to be able to assist, and not get in the way of, this most valuable preaching mission of yours.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Āśrama, Adelaide, Australia.

## Agartala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

From the moment I read your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* my life changed. I joined ISKCON twenty-five years ago, and my love for You Divine Grace is increasing day by day. My position in ISKCON is the temple president of a small temple in Agartala, Tripura, India. From doing my devotional service at this small temple, I can understand more clearly how powerful you are. If I have so much difficulty dealing with the many problems that arise from managing this small temple, how enormous must have been the power you possessed to manage so many temples throughout the world? I can clearly understand that only a pure devotee empowered by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself could have done that.

You are the perfect example of determination, steadiness, mercy, compassion, perfect knowledge, and love. I thank you for the strength I have received and am receiving from you to overcome all the difficulties in my service. Please give me the courage to continue performing my duties, by which I try to satisfy you. Please always help me and protect all devotees from the danger posed by powerful Māyā Devī. I offer unlimited obeisances to your lotus feet.

Your servants in Agartala, Tripura, India.  
(written by Premadatta Dāsa)

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

This year I want to simply read from your own purports the qualities of a pure devotee spiritual master. The following is the perfect description of the whole process of guru-disciple relationship.

Lord Kṛṣṇa speaks to Uddhava in the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.**

\[Purport:\] This verse appears in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 1.46). His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda has commented on this verse as follows.

“This is a verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.17.27) spoken by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa when He was questioned by Uddhava regarding the four social and spiritual orders of society. The Lord was specifically instructing how a *brahmacārī* should behave under the care of a spiritual master. A spiritual master is not an enjoyer of facilities offered by his disciples. He is like a parent. Without the attentive service of his parents, a child cannot grow to manhood; similarly, without the care of the spiritual master one cannot rise to the plane of transcendental service.

“The spiritual master is also called *ācārya,* or a transcendental professor of spiritual science. The *Manu-saṁhitā* (2.140) explains the duties of an *ācārya,* describing that a bona fide spiritual master accepts charge of disciples, teaches them the Vedic knowledge with all its intricacies, and gives them their second birth. The ceremony performed to initiate a disciple into the study of spiritual science is called *upanīti,* or the function that brings one nearer to the spiritual master. One who cannot be brought nearer to a spiritual master cannot have a sacred thread, and thus he is indicated to be a *śūdra.* The sacred thread worn on the body of a *brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya,* or *vaiśya* is a symbol of initiation by the spiritual master; it is worth nothing if worn merely to boast of high parentage. The duty of the spiritual master is to initiate a disciple with the sacred thread ceremony, and after this *saṁskāra,* or purificatory process, the spiritual master actually begins to teach the disciple about the *Vedas.* A person born a *śūdra* is not barred from such spiritual initiation, provided he is approved by the spiritual master, who is duly authorized to award a disciple the right to be a *brāhmaṇa* if he finds him perfectly qualified. In the *Vāyu Purāṇa* an *ācārya* is defined as one who knows the import of all the Vedic literatures, abides by their rules and regulations, and teaches his disciples to act in the same way.

“Only out of His immense compassion does the Personality of Godhead reveal Himself as the spiritual master. Therefore in the dealings of an *ācārya* there are no activities but those of transcendental loving service to the Lord. He is the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. It is worthwhile to take shelter of such a steady devotee, who is called *āśraya-vigraha,* or the manifestation or form of the Lord of whom one must take shelter.

“If one poses himself as an *ācārya* but does not have an attitude of servitorship to the Lord, he must be considered an offender, and this offensive attitude disqualifies him from being an *ācārya.* The bona fide spiritual master always engages in unalloyed devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By this test he is known to be a direct manifestation of the Lord and a genuine representative of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu. Such a spiritual master is known as *ācāryadeva.* Influenced by an envious temperament and dissatisfied because of an attitude of sense gratification, mundaners criticize a real *ācārya.* In fact, however, a bona fide *ācārya* is nondifferent from the Personality of Godhead, and therefore to envy such an *ācārya* is to envy the Personality of Godhead Himself. This will produce an effect subversive of transcendental realization.

“As mentioned previously, a disciple should always respect the spiritual master as a manifestation of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, but at the same time one should always remember that a spiritual master is never authorized to imitate the transcendental pastimes of the Lord. False spiritual masters pose themselves as identical with Śrī Kṛṣṇa in every respect, to exploit the sentiments of their disciples, but such impersonalists can only mislead their disciples, for their ultimate aim is to become one with the Lord. This is against the principles of the devotional cult.

“The real Vedic philosophy is *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva,* which establishes everything to be simultaneously one with and different from the Personality of Godhead. Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī confirms that this is the real position of a bona fide spiritual master and says that one should always think of the spiritual master in terms of his intimate relationship with Mukunda (Śrī Kṛṣṇa). Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, in his *Bhakti-sandarbha* (213), has clearly explained that a pure devotee’s observation of the spiritual master and Lord Śiva as being one with the Personality of Godhead exists in terms of their being very dear to the Lord, not identical with Him in all respects. Following in the footsteps of Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī and Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, later *ācāryas* like Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura have confirmed the same truths.
