Vedavyāsa knew that the sleeping souls, upon hearing from these literary works, would remember their relationship with Kṛṣṇa and want to go back to Him. In fact, all the great *ācāryas* have a sense of urgency for distributing spiritual knowledge, and as the *vraja-gopīs* confirm in their prayers (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.31.9), anyone who takes up this task is “the most munificent welfare worker.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have instilled in us this same imperative for distributing spiritual knowledge, repeatedly instructing us that your books — passed down from Vedavyāsa himself — are so spiritually powerful that simply by touching them, keeping them in their homes, or reading one word or one line from them, people would be turned toward Kṛṣṇa.

Your books are unprecedented in quality, and still the presses are running, printing mass quantities of your richly illustrated, meticulously edited books in dozens of languages. Still people receive them with gratitude, and upon reading them they dedicate their lives to you and your mission. It’s true: you live in your books, and those who read them live with you.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, the members of ISKCON of Silicon Valley, worship you as the true representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. We aspire to stay close to you by hearing daily from your books, the most wonderful manifestations of your mercy. And rather than merely offering fruits, flowers, and incense to you on this day of your appearance, we additionally offer you the ever-increasing results of our work in distributing your books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika Dāsa)

## Slovenia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Year after year we are baffled in our attempt to realize the magnitude of the mercy you have shown us. In our preaching activities we can observe how people eagerly accept your books and how people’s hearts are changing. There are so many groups who attempt to create a better world and promise spiritual reality, but we can observe in the followers of such groups that there is no great change in their level of purity. In their hearts remain lust, anger, greed, hatred, and other impurities, headed by envy of the Lord and the propensity to lord it over material nature. Nothing can compare to Lord Caitanya’s movement in its power to actually purify people of all these impurities and cause a change of heart. It is amazing how Your Divine Grace transplanted this very different culture into the modern Western civilization.

In 1885 Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura wrote in his *Sajjana-toṣaṇī:*

Lord Caitanya did not advent Himself to liberate only a few men in India. Rather, His main objective was to emancipate all living entities of all countries throughout the entire universe and preach the Eternal Religion. . . . There is no doubt that this unquestionable order will come to pass. . . . Very soon the unparalleled path of *hari-nāma-saṅkīrtana* will be propagated all over the world. . . . Oh, for that day when the fortunate English, French, Russian, German, and American people will take up banners, *mṛdaṅgas,* and *karatālas* and raise *kīrtana* through their streets and towns! When will that day come?

The Ṭhākura must have amazed his Indian readers with such a statement. His son, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, with the aid of his disciples, presented the teachings of Lord Caitanya throughout India through his Gauḍīya Maṭha, and he made some forays into the West. Later his disciples were doubtful whether it would be possible for Western people to accept Vaiṣṇavism.

Only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had the staunch faith in the words of your guru and the previous *ācāryas* to risk everything and try to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We are trying our best to push on your mission and present Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism in Slovenia. There are certainly challenges from without and within, but we are praying to have unflinching faith in your vision and to become empowered by Your Divine Grace to accomplish acts that are possible only by Your Divine Grace’s mercy.

Your servants in Slovenia.

## Solapur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you! All glories to His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda!!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came . . . you saw . . . and you won over millions of hearts by cleaning them. You showed us Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the king of everyone’s heart, coming straight from your heart. Yet we are such blind and foolish creatures that we do not appreciate Lord Kṛṣṇa. The practical proof of this is that we still have unlimited material desires in our hearts and we do not fully accept your teachings and your compassionate efforts to deliver us.

To us who have been shelterless for unlimited lives, you have given us a nice house to live in, ISKCON. Yet instead of living peacefully in that house, which is supported by your teachings and decorated with your divine qualities and your vision for benefiting the entire human race with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we allow ourselves to be barred from that house by our speculations, our plans for sense gratification, and our impious existence. Many times you correctly described those who reject Kṛṣṇa as dogs and hogs. While we pretend to be devotees, our acts say otherwise — that we are stealing your wealth. But you have accumulated so much divine wealth that we rats, though millions in number, can never spoil even a small fraction of it. Actually, our position is to beg from you, but we are such rascals that we are stealing the things in your house. We are so much indebted to you that we should beg everything from you, even breathing. But we are beggars in the dog’s category. Still, out of your mercy you continually try to engage us in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service.

In spite of all our disqualifications, your presence in our lives is so auspicious that we are confident that one day we will become truly devoted to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Of this there is no doubt. When will that day come when your words, your work, your mercy, and your love will make us cry? When will our stonelike hearts melt? We do not have any pious acts to our credit, but your causeless mercy alone can bring us to life. Then we will live for you, breathe for you, work for you, dance for you, eat for you, cry for you, and die for you. We are looking forward to that day when our hearts will become clean by our receiving your causeless mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our father, mother, relative, friend, well-wisher, and master. It is only because of you that we know anything of Kṛṣṇa, who is our all-in-all. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your helping hand is so big and we are such tiny misers that we cannot catch hold of your helping lotus hand. This is our pitiful condition. Please have mercy upon us and kindly place your lotus feet on our tiny heads. Thus we will begin to be able to understand your position, your compassion, and your glories.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaur-Nitāi! All glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants in Solapur, India.  
(written by Shursendas)

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you, the *senā-pati* of Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu!

When you lived in Vṛndāvana, preparing to go to the West to fulfill the mission you had received from your spiritual master, you chose to stay at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, in a room close to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmīs *samādhi*. It was surely not by chance that this became your *āśrama* at that time. In *The Nectar of Instruction* you make the point that the members of ISKCON are *rūpānugas,* living a spiritual life based on the principal teachings of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. But what is our qualification to be *rūpānugas*? We can be *rūpānugas* only as far as we are your followers, Prabhupādānugas. For you are the perfect *rūpānuga*. Before you left for the West, you received the direct blessings of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple compound to establish Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* mission all over the world.

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī was directly empowered by Lord Caitanya to establish the mission to fulfill His desire within this material world. The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* explains that this desire is that everyone get love of Godhead. As Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī mainly established Lord Caitanya’s mission by providing a profound philosophical and theological foundation for it by writing books, so you, as the empowered *rūpānuga* in modern times, wrote your books carefully on the basis of Rūpa Gosvāmī’s teachings. But not only did you endeavor to write your books; you also struggled hard to establish your International Society for Krishna Consciousness — ISKCON — which you specifically designed to give everybody an opportunity to live the life of a *rūpānuga-bhakta* by progressing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness according to the principles established by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in *The Nectar of Instruction* and *The Nectar of Devotion*.

Several times you emphasized that your only credit is that you cent-percent followed the instruction of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. As you in this way very carefully delivered the spiritual inheritance from your spiritual master to the whole world, you simultaneously handed down the spiritual inheritance from the whole *paramparā*. By doing this in such a grand way, you insured that your success as a *rūpānuga* establishing Lord Caitanya’s mission within this world would be unparalleled. But in addition to that, you also ingeniously arranged for that mission to go on for hundreds and thousands of years by providing the ’’BBT’ and ISKCON with clear directions for how these institutions should carry on Lord Caitanya’s mission in your physical absence. Therefore, during your presence here you expressed great concern that the ’’BBT’ and ISKCON should be protected and maintained nicely by the cooperative effort of your sincere followers.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us and the whole world your books and a society of pure devotees. Thank you for giving us fallen souls in Kali-yuga a place in your sublime mission of assisting the previous *ācāryas,* headed by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, to fulfill the innermost desires of Lord Caitanya. And most of all, thank you for still being present with us to protect us as long as we try to follow your instructions despite so many discrepancies.

Your servants at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Center in Stockholm, Sweden.

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**We offer our respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are so kindly teaching the message of Lord Caitanya and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with voidism and impersonalism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

There is a popular slogan produced by philosophers that says, “The only thing constant is change.” We note also that your devoted follower George Harrison wrote a song many years ago that said, “All things must pass.” However, it is the experience of most, if not all, of your servants in the Sydney *yātrā* that Your Divine Grace has provided an enduring quality to our lives that has not diminished with the passing of time. When philosophers and poets speak of temporality and constant change, we understand they are referring to the state of affairs in this material world. In contrast with this, as soon as we achieved the blessing of your divine association, we understood that you have come directly from the spiritual realm to deliver us into the eternal reality of our true nature and purpose in life.

Before meeting you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and imbibing from you the inclination to hand over the care of our lives and our strongly independent wills to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we found no purpose or direction that could bring us permanent satisfaction. We found it difficult to trust and love others in a fully devoted way because the lives of everyone in this material world, including our own, are characterized by selfishness and dominated by lust, anger, and greed. We may have thought, under the powerful illusion of the material energy, that our desires for name and fame and for wealth and security in the vast ocean of material existence would be sufficient to bring us happiness. Instead, we found that we were building our castles upon sand. We found that there was no permanent foundation in our lives until we met Your Divine Grace.

What a wonderful boon your association has been! By connecting us with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you have purified relationships that were previously fraught with difficulty and anxiety. We stood by powerless as the sad, empty tale of birth, death, disease, and old age played out its sorry melodrama within the lives of the heroes and villains of this world, and within the lives of our own family members and friends. We often responded with joy at the temporary happiness of others, and with sadness at their reverses, but more often than not we struggled to survive and maintain our own equilibrium in the quicksand of Māyā. We watched helpless as inexorable time swallowed all around us, but you provided a beacon in the night. You are the captain of a ship that has come to save us from drowning in this fearful material ocean. Even more than that, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given the opportunity to go back to Godhead to those dear to us, merely by dint of our association with you. In his way, you have given meaning and substantiality where previously there was hopelessness. You have given love where there was mistrust and envy. You have shown us our real identity where previously we were blind to our eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa, having buried our heads in darkness.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we rededicate our lives to your mission to uplift human society, and we place our heads at your lotus feet. For we know that at your lotus feet the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness shines bright eternally. We know you will look upon us kindly and embrace our efforts, however clumsy or apparently insignificant they might be, to contribute to your great work of delivering all fallen souls back home, back to Godhead. In the same way that Kṛṣṇa is all-pervading, we sense that your mercy is ever-present and manifest in every opportunity this life provides for us. With complete conviction and unwavering dedication, we surrender at your lotus feet and beg for continued enthusiasm to carry on your preaching. We confirm today that we have no other business than to make your desire to benefit others by bringing them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness a reality. We can do this only by your divine grace. For this reason, we say together as one, “All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

With love from . . .

Your humble servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandira, Sydney, Australia.

## Taipei

Dear and respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your ISKCON! Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for offering us the opportunity to assist in your glorious mission. Whatever small contribution we are able to make, it is by all means due to your gentle mercy upon us.

While not all that long ago your followers in Taiwan comprised only a handful in this capital city of Taipei, now there are several small groups of your followers in various cities throughout the country. The devotees gather regularly, discuss your teachings, chant the *mahā-mantra*, and take *prasādam*.

The movement here is not booming as it did back in the sixties and seventies in Western countries — there are no grand achievements, no temples as such, no daily *saṅkīrtana* parties going out.

Still, many more people now have and read your books (and continue to get them), and thus they know about Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and you. Many more people have taken *prasādam* and heard and chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa, and more and more continue to do so. We’re out on the streets regularly, and in the media now and then. We sometimes take part in fairs and small festivals.

The times that we’re engaged in service to your mission, alone or together with our devotee friends, are the best times of our lives.

We bow down in gratitude. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your followers, also, who help us on the way. Thank you.

Your devotees at the Taipei City Foundation for Krishna Consciousness, Taipei, Taiwan.  
(written by Dayāl Nitāi Dāsa)

## Tallahassee

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the *Caitanya-maṅgala* it is stated that Nārada Muni, while visiting the spiritual world, came upon Lord Gaurāṅga. The Lord told him of His mission to destroy the sinful activities of the Age of Kali. “With the powerful chopper of *nāma-saṅkīrtana*,” He said, “I’ll cut the hard knots of demoniac desires from the hearts of everyone. Even if the sinners reject religion or flee to foreign countries, still they will get My mercy. I will send *mora senā-pati bhakta* to go there and deliver them.”*Mora senā-pati bhakta* thus refers to that devotee who will lead Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army, or in other words that devotee whom Lord Caitanya would empower to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. That commander of the *saṅkīrtana* army is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

If we look at the list of qualities possessed by a pure devotee, we can see that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have them all to a superlative degree. You have compassion for all living entities. You have unshakable faith in Kṛṣṇa and pure attachment to satisfying His desires. You are completely free from desires for name, fame, and glory. Not a single trace of lust, envy, or greed can be found in you. Your humility is a gem. Your determination is ironlike. Your patience is infinite. Your humor is sweet and sublime. Your kindness is all-pervading. Your enthusiasm is boundless. You are expert, silent, poetic, forgiving, and detached. You are a great spiritual master, an accomplished Vedic scholar, and a pure, confidential servant of Kṛṣṇa.

Daily I am trying to simply follow the path laid out by you, and I become inspired by the perfect example you have given us by your dedication to simply following the orders of your own spiritual master.

I beg you to allow me to become your first-class servant, a servant who understands your desires and acts to fulfill those desires, even without your asking him. I want this boon.

I know very little about devotional service, I have no attraction for the process of devotional service, and I have no good qualities, but I am convinced that by following and serving you and your mission all my shortcomings will be overlooked by the Supreme Lord and I will be allowed to serve you eternally.

Your servants at ISKCON Tallahassee, Florida, USA.  
(written by Ramīya Dāsa)

## Tijuana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance from the lowest candidates aspiring to be your servants.

First we want to offer you our gratitude for allowing us to be in contact with this sublime and ecstatic process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Because of your divine grace, this materialistic civilization has been illuminated with transcendental knowledge. We humbly request you to please keep pouring your mercy on the people of this dark Age of Kali.

We are unable to do any proper service for you, but by your causeless mercy you have given us the wonderful service of managing a preaching center in this northwestern corner of Mexico for the last ten years, beginning one year after your Centennial celebration. We know full well that our service to you is full of faults, but we are trying to improve it day by day, year after year.

The part we like the most is that we witness how people are taking your divine teachings to heart, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and practicing the precepts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We see how their lives and ours are being cleansed and illuminated by the words emanating from your lotus mouth. By your grace, the message of Lord Caitanya is being spread in this part of Mexico, which has supposedly been “forgotten by God,” full as it is with crime, violence, ignorance, and the like. Fortunately, you have given us various ways to counteract these effects of Kali-yuga, chiefly the chanting of the holy names and the distribution of your wonderful books and *prasādam*. We employ these effective counteracting forces especially in the annual *Festival de la India Tijuana,* giving so many people the chance to be engaged in devotional service.

Your unworthy servants in Tijuana, Mexico.  
(written by Mahānanda Nitāi Dāsa)

## Togo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā is an opportunity for us to glorify Your Divine Grace, and also to report how the revolution you started is continuing to create tidal waves in all the nations of the world.

Before reporting the news of our latest adventure in your spiritual movement, I want to first thank you for your able representatives, without whose sacrifice and dedication to your service we would not have this opportunity to be on this transcendental train going back home, back to Godhead. All your disciples who have served your mission, and who are still serving, are like waves in the ocean of mercy that is cascading down from the spiritual sky. By their grace, the treasure of love of God you carried in your heart to distribute in the West has doubled and is being distributed all over this planet.

When you were on the planet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you traveled around the globe several times. In your missionary voyages you even visited both East and South Africa. However, North, West and Central Africa did not receive your holy steps on their lands. Just as Lord Caitanya traveled the length and breadth of India preaching the *yuga-dharma* but left the rest of the world for you, his *senā-pati,* to conquer, in the same way you have left these other parts of Africa for your trusted disciples, to complete your conquest of the world.

Indeed, the frontiers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are expanding daily. Just last year another center was opened in the Republic of Benin, a Francophone country in West Africa. There are pending invitations from Burkina-Faso and Mali, also French-speaking countries.

Here in Togo, where a temple was opened barely four years ago, Hare Kṛṣṇa has already become a household word. By the strong compassion of your glorious son Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, who instructed us, his disciples, that our temples and preaching centers should not be just places of religious ceremonies but places where people can have immediate relief from all sorts of material miseries, thousands of people are benefiting from the flood of *prasādam,* book distribution, and the holy name. Devotees go out daily on the streets, and also onto the university campus, to distribute *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and your books. In Lome and its environs it is common to see hundreds of people in a long queue behind the Hare Krishna Food for Life bus, waiting for *prasādam*.

You have given us three powerful weapons by which the atheistic world can be brought to submission. *Prasādam,* book distribution, and the holy name. And by your mercy we are deploying these weapons everywhere.

I would like to take this opportunity to pray for your guidance so that when we enter other phases of this transcendental inundation, we shall also be equally equipped as real spiritual warriors, not lacking in the qualities of humility, fearlessness, truthfulness, compassion, pridelessness, patience, selflessness, perseverance, firm faith, and surrender to the divine will.

We also pray that you protect us from the most devastating enemy of progress on the path of *bhakti* — blasphemy of saintly persons who are preaching the glories of the holy name. This terrible offense is worse than being infected with AIDS. It at once destroys all our immunity against Māyā, and the once-blissful path of *bhakti* immediately becomes the path of gloom and doom.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to provide for the well-being of your ISKCON society. We, the disciples of your disciples, want to see all your visions and those of your dedicated servitors fulfilled in our lifetime. We want to see Kṛṣṇa conscious villages, self-sufficient Kṛṣṇa conscious farm communities, devotee judges wearing *tilaka* to court, and Kṛṣṇa conscious leaders ruling the world. You have laid the foundation for all these manifestations of the Lord’s mission, and we believe these and many more of your grand goals are not far-fetched as long as we remain in the framework of the devotional principles you laid down for us.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your granddisciples in Togo.  
(written by Varāha Dāsa)

## Tokyo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once wrote to a disciple in New York that when you went out to chant your intention was not to attract a crowd. Rather, your purpose was to glorify the Supreme Lord. You said that if our chanting is pure, the Lord will send sincere souls in our direction.

I never thought about the audience. I was prepared to chant if there were no men to hear me. The principle of chanting is to glorify the Lord and not to attract a crowd. If Krishna hears nicely then he will ask some sincere devotee to gather in such place. Therefore, be advised that thousands of centers may be started if we find out a sincere soul for each and every center. We do not require more men to start. \[Letter to Brahmānanda, 11 November 1967\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your words are always instructive and backed by your personal example. With your little band of original followers you joyfully went out on *harināma* in New York’s Washington Square Park and Tompkins Square Park. As you sang out the holy names of the Lord, the benedictions of the holy name were showered upon the listeners, the most fortunate of the unfortunate. You taught us how to give association to diseased souls without becoming infected.

New York City is sometimes bitter cold; people there can sometimes be impersonal and frighteningly aggressive. In the beginning no one came forward to assist you, yet you were undaunted and remained faithful to Śrī Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

With full enthusiasm, confidence, and tolerance you followed in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas,* such as Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and Lord Nityānanda. You never asked us to do anything you were not prepared to do yourself.

On the path back to Godhead we sometimes encounter dark tunnels of doubt and confusion. Your personal example of fidelity and devotion to your spiritual master awakens our trust and guides us forward.

Inspired by your unmotivated glorification of Lord Hari, from every corner of the globe Kṛṣṇa continues to send generations of beelike souls who eagerly lap up the nectarean words in your books, which overflow with hope and mercy.

We pray to always be surrounded by your beelike followers.

Your servants at New Gaya dhāma, Tokyo, Japan.

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When I first joined your society  
I knew little of sobriety,  
A life full of impiety,  
Always looking for variety.  
Your devotees taught me well,  
Saving my soul from most certain hell,  
Picking me up when I fell;  
Material desires I learned to quell.  
Now thirty-three years to the day,  
Still trying to somehow repay,  
Always looking for that novel way  
To surrender more to you each day.  
Let me vow to focus my heart,  
To take His name each day at the start,  
Never allowing offenses to keep me apart;  
Finding balance is truly an art.  
Let me go forward from darkness to light,  
From falsehood to truth, from wrong to right,  
With you in the center, never losing sight,  
Keeping Māyā at bay, alert to the fight.  
It’s selfless service I must attain;  
The preaching spirit must never wane;  
Love for Kṛṣṇa is my aim,  
That lost connection to reclaim.  
All your instructions let me embrace;  
Go to the end, not letting up on the pace.  
Allow me not to fade from grace;  
Upon your lotus feet my head I place.

Your servants in Tucson, Arizona, USA.  
(written by Sandāminī Devī Dāsī)

## Udaipur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

On this most auspicious anniversary of your appearance on this earth, all over the world the devotees are very intensely remembering you. How merciful Kṛṣṇa is that He sent His dear pure devotee here to deliver the fallen souls and bring them back to Him!

A very important verse in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.13.40) describes your glorious qualities:

*karṇadhāra ivāpāre bhagavān pāra-darśakaḥ*  
*athābabhāṣe bhagavān nārado muni-sattamaḥ*

**You are like a captain of a ship in a great ocean and you can direct us to our destination. Thus addressed, the godly personality, Devarṣi Nārada, greatest of the philosopher devotees, began to speak.**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest philosopher and pure devotee. Through your books and recorded lectures, you are speaking about Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We are so fortunate to hear your lectures every day and feel very much indebted to your for your wisdom. You have delivered to the world the most sacred scripture, the *amala purāṇa, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. I’m so indebted to you for this great gift, which brings eternal benefit for the soul. You desire very much that all your followers know the art of distributing your books. From my heart I pray that you please bless me and give me the strength to preach and distribute *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Kṛṣṇa is so kind; due to His affection and compassion for the fallen souls suffering in this Kali-yuga, He sent you among us. What you have done will forever remain in the hearts of your devotees. Thank you very much for all you have given us. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

Please bless all the devotees here in the state of Tripura.

Your servants in Udaipur, India.  
(written by Durgama Dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

I would like to tell you my heart, but it is difficult to find suitable language to address you.

Recently, while helping with the production of the new extralarge book containing some of your most significant photos, I realized how much I don’t know you. The only thing I know is that you always guided me and are guiding me in the right direction and that I cannot do without your guidance. Nothing can give me any satisfaction unless I feel it to be part of your guidance. Even now, while writing these few lines commemorating your all-auspicious appearance in this world, I am unable to continue till I am able to feel your loving guidance directing me. I have no confidence in my own capabilities. I know very well that I can on my own accord do whatever I like, without consciously depending on you, and I confess that I do so quite often with the excuse of necessity. But after I’ve done so, I often realize that your guiding hand had not relinquished its hold on me, even when I was quite unconscious of its support. In fact, I am never wholly unconscious of your support. Still, I feel a special satisfaction in consciously imploring your help. I feel helpless when I do not do so.

In offering this homage, I pray to you for support because in that consists the whole of my originality. In anything I do or say or think, I always find that I can excel my utmost expectations of success with such help. Even those who do not accept this method for themselves admit its wonderful efficacy in my case.

In any activity, be it an homage of word, an offering of action, or a simple meditation, the one thing most necessary for me is your acceptance. Whatever pleases you has the natural quality of pleasing me in the only real satisfactory way.

Once you said that you have created our fortune. Actually, to you entirely belongs the credit of making me know something without even seeking it. How could I seek that of which I am fully ignorant? If it were not for your causeless mercy, which forced me to open my eyes, I would still be in the well of blissful ignorance, merged in egoistic views, careless of anything else but myself and my egoistic satisfactions.

The only necessary thing for me now is to know you as you really are. But that is probably impossible. It is only necessary to know one thing of you: that you are our only friend and well-wisher. Anything short of this is not to know you at all. You are our ever well-wisher and friend, and those who tell me that I have other friends beside you are my real enemies. By this test I have found a considerable number of real friends after I met you. They are the only other friends, besides you, that I have. I wonder why there should be so many persons who do not know you. I myself once happened to be unaware of your very existence. That is one kind of ignorance. But there is another kind. There are persons who claim to know you but do not recognize you as their only real friend and well-wisher. That is also partly your desire, because I know that you can make them know you truly, if you like. It is impossible to know your ways fully. But those who know you are well aware that you are the only real friend of everybody. Those who don’t know you are, however, the only persons who are fully miserable. Now I have realized this fact by your causeless mercy. Those who have known you only once cannot misunderstand you completely. It is only to them that it is worthwhile to describe you as you really are. They are never satiated by hearing about you from the lips of those who really know you, and those who know you are never tired of speaking about you to please those who know you. But those who don’t know you find the talks of your devotees unintelligible.

I have heard of Kṛṣṇa from your lips. Therefore I believe in Him. Since the day I met you, my life has become very simple. I don’t have to worry anymore about understanding the goal of life, the meaning of existence, and my self-identity, because you have given me this knowledge in a very clear and unmistakable way. You are to me the only sure standard of all real knowledge. If I am to find the Supreme Personality of Godhead, I have to find Him in you. But I am not so anxious to find the Lord, as I have very little knowledge of Him. I don’t know if I may or may not have the good fortune to ever be able to really know Him; however, I wish to know Him through you only in order to please you.

You are the link between me and your other servants. It is you who made me know them as they really are. They are dear to me because they enable me, by each of their acts, to know you more and more intimately. But only when you, pleased with my service rendered in a spirit of surrender, will introduce me to the Supreme Person — only then will He enable me to know you fully and permanently. This supremely munificent quality of yours is more than human. You are the best beloved of the Lord, the most dear to Him, and in this relationship with Him consists your divinity. Of course, it is possible for an ordinary soul like myself to attain to your service by your grace and also recognize it as identical with service to the Lord Himself, but for us it is not possible to understand the nature of your relationship with the Supreme Lord as the object of your worship, even if we would be allowed, in due course of time, by your inconceivable mercy, to assist you, in a subordinate way, in your personal service to the Supreme Couple. That is your very special mercy.

Going through hundreds and hundreds of images of Your Divine Grace, I could see so many different kinds of expressions on your lotus face, but in each one of them is evident the power that emanates from your personality: it is the power of the *ācārya,* who wields the power of Kṛṣṇa Himself. The *ācārya’s* words are those of Kṛṣṇa Himself. If they are not intelligible, it is because those who listen have not obtained the mercy of the *ācārya*. It is as simple as that. You always warned us not to approach the Absolute in a challenging spirit. One who approaches you in a genuinely submissive mood is able to gradually understand you. Kṛṣṇa does whatever you ask Him to do, but Kṛṣṇa never forgives one who does not give up the challenging spirit and submit fully to you.

Your guidance is therefore absolutely necessary for every one of us, and nothing will save us from being punished with ignorance and forgetfulness if we withhold our full submission from you. At this point we have no choice. You promised to give everyone who follows your teachings an eternal life of eternal loving service to Kṛṣṇa. Your purpose was not to bestow on mankind merely the barren gift of a plausible system that leaves us comfortably where we are.

All of us came to you to learn the truth, and you declared that you were able to give us the truth. This is the only function of the *ācārya*. Why, therefore, should we aspire for something less than that?

You are not an ordinary mortal being like us, despite the resemblance. Those who deal with you as an ordinary man are barred by the Lord’s deluding energy from having any real knowledge of you. Many of us came to you to learn about the truth, and for that purpose we followed the proper method of inquiry. But if in due course of time, driven by impatience, lack of determination, or some other weakness, we adopt a different goal, why should the illusory energy, whose only purpose is to keep at a safe distance from Kṛṣṇa those who don’t have real love for Him, not prevent us from understanding your real nature? Therefore, my dearmost master, by your mercy may my determination be strong, may my patience be durable, may my weakness be cast away to give space to a stronger faith, may my desires be directed toward you and you only, and may my life air be kept in this body only for serving you, because you are my real and only well-wisher and friend and forever my only resting place.

Please forgive my impudence and kindly be pleased to open my eyes, sealed with the impenetrable gloom of ignorance, with one drop of the collyrium of your knowledge.

Your faithful servants at Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa and Govinda’s in Milan, Italy.  
(written by Āli Kṛṣṇa Devī Dāsī)

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

What an amazing, fascinating, intriguing life Śrīla Prabhupāda offered us! A life of eternity, complete cognizance, and bliss, opposed to the temporary, miserable life full of illusions we all lived prior to contacting him.

He gave us tools to cut through the labyrinth of ignorance spun by the complex web of materialism, and revealed the mysterious path leading to Goloka Vṛndāvana — a path not well known in this world.

As Lord Kṛṣṇa’s dear friend and representative, he established the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir in Ramaṇa Reti, Vṛndāvana, where hundreds of thousands of devotees from all over the planet flock to tread together along this pathway to the spiritual world. As we traverse along this conduit to the pastures of Vraja, pieces of the Lord’s divine puzzle gradually fall into place with sublime symmetry unfolding in each of our lives.

An expert gardener cultivating crops of *vraja-bhakti-rasa,* Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us instructions that act like nourishing rain on those thousands of *bhakti-latās,* each one growing according to its own individual relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Today we are honoring the 111thanniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance. Each time we gather for this celebration, the plants have grown a bit stronger; some are even blossoming buds of ecstatic love.

As Lord Nārāyaṇa pulled an elephant by its trunk through the eye of a needle, Śrīla Prabhupāda is pulling conditioned souls from the dark, miserable material world and leading them to the topmost planet in the spiritual sky — a seemingly impossible task. One of his most important instruments in this mystic formula is the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira in Ramaṇa-reti, Vṛndāvana.

Nondifferent from the Lord’s abode in Goloka, this *bhauma-vṛndāvana* manifests to entice conditioned souls back to their original home. Just see the beautiful smiles of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara!

Who hasn’t felt the unique sense of coming home, of finally finding suitable shelter, as one enters the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira? This temple is a direct manifestation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s genius, and he continues to guide and instruct us even after his physical disappearance from this world.

Of course, there are skeptics, like the doubting *brāhmaṇa* in the elephant-through-the-eye-of-a-needle story, who can’t believe Prabhupāda can perform the miracle of taking his followers to Goloka Vṛndāvana. Such doubters feel they must seek elsewhere for guidance along this miraculous path. But servants of the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira are rather like the cobbler, who danced with awe and jubilation at the wondrous powers of his Lord.

What an amazing, fascinating, intriguing life Śrīla Prabhupāda offered us! A life of eternity, complete cognizance, and bliss, opposed to the temporary, miserable life full of illusions we all lived prior to contacting him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Dhruva Mahārāja Dāsa)

## Wiesbaden

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances! All glories to your divine lotus feet! As we chant, we ask for it: “200% engagement in your service: keeps Māyā away!” You once said: “Do not wonder about who goes away. Wonder about who stays!”

Oh Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came into our lives here in the West. Your efforts are successful: you are taking over everyone’s heart. You are our master, our friend, and our shelter.

In these days of increasing collective confusion, we need you more than ever. We take shelter at your lotus feet by reading your books and listening to your lectures.

By your appearance on this planet you have made Kali-yuga so glorious that even the demigods want to be born in this age. O spiritual grandfather, I consider myself most fortunate to have received such an invaluable treasure.

There was a time that I walked in the darkest forest of ignorance, but one day Lord Kṛṣṇa came to me in the form of your *Bhagavad-gītā*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you translated those words so perfectly that I hope they will remain engraved in my heart and soul forever. May the rising sun of those words illuminate all such dark forests for all ages to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a tool in Kṛṣṇa’s hands. You remind us of our eternal relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You show us the way to develop love for Him. You engage everybody in devotional service for all time. Thank you very much for this matchless gift.

Without one’s being fixed in the transcendental loving service of the Lord, it is not possible to understand Him. Only by your mercy will we be able to execute devotional service. Although we are not qualified, we beg you to accept us as your eternal servants at the dust of your lotus feet.

Your humble and fallen servants in the ISKCON Wiesbaden community, Hari Nāma Desh, Germany.

## Yogyakarta

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day, please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is because of your causeless mercy that we have been able to maintain our worship of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. Of course, we perform our worship with the simplest standard, in accordance to our local situation. But still, this means a lot to us, since by performing this worship we are always in touch with the sweetness of devotional service.

We realize that any improvement in our worship would demand more duties and responsibilities. Slowly we are trying to increase our service. Recently, we have made a new altar, and on April 23, 2007, we had an installation ceremony for the Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, a dream we have been waiting so long to realize.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, most of our *āśrama’s* residents are fulltime students at different colleges here in Yogyakarta. Yogyakarta is well-known as the “student city” of Indonesia, and also as a center of culture. Here we find many archeological artifacts of the Vedic culture that once prevailed in the ancient Hindu kingdoms of what is now known as Indonesia. It is our humble desire that the presence of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara will increase our enthusiasm for preaching in this wonderful place. More and more we are realizing that to spread Lord Caitanya’s mission here in Indonesia, we have to adopt a more academic and cultural approach. Please give us the necessary intelligence.

Please kindly be merciful to us so that we will be able to serve the Deities properly under the guidance of our spiritual master and your senior devotees.

Thank you again for your matchless gifts.

Your devotees at the Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Ashram in Yogyakarta, Indonesia.  
(written by Suryalocana Dāsa)

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (India)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this wonderful day, we, your followers at the BBT in India, want to convey to you our deep gratitude for allowing us to serve you by publishing your books. Your books are not ordinary, and the proof is that even after so many years of printing the same books they never become old or hackneyed.

Thirty years ago your books were giving so much knowledge and inspiration regarding devotional service. Today also so many persons are finding newer and newer evidence about the ultimate goal of life from your books.

We are completely satisfied keeping your books in print, which is a challenging job because your books are selling as fast as we can print them. In India a record number of your books were distributed during the 2006 Prabhupāda Marathon. Over one million hardbound books were distributed by your mercy. Almost 200,000 small books and over 100,000 *Back to Godhead* magazines were also distributed. Never in history have so many religious book been sold in India in such a short time.

Reliance, one of the biggest companies in India today, wanted to print your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and give it as a gift to their foreign customers. When the people from Reliance came to the BBT with their proposal, I asked them why they chose to print your *Gītā,* and they replied, “Because when anyone thinks of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* this one comes to mind. Also, *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* is the only *Gītā* with such beautiful artwork.”

Everything you do, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is just perfect for attracting the conditioned souls to Kṛṣṇa. Your ability to engage all kinds of persons is your most glorious quality. If someone knows painting, you engage them to paint for Kṛṣṇa. In this way you encourage the devotees and attract the conditional souls to Kṛṣṇa by devotional service.

We pray for your continued blessings and mercy.

Yours servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in India.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**How I Met You**

I remember sincerely inquiring into spiritual life, and you somehow made an arrangement for me to touch several of your books at an airport, even though I did not take one then. Later you arranged for your disciples to share some *prasādam* with me while I heard the holy names on a *harināma* party. Still later you somehow or other appeared in my dream and I saw you being worshiped with a *cāmara* whisk. I didn’t even know what a *cāmara* was then.

When I eventually read your magazines and books, I was very attracted — I thought, “I would like to work in your publishing department.” That was my lofty daydream.

I met one of your disciples who explained a little about Kṛṣṇa consciousness to me. I was invited to your temple, and the devotees engaged me in the service of the Deities and your many disciples. They encouraged me to read your books, chant the holy names, and especially to go out on book distribution. I still don’t understand my good fortune.

I was sitting next to your *mūrti* in your rooms in Los Angeles, and I sincerely asked you, “How can I serve you?” Then you made an arrangement for me to serve you directly in your publishing department at the BBT. Now I have the honor of writing my first letter to you as a Vyāsa-pūjā offering on behalf of the North American offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

I just want to thank you for your mercy and for fulfilling my lofty daydream. Thank you for being my grand–spiritual master and the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON and the BBT. Out of your constant mercy you have given the conditioned souls the opportunity to come in contact with you, even without meeting you personally, just as I have. Although I have not met you personally, I have met you! You are present in the transcendental words of your books. You are present in your many instructions. We have your continued association through your *mūrti*. And you are clearly present in the cooperation among your world-wide family of disciples and granddisciples.

It is a great honor to be able to serve your mission by working for your BBT, which is so dear to you. Its value cannot be estimated, because you and your gifts are not of this world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me the strength and intelligence to serve you, your servants, and your mission. Please bless me and the other members of the BBT staff so that we may continue as part of your *saṅkīrtana* army.

Your servants at the North American offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Gauḍacandra Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

You once crossed the ocean with forty unspendable rupees and a treasure hidden in a battered Indian trunk. No one expected you and no one announced you. No willing or receptive audience awaited. Yet you came alone on the strength of the preaching assignment you had received from your guru, suffering separation from your beloved Vṛndāvana, to an unknown land with unknown prospects.

Yet you did not actually travel alone. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* was your hidden treasure, and the *Bhāgavatam* is Kṛṣṇa incarnate. While living in Vṛndāvana and writing your First Canto purports, every day you offered fresh *tulasī*leaves to the *Bhāgavatam*. Someone noted, “That was a beautiful thing.”Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was a beautiful thing that you carried the Kṛṣṇa-*Bhāgavatam* in a trunk on an Indian steamer after first writing commentaries in the vernacular of an audience you had not yet met. How could you have known how to touch the spiritual consciousness of so many covered souls in so many lands and bring it to life? You electrified us by touching us with the finger of your faith in the holy name, in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and in the intrinsic need we each have to heed Kṛṣṇa’s call.

The personal sacrifice you made to deliver *kṛṣṇa-nāma* and the Kṛṣṇa*-Bhāgavatam* is unprecedented in Vaiṣṇava history. In a bewildering ocean of *kaitava-dharmas* you became and remain the essential agent of faith. Your compassion and unrelenting clarity relieve our fatigue of worldliness and make Kṛṣṇa tangible to us. Service to you is not optional, yet the opportunity to serve you is your blessing.

We at the North European BBT have somehow received your blessing to offer a service vital to your mission. You wish to induce all people to engage in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. For people to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your association is essential. You are the creator of faith and the repository of Kṛṣṇa’s compassion. Without the faithful production and dissemination of your books and teachings, where will people find you? You write,

Transcendental loving service for the Supreme Lord is the natural inclination of every living being. The instinct is dormant in everyone, but due to the association of material nature the modes of passion and ignorance cover this from time immemorial. If, by the grace of the Lord and the great-souled devotees of the Lord, a living being becomes fortunate enough to associate with the unadulterated devotees of the Lord and gets a chance to hear the unadulterated glories of the Lord, certainly the flow of devotional service takes place like the flow of a river. As the river flows on till she reaches the sea, similarly pure devotional service flows by the association of pure devotees till it reaches the ultimate goal, namely, transcendental love of God. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.28, purport\]

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow us to serve you with gratitude and humility as through your books you release the floodgates of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. We are unqualified servants, but we belong exclusively to you.

Your servants at the North European BBT.  
(written by Kaiśorī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Spanish)

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you on this divine day of your holy appearance!

The manifestation of Your Divine Grace in this world is an event that is so extraordinary and hard to understand. When scriptures use the concept of causeless mercy to express how the appearance of the spiritual master happens, one idea that comes to mind is that your descent is not forced by karma or any other material law. At the same time, another way to understand the concept “causeless” is in relation to the fact that everything anyone does in this world has a materially motivation or purpose, and that no one living in this world goes beyond this condition of doing something, giving something, or working on something without personal selfish interest. Only those whose transparent heart is devoted without any reservation to the Supreme Lord are beyond this conditioning of coming to this world to satisfy their personal desires. Therefore your coming to this world is a fact that leaves our minds perplexed, because your appearance is not the result of a material personal cause.

Even more difficult to understand is the fact that you left the manifested Vṛndāvana in this plane to come to the West for twelve years, since there is no material cause to your coming to the most godforsaken part of the world. The only possible explanation is that you were the only Vaiṣṇava of the time willing to help us.

And what is most incomprehensible is that when you left this world you decided to permanently stay with us to give us your guidance, association, and mercy though your wonderful and transcendental books.

The laws of the Supreme Lord organize things in such a way that people with the same purpose and karma come together to try to fulfill their goals. You know better than anybody else that we are not free from personal motivations, so by mundane logic we would not have been able to share in your activities of service to Kṛṣṇa. Our minds do not find a proper explanation, and we are left in complete wonder by the fact that, although you knew very well that we lacked all qualifications, you decided to engage us in Kṛṣṇa’s mission of printing and distributing your transcendental books. This is your very confidential *kṛṣṇa-karma,* or Prabhupāda-devotional-karma.

Eternally indebted to you for such an undeserved privilege, we, the members of the “BBT Hispano” team, are dedicated to introducing your divine and merciful personality to every single individual in the Spanish-speaking world in the form of your transcendental books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for as long as we are the recipients of such a wonderful opportunity, please give us the power to take it and serve you to the best of our capacity.

Your servants at BBT Hispano.  
(written by Aravinda Dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Western Pacific)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our worthless obeisances in the dust of your holy lotus feet. All glories to you, who mercifully light our stumbling, bumbling way with your perfect example of complete surrender and love for Their Divine Lordships!

It is so gratifying to hear the ongoing and increasingly wonderful service of your disciples and granddisciples, providing us with vivid examples of your ever-present mercy. For it is only through your mercy that all this service is really happening at all. Only a fool would say that you are not still here with us! How else could all of this worldwide book distribution and these global preaching projects continue and expand so nicely? If we but open our eyes and ears, we can see that your transcendental magic is happening, in some way, nearly everywhere on the planet (e.g., your divine message now travels and changes hearts through the Internet, through Krishna.com, to those rare places where we may not be able to physically go at present).

This is, all of it, real spiritual service, which requires real spiritual inspiration. And that inspiration is ultimately all coming from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You are the eternal foundation of our lives, and everything we may attempt to do to help spread the glories of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa owes one hundred percent of its success to this very same foundation. Your smile is there in every *kīrtana*, and it is right there alongside every transcendental book that is distributed. And through your books, your merciful smile is within every heart that suddenly “gets the taste” for Kṛṣṇa and then eagerly wants to begin serving Him, once again.

Your presence is felt intensely at your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust. We feel somehow that we share in your excitement whenever one of your titles is printed in a new language. We hope and beg to share that excitement with you as new titles of your books appear soon in Turkish, Khmer, Korean, Chinese, and other languages whose readers we are fortunate to be serving through our BBT division.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to inspire us in this service, which is so very dear to you. We beg you to please never withdraw your inconceivably kind and causeless mercy. And please allow us to continue doing something, anything, to serve your most dear and vital mission: Getting your transcendental books into the hands of everyone in their own primary language. In this way, your divine blessings and this eternal knowledge will enter and awaken their hearts in the most profound way possible. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your servants at the Western Pacific division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa Dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Academic Outreach Preaching (Lagos)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya*!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, over the years we have been involved in the advancement of Vaiṣṇava philosophy by proffering solutions to political, social, and economic issues in the global village. In this regard we present papers at academic conferences, publish in specialty journals, and teach courses in universities based on the teachings of Your Divine Grace.

On this auspicious occasion of the appearance of Your Divine Grace, we humbly report our engagement in major academic conferences, from January to June 2006.

On the 30th of March, 2006, the North Carolina Political Science Association (NCPSA) had its 35th annual conference at High Point University. The theme of the conference was “Whiter Governance.” At the said conference we presented a paper entitled “Self-restraint and National Development.” The paper was presented at the “Issues in Foreign Policy” panel. The abstract read:

Self-control over speech, behavior, and action amongst political stalwarts engenders good governance. Good governance is the bedrock of national development. Focus, health, colossal output, selfless service, Sofia or divine wisdom, sharp intellect, freedom from social dilemmas, economic development, superlative political power, higher pleasure, superhuman potentials, perfection, longevity, and *vichara-sakti*, or “the power of inquiry” about the Absolute Truth — all results of self-restraint — are elements that enhance national development. The indices of Sub Sahara Africa’s present political, social, and economic trends show that policies do not take cognizance of self-restraint as a basic factor for national development. It is in view of this that the study focuses on the relevance of self-restraint in national development. . . .

Prior to and after the panel presentations, we had pleasant exchanges with some of the political science professors, including the president of the North Carolina Political Science Association. The president gladly introduced us to his friends, and he was particularly emphatic that we keep in touch and requested that we try to come for the NCPSA 2007 conference, which he said was scheduled to be held on his campus.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we had the opportunity of being at San Antonio, Texas, to present a talk at the Southwestern Social Science Association (SSSA) annual conference, April 12–15, 2006. The title of my paper was “Women’s Self-restraint for National Transformation: Vedic Approach.” The paper was slated for delivery on the “Women and Social Policy” panel. We had three speakers on this panel. A female professor presented her paper first, and then we presented our talk. The abstract read, in part:

Women, being carriers of culture, have a vital role to play in transforming human society. The relevance of self-restraint to societal transformation cannot be overemphasized. Women’s self-restraint, as evidenced in Vedic literature, is of tremendous effect on societal transformation. . . . Women’s self-restraint is effective in evoking their potential for societal transformation. The cases of Silavati Mataji and Tulasi Devi, who, by dint of the potency of their chastity created fortune in their micro-communities, are very intriguing. Since micro-communities are the microcosm of the nation, it is evident that women’s self-restraint is capable of changing the world positively. . . .

Our presentation generated some salient questions, which we answered to the satisfaction of the professors. The professor who chaired our panel, who also presented a paper, made references to our paper during his presentation. The paper was of high acceptability to the co-panelists and the audience.

On April 22, 2006, we were at John Jay College of Criminal Justice, Manhattan, New York, to present a paper at the New York State Political Science Association (NYSPSA) annual conference. Our paper was titled “Self-restraint and Nigeria’s Development: An Ethical Inquiry.” After the presentation, some of the professors asked relevant questions, which triggered further interesting discussion. The abstract read:

In Nigeria, past governments have experienced hiccups in politics, economy, and the social milieu. This is not unconnected with lack of self-restraint among the citizenry. The subject matter of this study is to bring to bear how self-restraint could foster Nigeria’s development. Connotatively, by self-restraint we would be referring to the ability to control the urge to speak hogwash, yield to the mind’s demands, yield to actions of anger, and obey the urges of the tongue, belly, and genitals. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, as cited by Śrīla Prabhupāda, asserts that our material identification creates three kinds of drives: the urge to speak, the demands of the mind, and the demands of the body. These urges, when not properly moderated and channeled in the service of our constituency and the Absolute Truth, could bear dismal consequences for the nation. When the citizenry is unduly under the sway of the aforementioned three urges, the nation’s future becomes bleak. Antithetically, when the citizenry is endowed with self-restraint rooted in the consciousness of the Absolute Truth, more often than not the nation is permeated with ethical orientation, and sustainable development ensues. Indices of Nigeria’s present political, social, and economic trends show that policies do not take cognizance of self-restraint as a variable for national development. It is in view of this that the study focuses on the relevance of self-restraint in Nigeria’s development. . . .

At a conference on Religion, Globalization, and Conflict: International Perspectives, organized by the Center for the Study of New Religions (CESNUR) and held from July 13 to 16, 2006, at San Diego State University, our paper, “Resolving Religious Crises for Sustainable Democracy in Nigeria,” was accepted after due assessment and is now available at the official website of the CESNUR 2006 conference, at http://www.cesnur.org/2006/sd̮das.htm. The abstract read:

Wanton slaughtering of humans and destruction of properties in the name of religion is not something new in Nigeria. *Vāsanās,* or desires, ignorance, the three *guṇas*, or modes of material nature, cognitive deficit, and stress have driven more than a few Nigerians to the brink of disaster. Television, gangs, unemployment, poverty, drug abuse/alcoholism, denial of basic civil and human rights, and pseudo religiosity have contributed immensely to religious crises in Nigeria. When religion is practiced under the checks and balances of shastra, or authentic scripture, an *ācārya*, or exemplary anti-material mentor, and *sādhus*, or holy men or women, it creates cohesion, which engenders sustainable democracy and national development. The study viewed the foregoing, amongst others, and proffers that if we repose unalloyed love in the Supreme Personality of Godhead, automatically religious crises become historical remains and sustainable democracy and national development will thrive.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank Your Divine Grace a trillion times for allowing us to be an aspiring insignificant servant of the servants of the servants of your devotees who are mercifully furthering your mission.

Your servants Academic Outreach Preaching, in Lagos, Nigeria, West Africa.  
(written by Vāsudeva Dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine / Krishna.com

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Even while contemplating this 111thanniversary of your transcendental appearance, I was also reminded that this year marks thirty years since your disappearance. So much has changed over these thirty years, but your spirit of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness continues in so many varied ways.

In the 1970s you were focused on producing your books and making sure they were read and distributed all over the world. You were opening temples and speaking to audiences around the globe. You had incredible energy and determination to spread Lord Caitanya’s message and the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and you requested and empowered your disciples to carry on this mission.

You began *Back to Godhead* in 1944 on the auspicious appearance day of your spiritual master, to help fulfill his instruction to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in English. By your mercy, there were nearly a million copies of *Back to Godhead* distributed around the world in 2006, and we hope to keep that number increasing.

Thirty years ago, I was still a schoolgirl and was not fortunate enough to have met you or your disciples. But five years later I received the mercy of guru and Kṛṣṇa through kind devotees who gave me *Back to Godhead* magazines. When I received my first copies, I couldn’t wait to read them. Each issue was so beautiful, and my favorite section of the magazine was “How I Came to Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.”

We have heard so many accounts from devotees that their first contact with Kṛṣṇa consciousness was through a *Back to Godhead* magazine, and we hear from many who say that their main connection to this society of devotees is now through your *Back to Godhead*, which you called the backbone of this movement. *Back to Godhead* is now bigger and more beautiful, colorful, and vibrant than ever, and published in several languages.

So much of my own devotional life has been connected to your *Back to Godhead* magazine. While in college, I kept in touch with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement with a BTG subscription, and for the past thirteen years I have worked for BTG/Krishna.com in various services. I owe so much to you and to BTG, and I am very happy to work every day to try to pay back this unpayable debt by helping spread the message you began teaching over sixty years ago.

You followed in the footsteps of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, using technology in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Someone once said to me, “Śrīla Prabhupāda would never have used computers or the Internet. His message was all about simple living.” I couldn’t disagree more. We cannot forget about the “high thinking” part, without which the “simple living” has no spiritual basis or significance. You were in the forefront of dovetailing technology in the 1960s and 70s, using everything for Kṛṣṇa, including jet airplanes and printing presses and the then state-of-the-art Dictaphone tape recorder. You knew that the perfection of these devices was to use them to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, just as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī taught with his principle of *yukta-vairāgya*. Krishna.com also tries to follow in this tradition and use everything at our disposal to bring Kṛṣṇa’s holy name and message to every town and village.

I can’t help thinking that you would be so pleased to see your movement and the message of Lord Caitanya spread so widely over the Internet through Krishna.com. Over one million people came to the Krishna.com website last year, including over 10,000 unique visitors on Janmāṣṭamī day 2006 alone! Through the use of webcams, Krishna.com is now bringing the temples to people who would otherwise have no access. Temple *kīrtanas,* programs, and lectures are broadcast on our website and heard by thousands of people as part of their daily *sādhana*. All of your books are available online for persons in other countries who have no access to your books or who are forbidden by their country to receive this literature. Anyone can listen to your *bhajana* recordings on our website. Krishna.com Live Help volunteers are available to chat online with persons with questions about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And new this year is Krishna.com’s weekly Sanga podcasts, designed to bring news, discussions, interviews, and meditations to our worldwide audience. This is your *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga* at work today. Krishna.com has been translated into Chinese and Portuguese, too, with more languages to come. All of your timeless teachings are freely available with just a click of the mouse, and they continue to inspire devotees and aspiring Vaiṣṇavas alike, reaching households everywhere in this digital age. I strongly believe that these technologies have been created just so that we can archive all of your recordings, books, videos, photos, etc., to preserve them and to share them for generations to come.

We pray for your blessings so that we may continue in these efforts to assist you.

Your servants at Back to Godhead/Krishna.com.  
(Lokasākṣinī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.19.33), Mahārāja Parīkṣit addresses Śukadeva Gosvāmī:

*yeṣāṁ saṁsmaraṇāt puṁsāṁ sadyaḥśuddhyanti vai gṛhāḥ*  
*kiṁ punar darśana-sparśa- pāda-śaucāsanādibhiḥ*  
*sānnidhyāt te mahā-yogin pātakāni mahānty api*  
*sadyo naśyanti vai puṁsāṁ viṣṇor iva suretarāḥ*

**Simply by our remembering you, our houses become instantly sanctified. And what to speak of seeing you, touching you, washing your holy feet, and offering you a seat in our home? Just as the atheist cannot remain in the presence of the Personality of Godhead, so also the invulnerable sins of a man are immediately vanquished in your presence, O saint! O great mystic!**

While we cannot be compared to Mahārāja Parīkṣit, you certainly are the representative of Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī at this time. Just as Śukadeva Gosvāmī brought the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to Mahārāja Parīkṣit and the great sages assembled at Naimiṣāraṇya, you have brought that same *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to every town and village on this planet. And just as the presence of Śukadeva Gosvāmī sanctified all who came in contact with him and his recitation of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, so you are purifying this planet by the mercy of your books.

Your definition of preaching is much more than just speaking words to unsuspecting people. It is a whole package, from the highest reaches of philosophy to cooking and cleaning and simple living. From the most esoteric *pūjā,* like *śālagrāma* worship, to the door-to-door selling of your books. It is at the same time very easy to perform the individual activities required and yet very difficult to maintain as a total way of life. You embodied this life of higher consciousness in such an exemplary fashion that one begins to appreciate the verse in the *Bhagavad-gītā* beginning *manuṣyāṇāṁ sahasreṣu*. You are indeed one of those very, very few *siddha* devotees that Kṛṣṇa mentions in this regard.

Thirty years after your disappearance, you remain our guiding light and inspiration. If one faithfully reads your books and follows your instructions, one is assured of receiving the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Your purports and lectures combine simplicity and depth. Your preaching of the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the science of self-realization, blend Eastern mysticism with Western pragmatism. This style of preaching began with Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and was continued in the line by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda.

We can only aspire to follow your example and help spread the words of the great sages and spiritual scientists. This is the only remedy that can deliver the world, and in the process we also may sanctify our lives and be delivered from the curse of atheism and impersonalism that is so rampant in this dark age.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (South Africa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace!

You wanted that we preach as vigorously as you did  
If to you we at all feel indebted.  
Alas! Our attempts are like a dwarf trying to touch the moon.  
That you granted us this opportunity is our good fortune.  
You spent tireless nights in writing purports and translating;  
To scrutinizingly study them you were constantly encouraging.  
Hence we offer our humble attempts to fulfill your desire:  
To study your books and preach is to what we aspire.  
You expressed a desire that we write examinations.  
Last year 826 students went through BCEC graduation.  
Classes were held last year in Cape Town, Durban, Brampton,  
Inisrath, Kisumu, Johannesburg, Mauritius, London,  
Montreal, Nairobi, Pietermaritzburg, Pretoria  
Portland, Toronto, Vancouver, and Mombasa.  
Our humble attempt is to present your teachings  
In interactive ways the students find enlivening,  
Thus bringing newcomers to our movement  
And transforming them into devotees and serious students.  
Many continue to serve and even take initiation,  
Assuming responsibility to teach and thus continue your mission.  
On this auspicious day, falling in the dust of your lotus feet,  
We pray that your instructions in our hearts become concrete,  
That our service to you becomes increased,  
And that by our humble attempts you become pleased.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture.  
(written by Mādrī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta College (Radhadesh)

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to the fathomless ocean of your Vaiṣṇava compassion!

We at Bhaktivedanta College in Rāḍhadeśa consider ourselves privileged to be sporting in a rising wave of that nectarean ocean — the development of systematic education in ISKCON.

The undercurrent of this wave is the cumulative learning needs of your ISKCON members and descendants. Learning needs are teaching opportunities — this is a foundational principle taught in the teacher training courses at your Bhaktivedanta College. Those who would be educators in your ISKCON need first-class education in order to expertly represent Your Divine Grace.

Bhaktivedanta College thus aspires to serve your mission by providing a sustainable and sustaining program of education that combines suitable teaching techniques and a well-rounded Kṛṣṇa conscious curriculum with a sound practice of *sādhana*.

We are increasingly grateful to Your Divine Grace for providing us with so many service opportunities. Our fervent prayer is that we may be blessed to become, and to offer at your lotus feet, Vaiṣṇavas who fulfil the purport of “loving servants of Kṛṣṇa and humanity.”

The students and staff of your Bhaktivedanta College at Rāḍhadeśa, Belgium.

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!
