The best thing about modern technology is how it enables me to be with you. As I walk about with my mp3 player and earphones, I feel like I am in the front row as you are giving class, walking beside you on Juhu beach, or sitting in on a garden conversation in Los Angeles. Your voice — strong, urgent, leading, powerful, empowering, and also soft, gentle, beckoning, assuring, confident — goes in the ear and straight to the heart. The mind becomes quiet. Everything stops and listens. The voice of the master commands and gives shelter.

I joined because of you and am still here today because of you. When I read your biography back then I knew that you knew the truth and that you were someone who was telling it truly. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can I ever express my gratitude, how can I ever begin to thank you for what you have done for me and countless others by your selfless service to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

You have changed my life for the better, forever. And I see how you are daily changing the lives of the community of learners in the *gurukula*. We are no longer satisfied with the black-and-white version of living, having been splashed with the colors of your teachings, your Vṛndāvana, your Kṛṣṇa. You have given us a Kṛṣṇa that is too beautiful to resist.

We pray we can do something for you, and that the graduates from your *gurukula* in Vṛndāvana will make you proud by their preaching.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvaneśvarī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You want a Bhaktivedanta Institute that does scientific research on the nature of life, consciousness, and the universe. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* provides the basis for this research, since it gives so much knowledge about the natural world in the teachings of Lord Kapila to His mother Devahūti. Many scientists can perceive the problems with their current classical worldview. In time, if we endeavor earnestly and cooperatively, then they will come to accept the *Bhāgavatam’s* knowledge of the natural world. Our realization is that this will necessitate that the BI scientists come up with alternative ordinary-language descriptions of the natural world that enable us to introduce the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* description of the *tanmātras*. A corollary effect will be that this new science will have a place for values in the world, which the current classical science cannot provide.

But what did you mean by “scientific”? Your Divine Grace used the word “scientific” in different ways. For example, at times you would say that devotional service is a great science. From the point of view of a modern scientist, this statement is very difficult to understand, since science today is concerned with an empirical study of the natural world. Of course, philosophers of science have debated the precise difference between metaphysics and science, and although many books have been written arguing one view or another, there is still a lack of a clear distinction.

Your Divine Grace berated scientists because they are blind to the spiritual dimension. Your Divine Grace berated scientists even more because they were not being scientific in their materialistic claims, by their own empirical standards. Your Divine Grace thus made the following *empirical challenge* to the scientists:

The scientific method includes observation, hypothesis, and then demonstration. Then it is perfect. But the scientists cannot actually demonstrate in their laboratories that life comes from matter. They simply observe and then speak nonsense. They are like children. In our childhood, we observed a gramophone box and thought that within the box was a man singing, an electric man. We thought there must have been an electric man or some kind of ghost in it. \[*Life Comes from Life,* Second Morning Walk, 19 April 1973\]

Because of their meditation on and fascination with natural phenomena, you classified the scientists as *śāktas:*

The modern scientist is also captivated by the wonderful actions and reactions of natural phenomena and therefore is also a *śākta*. These lower-grade persons gradually rise to become *saurīyas* (worshipers of the sun-god) or *gāṇapatyas* (worshipers of the mass of people as *janatā janārdana* or *daridra-nārāyaṇa,* etc., in the form of Gaṇapati) and then rise to the platform of worshiping Lord Śiva in search for the ever-existing soul, and then to the stage of worshiping Lord Viṣṇu, the Supersoul, etc., without any information of Govinda, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the original Lord Viṣṇu. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.6.43–45, purport\]

In this passage Your Divine Grace is saying the scientists may ultimately rise to the platform of worshiping Viṣṇu, but they will remain ignorant of Kṛṣṇa.

Your Divine Grace berated the *karmīs* for working like donkeys night and day for useless sense gratification. However, devotees understand that Your Divine Grace also showed how such *karmīs* can be directed to engage the fruits of their work in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Devotees understand that the fruits of *karmīs’* labor in the form of *lakṣmī* can be used for performing their own devotional service. In the same way, Your Divine Grace is also revealing through the BI how the fruits of scientists’ labor to understand material reality can also be engaged in devotional service, though it might be thought that scientists’ endeavors are irrelevant and useless for devotional service. You have given the clue in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34. Also, in *Kṛṣṇa Consciousness: The Topmost Yoga System,* Your Divine Grace has stated:

Kṛṣṇa says that out of many millions of people, one may understand Him. But, by chanting of this Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, as introduced by Lord Caitanya — chanting and dancing— you can understand Kṛṣṇa within a very short time. Knowledge begins not from Kṛṣṇa but from things which we are accustomed to see every day.

Land is gross. If you touch it, you can feel its hardness. But, as soon as the land becomes finer, it is water, and the touch is soft. And then again, from water to fire, still finer. After fire or electricity the air is still finer; and after air, the sky, ether, is finer still. Beyond ether, the mind is still finer; and beyond the mind, intelligence is still finer. And, if you go beyond intelligence to understand the soul, it is finer still. From these elements people have discovered so many sciences. There are many scientists, for example, who are soil experts; they can say, by analyzing a particular type of earth, what kind of minerals are there. Somebody seeks out silver, somebody seeks out gold, somebody seeks out mica. This is knowledge of gross things — the earth. If you go to finer substances, then you study water, or liquid things, such as petrol and alcohol. Go still finer, and from water you will go to fire and electricity. If you study electricity, you have to study all sorts of books. And, from this finer fire, you will come to air. We have so much advancement in our airplanes; we are studying how they move, how they are made — now sputniks and jets — so many things are being discovered.

Next comes the study of the ethereal: electronics, ethereal transformations from one thing to another. Then, finer still, is the mind — psychology and psychiatry. But for intelligence, rationalism, there is only a little philosophical speculation. And what about the soul? Is there any science of the soul? The materialists have none. Material science has advanced to the study of the ether, or the mind and intelligence, but there is no advancement beyond that. Beyond intelligence, they do not know what exists. But in the *Bhagavad-gītā* you can find this.

We are attempting to dedicate our lives to your transcendental mission of scientific preaching in the Bhaktivedanta Institute. Your Divine Grace has stated that the Bhaktivedanta Institute is the scientific preaching arm of your ISKCON movement. Your Divine Grace has also stated that the Bhaktivedanta Institute is meant for those who would not otherwise come to our temples. We are practically experiencing that the scientific preaching we have developed at Mumbai and Berkeley during more than twenty-four years under your divine guidance has immense potential to take the preaching of the ISKCON movement into domains where we would have no access if religion is mentioned in any way. Soon we hope to offer you for your transcendental pleasure an official recognition from India’s official Department of Science and Technology for the scientific work of your Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai. We continue to depend on Your Divine Grace in the full faith that this is the frontier of scientific preaching that needs to be developed as per your wishes, and we only need to continue to strive to serve you for your transcendental pleasure.

Recently we received a letter from Bhakta Venkatesh, a Ph.D. student in physics at the world-famous Stanford University, who has come in touch with your Bhaktivedanta Institute in Berkeley. We offer Your Divine Grace excerpts of his letter for your transcendental pleasure.

From: Bhakta Venkatesh  
Sent: Monday, April 09, 2007  
To: Rasaraja Prabhu  
Subject: Realizations

Dear Rasaraja Prabhu,  
Hare Krishna. Pamho. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I would like to share some of my realizations that I have had so far in your association about the work of Bhaktivedanta Institute at Berkeley. First of all, I must say that I am extremely happy to have come in contact with you all and learned about your work. Apart from your scientific achievements, your dedication to serving Srila Prabhupada against all odds is very, very inspiring to me. Such an attitude of selfless service is very rare, and to render such service all alone in the midst of all kinds of criticism is even more rare. This service is very special and also extremely challenging, but it has far-reaching consequences. Srila Prabhupada wanted the devotee scientists to bust the hoax of modern science, and I think that the approach being taken in B.I. Berkeley and Bombay has truly captured the essence of how exactly that can be done. This I say not only from a devotee’s point of view but also from a scientist’s point of view. . . .

I have been studying quantum mechanics and also applying it for many years, but there were several aspects of it that I never really understood on a conceptual level. I just took things for granted, assuming that they were all correct. Only after discussing these issues with you did I start to see the real problems in the current understanding of quantum theory. From the physics point of view, I agree with you that the problems you are pointing out are valid. . . .

What surprises me is how big, big scientists could not have seen this before. That, I think, is because they are under the clutches of Maya. Unless devotees, by following Srila Prabhupada, show them the reality, they will never see it. This is the unique contribution that real scientists can make: to remove the illusion of all so-called scientists and of the general public who are blindly following them. To bring them to the light from the darkness of this so-called science is possible only by developing science in the right way, the way you are doing it.

After getting introduced to Krishna Consciousness, I started to think that the science I was doing was all Maya. All study of matter is like that. Clearly, I was ignorant about the Sankhya philosophy of Lord Kapiladev and was therefore contemplating leaving science altogether. It is only on meeting you that I realized how important a contribution we as scientists can make by attempting to serve Srila Prabhupada. Srila Prabhupada clearly saw the hoax of modern science and wanted the devotee scientists to prove this in a scientific way, so that everybody *has* to accept it. The way I see the B.I. doing this is by employing the scientific way. Just attacking the ideas of modern scientists will not help. The moment these new concepts in quantum theory become noticed in the scientific community, they will create a revolution in the world of science and the whole of human civilization.

How can I say all this with confidence? Because I have never come across concepts like these before. The very quality and beauty of the concepts that you have enlightened me with directly reflect to me divine intervention in your work. It is nothing like the usual mundane speculation. Also, from whatever little service I have done so far I have realized that serving Srila Prabhupada in science is not different from any other transcendental service in Krishna Consciousness. . . .

From whatever I have understood, I can . . . say that the developments that Srila Prabhupada’s B.I. at Berkeley and Bombay is making within science are very fundamental and revolutionary. The world of science is soon going to change due to these contributions. Establishing the correct understanding of matter based on Srimad-Bhagavatam will truly glorify Srila Prabhupada, all the previous Acharyas, and Lord Sri Krishna amongst classes of men who would otherwise not get the opportunity of coming in contact with the great Krishna Consciousness movement.  
Yours,  
Venkatesh

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance, we have tried to record our humble glorification of your amazing breadth of vision in every frontier of preaching on the planet. You are truly the savior of all living entities, being specially empowered by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your endeavoring servants in the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Berkeley and Mumbai.

## Book Distribution Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You started book distribution in America in 1965 when you brought your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatams* from India and put them in various stores in New York City. They would sell on consignment. That was the beginning. Since then over 500,000,000 of your books have been sold! Every seven seconds a book is sold somewhere in the world! The sun does not set on your book distributors.

A few days ago I distributed your *Bhagavad-gītā* to a student at a university. The next day I saw him again and he said, “I read the first chapter last night. Nice. Thank you for being here.” All over the world people are appreciating your words of wisdom. Śrīla Prabhupāda, not only are we distributing them, but the professors are using them in their classrooms. When another student saw me with your *Bhagavad-gītā* he said, “We study that book in our humanities class.”

I asked, “You mean the one with these pictures in it?”

“Yes, that very one.”

Some devotees have been distributing your books for over forty years. One disciple of yours has been in a wheelchair for years with no legs, but he has never stopped distributing your books. Recently, while I was at the San Diego temple distributing books with a nice young *bhakta* named Bronson, I asked him how he had come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He said he had received a book from the devotee in a wheelchair. His name is Rādhānātha Dāsa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us a wonderful service, in which we are being purified and the conditioned souls are learning about your object of love, Lord Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us with a fraction of the compassion you have; then I’m sure we could flood the whole world with your books.

Your servants at the Book Distribution Ministry.  
(written by Vijaya Dāsa)

## Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I wish to express to you my deepest feelings of esteem, affection, and gratitude.

When I think of you, the first impression that comes to me is your profound devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, from which came your firm dedication to His service, which characterized your saintly life.

When I listen to your lectures, I am most often struck by your deep faith in the *śāstras*. Your vast knowledge of holy Vaiṣṇava scriptures and your life totally dedicated to God — to me, these are the conjunct causes of your unbreakable convictions about the meaning of life, its sacredness, its origin, and the aim of the universe.

When I read the letters you wrote through the years in diverse circumstances and to diverse recipients, I am struck by your profound compassion toward every single person. Whether through your letters, your books, or in person, you unceasingly attempted to inspire trust, courage, and hope for a better world based on the awakening of one’s loving relationship with Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your message of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* was meant not only for your disciples but for anyone looking for a genuine spiritual guide. In the spirit of abnegation you always acted for your listeners’ or readers’ awakening, for their ethical, moral, and spiritual upliftment, without discriminating between those who were ready and those who weren’t ready to receive teachings inspired by the highest divine consciousness. Hippies or bourgeoisie, intellectuals or the uncultivated, the young or the old, men or women — all those you came in contact with received a genuine opportunity to illuminate their lives and to joyfully set off toward knowledge and love.

As I listen to your disciples’ stories, episodes of exchanges of spiritual love between you and them constantly emerge from their memories. Thirty years after your physical disappearance, your disciples’ attachment to Your Divine Grace and your great and pure affection for us constitute the strongest affective link and the most reliable practical model of spiritual behavior in this world. The seed of love you implanted in our hearts has not only outlived your disappearance but in some cases, by germination, has turned into a luxuriant tree loaded with spiritual flowers and fruits.

When I look at your images, I am always struck by the intensity and depth your look expresses: it goes right to the viewer’s spiritual essence; it comes from and points to a dimension out of time and space. From your person emanate compassion and trust in the divine nature of man and, consequently, in his latent possibility to reconnect with God in love, freeing himself from all his conditionings. Especially in films, your images express those rare and precious dynamic and ambivalent combinations of calm and urgency, gravity and joy, worry and trust, vitality and inner dominance, caution and boldness, rationality and intuition, command and submission, adherence to the law and the authority of forgiveness. These and many other rare dynamic balances present in you are the fruit of a perfect integration of your personality, typical of an intelligence illuminated by divine inspiration, which can harmonize and transcend pairs of opposites and thus unfold on transcendent and immanent levels, as well as, according to context, conceptual and operational levels.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Śrī Kṛṣṇa defines such a personality by the term *nirdvandva:* free from the influence of opposites (duality). To my eyes you appeared more than a liberated being — rather, a most generous lover of God, impatient to share your joyous realizations with whoever desired them.

By observing the trails you have left in history, by analyzing the ways that, in a few years, without means and in old age, you performed your precious deeds in this vast and turbulent world, one can have an insight into your inner greatness. The first reflection that comes to my mind is your immense faith in divine providence. While an egotistical personality would ascribe to himself the success of his activities, however temporary, your God-centered character allowed you to see, realize, and describe Śrī Kṛṣṇa as the supreme author of your enterprise. What you counted on for your actions was not your personal strength but the Supreme’s, to which you were always connected through your feelings of love for and devotion to your Guru Mahārāja.

May the Lord of the worlds and of our hearts allow me to dedicate my life to you, too. To you, who have freed me from illusion and placed the seed of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* in my heart, may I humbly offer all my desires, thoughts, words, and works in a progressive feeling of pure and sweet devotional love. One day, if you so will, may I also join you to play together in *kṛṣṇa-līlā*.

Your servants at the Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta.  
(written by Matsya Avatāra Dāsa)

## Dandavats.com

My divine spiritual master and ever well-wisher, dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this special day of your transcendental appearance, please accept my obeisances unto your lotuslike feet thousands and thousands of times.

This is me, the Greek-bodied lowly servant of yours named Citraka Dāsa. Lowly, yes, but the one you made fortunate by raising his consciousness and changing his life’s destiny forever, making him a candidate for re-entry into the transcendental realm of the Supreme Lord! Oh, how much happiness you have created for me all these years, Śrīla Prabhupāda, an infinite happiness found within the loving spiritual atmosphere your mercy generates, a mercy that is still, incessantly, nourishing me, loving me, protecting me, and inspiring me each and every day of my existence!

From the depth of my being I will never stop being grateful to you for having taken the burden of bringing the teachings of Lord Caitanya to the Western countries, where I was loitering in Kali-yuga’s kingdom, immersed in illusion and my selfish dreamland before I met your movement through your sincere followers.

I am grateful, yes, but mere sentiment or lip service is never enough to satisfy a Vaiṣṇava’s heart. I know too well your express desire about the best way you can be “repaid” for such mercy, which is by our extension of the same courtesy to other living entities, as well as bringing ourselves safely to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

And here is the “trick”: accomplishing this twofold mission automatically implies the realization of another very well known expectation of yours — that we, your disciples, would cooperate to maintain and expand your ISKCON society. How else could we ever change the lives of others? How could we possibly educate them and purify them by pushing back the influence of Kali-yuga unless we systematically combine our efforts, qualities, and talents for such service?

It sounds simple, natural and logical: cooperate! Cooperation is a well-known principle of all successful human endeavors, mundane or spiritual. But what deep maturity, self-control, and fortitude one needs to accomplish that! What powerful gross and subtle enemies must be defeated before we can even speak about actually cooperating!

The question to be answered is: Which principle should be honored most, unity or purity? It is a fact that purity brings unity. It was easy to feel united when we had the example of your pure personality. But without such an embodiment of spotless character as you, how can there be complete unity? After all, one may say, it is natural for the soul aspiring to purity to feel revulsion at any contamination that may manifest its ugly face in the form of a variety of human weaknesses. And such a striving soul may therefore resolve to keep distant from such impurities, not wishing to compromise or misrepresent the spotless science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But on the other hand, how can we ever become effective in the mission of bringing other souls to your lotus feet unless we act together in a systematic way?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am writing these words because I consider that my and our love for you should overcome the inconvenience we may sometimes experience from our fellow devotees’ *anarthas*. Am I correct? It ultimately depends upon our faith in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness process itself. If we cannot trust that it will eventually purify those who practice it, how can we ever convince newer people to adopt it?

Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us the proper way to perceive our sincere godbrothers, despite their faults. Once Prabhupāda was informed that one of his uninitiated disciples had begun to backslide and was not always strictly following the regulative principles. Prabhupāda declared, “No, no, he is very good boy . . . He is keeping Jagannātha within his beadbag and chanting . . . No, he’s our well-wisher, a good boy.” \[*A Transcendental Diary,* by Hari Śauri Dāsa\]

In my home city there lived a very well-behaved family with several children. Once one of the children fell into a ditch and became dirty. Some passers-by who saw the child considered him a street kid and avoided him completely. But I, what to speak of his parents, never took his temporary unclean state seriously. We knew it was just the result of an unfortunate fall into the ditch. With love we all helped him wash off the dirt and return to his original state of cleanliness.

Similarly, Lord Kṛṣṇa and you, with your transcendental vision, Śrīla Prabhupāda, know us all from before our falldown into this material world. You can see our real spiritual identity, free from the contaminated material coverings, so repulsive! So, without being disturbed by them, you trust and love us always and know it is just a question of time before we get back to our original pure state of existence. You never reject us as long as we follow you, but you trust us and love us and associate with us.

So, am I right to safely conclude that trying to adopt toward our fellow devotees the transcendental vision you have toward us, and placing our love for you — or at least the sense of duty and debt we owe you — beyond anything else, are the great facilitators for achieving the goal of cooperation that you desire from us? And is it also correct that the maintenance of such vision directly depends on the strict following of the four regulative principles and the offenseless chanting of the *mahā-mantra*? I would tend to say so, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because this is what you so many times instruct in your books.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue blessing me with your association and service, the only shelter in this constantly degrading Kali-yuga environment, and I am sure that it will not be long before you allow me to join you in the transcendental world of pure devotional service in whatever part of Kṛṣṇa’s creation He desires me to do so.

Your servants at Dandavats.com.  
(written by Citraka Dāsa)

## Festival of India (USA)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We try to remember you every day, but on this day, your holy appearance day, we make an extra effort to remember your transcendental potency.

Every year at our festivals, tens of thousands, if not hundreds of thousands, of conditioned souls read the Festival of India’s exhibit panels, with excerpts from your transcendental books, conversations, and lectures. They read about the philosophy you so kindly and clearly gave the world. They taste *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* hear the holy names Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, and then go home with one or more of your transcendental books.

Often we meet persons who have received and read one of your books. They tell us how their life was changed; again, we remember you, and how you are fully manifest in your books. In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.29.39–40, you write, “The secret of success in the cultivation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is hearing from the right person.” When we see how your words have changed the hearts and consciousness of countless conditioned souls, it becomes clearly evident that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are “the right person.” When we place one of your books in a person’s hands, you are entering their life. The seed of devotion is just waiting for them to start watering it by reading/hearing from you.

What you have given the world is beyond the greatest materially philanthropic service. You have actually carried out Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction/prediction:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“In every town and village, the chanting of My name will be heard.”**

To start something wonderful is a special thing in this world. To help deliver a conditioned soul from the cycle of birth and death is the ultimate humanitarian act. To write and print books that will deliver countless millions of conditioned souls by taking them back home, back to Godhead, to the spiritual world, is the ultimate benevolent and compassionate activity.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to deliver Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and as long as your books are available, you’ll be delivering people all over the world.

On this most auspicious day, we pray for the determination to continue serving your mission, primarily by distributing your books and the holy names, Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your aspiring servants on the 2007 Festival of India crew.

## Food for Life Global

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your statement “Everyone should be given a chance to take *prasādam* . . .” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 1.32) constantly beats in our head. We do not understand exactly how to fulfill this order of yours, but we do know that with your mercy it is possible. Our sincere desire is to please you by expanding *prasādam* distribution universally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the world is in so much pain at the moment. I don’t need to explain this statement. Everyone can see the wars and the lies, the raping of the environment, the inconsistent weather patterns, the starvation, pollution, and disease, and the gradual loss of all forms of freedom. It appears that the demons of the world have a firm stranglehold on basic morality and purity.

Even some of the devotees of the Lord are becoming polarized and desensitized to all these indiscretions, hypocrisies, and exploitations. Few, if any, do anything about it or speak out. It is a sorry state of affairs.

More than ever the world needs your leadership, your compassion, your wisdom, your vision, your mercy, and your unbreakable determination to fight the demons.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, without sounding like the foolish “end-timers” of this world, we plead for your presence. Please appear in our dreams, our plans, our actions, and our hearts. We need you so much now!

We feel strongly that expanding *prasādam* distribution can significantly improve life on the planet. The simple and caring act of preparing food and sharing it with others is universally recognized as fundamental to the development of deep and meaningful relationships. It is for this reason that our mission at Food for Life Global is “Uniting the world through food.”

You started the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement with the Sunday Feast, and you told us over and over again that preaching will be more effective when there is *prasādam* distribution. All the members of Food for Life projects and other *prasādam* services inside and outside of ISKCON have personal and practical realization of this truth. “When the people take to eating only *prasadam* offered to the Deity, all demons will be turned into Vaiṣṇavas,” you said. We believe it, and it is our sincere hope that more and more devotees of the Lord inside and outside of ISKCON will also come to believe this simple truth: *prasādam* has the ability to purify consciousness.

Every day we pray over our offering to Kṛṣṇa, with full faith that He will indeed accept the offering and transform the ordinary food into something transcendental. We can’t see this change, but we can certainly taste it! However, the faithless will not accept that any transformation has taken place. They always want proof. So we feed them too, and they also taste something special about the food, even if it is the simplest recipe or has been prepared by an inexperienced cook. If they cooked with love, the food was transformed.

In this connection I remember one funny incident that happened when I was cooking for the Food for Life Club at Sydney University. The student union had hired me to cater their meeting on Monday night. That day I was so busy with other services that I forgot to cook anything. Then at 6:00 p.m. I got a call from the secretary of the student union reminding me of the catering. In a panic, I felt my mind go into overdrive. I looked around and saw that nothing had been cut up as well. In a spirit of creation, I remembered that the day before I had cooked too much for the Sunday Feast. Great! I could “reincarnate” the leftovers into something exotic and tasty for the students in no time! The rice and *sabjī* soon became spicy *koftas,* and the leftover *purī* dough became breadsticks. I then added extra oats and coconut to the leftover *halavā* and made cookies. And the **\[fix\]** *piṝce de rīsistance* was my chutney, consisting of leftover pineapple chutney mixed with vanilla custard! Yes, the students loved it all, and I got special compliments on the unusual but tasty chutney dish. “What is the recipe for the chutney, Priya?” I just smiled and felt thankful for your mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees at Food for Life Global see themselves as the servants of your *prasādam* distributors. We beg to remain so and pray that you will empower us to do more and more and more, until one day everyone will have gotten a chance to take *prasādam*!

Your servants at Food for Life Global.  
(written by Priyavrata Dāsa)

## Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Cultural Organization (GLOVESCO)

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who continue to shower your mercy on all sincere souls!

In our own feeble way, those of us endeavoring to promote the ideals of a *varṇāśrama*-based rural society at GLOVESCO continue to strive toward fulfilling your mission and vision of self-sufficient Kṛṣṇa conscious communities all over the planet. We are not the only ones making this endeavor, and it is always refreshing and comforting to see tangible progress made by some of your staunch devotees around the world. We pray that your desires in this respect may become known and followed by more and more devotees.

As I write this offering, preparations are under way to organize a worldwide Go-Sammelan Conference in South India. Lakhs of sympathizers and supporters, from India and abroad, will attend this unprecedented nine-day global event. There is thus some awakening to this important cause of *varṇāśrama-dharma,* where the cows and bulls play such a crucial role. Traditionally, especially in the sacred land of Bhāratavarṣa, the cows and the bulls were accepted as the pillars of society. Practically everyone lived off the land, and nearly everyone maintained some cows. Unfortunately, with the influence of Kali-yuga, severe neglect of cows has been increasing at an alarming rate in the past few decades all over the world, including in India itself. According to the 2003 census of the Department of Animal Husbandry of the Indian government, the projected figures for the Indian cow population for 2010 is a mere 20 million, compared with 430 million just a few decades ago. Many years ago you planned to meet the then Prime Minister, Indira Gandhi, to propose that India take a leading role in preaching the *Bhagavad-gītās* message, of which cow protection is such a vital component. Perhaps this Go-Sammelan will help make more people aware of the urgent need to protect Kṛṣṇa’s cows, and India may yet take the leading role it is meant to take.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us the philosophical reasons why cow protection is so important, and this teaching of yours is one of your most essential contributions to our present-day ailing society, though it is poorly understood. You wisely taught that one’s character and how one meets the necessity for food are closely related. Character without Kṛṣṇa consciousness is imperfect, just as obtaining an adequate supply of food without cow protection is also not feasible. What we fail to understand is that cows cannot be protected unless we simultaneously protect our villages. We cannot protect and honor Kṛṣṇa’s cows unless we simultaneously protect and honor Kṛṣṇa’s plan to maintain His cows, i.e., the villages. It is in the villages, and mainly in the villages, where one can live according to the principles of simple living and high thinking, thus realizing the principles of self-sufficiency based on Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If only we can stop and ponder these simple truths, how clear this vision and mission of *varṇāśrama-dharma* could quickly become a reality.

Protecting and honoring cows and villages have far-reaching implications for the long-term economic viability of any society. No society can remain self-sufficient, sustainable, and prosperous without cows and villages. And without cows and villages, the entire *varṇāśrama-dharma* social arrangement cannot become manifest, because both the cows and the land are meant to feed the brahminical culture:

Progressive human civilization is based on brahminical culture, God consciousness, and protection of cows. All economic development of the state by trade, commerce, agriculture, and industries must be fully utilized in relation to the above principles; otherwise all so-called economic development becomes a source of degradation. Cow protection means feeding the brahminical culture, which leads towards God consciousness, and thus perfection of human civilization is achieved. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.19.3\]

Because we are so far removed from the care of cows and are ignorant of their special features in society and their value for all human beings, we have become more and more oblivious of the cows’ contribution to our lives. Our devotees serving at the Sahyadri Śrī Kṛṣṇa Balarāma Kṣetra, in the district of Udupi \[www.sahyadriksetra.org\], wrote in their recent Nārada Bulletin as follows:

Our cow Subhadra, calf Laksmi, and bull Balaram have adapted nicely to their new home at our farm. They are let out into the fields in the morning. After roaming about the whole day throughout our land, they come back inside the goshalla at dusk. This auspicious time of dusk, when cows return home, is technically called go-dhuli lagna. The word “go-dhuli” is made from go (cows) + dhuli (dust), and lagna means time. Hence, go-dhuli lagna signifies the time when the cows return, raising the dust of the earth with their hoofs. This dust raised by the hooves of cows is very purifying.

In this way the cows are showering their blessings on us by giving us milk, cow dung, cow urine, and also the opportunity for engagement in go-seva, which is so dear to Lord Krishna. Their sight itself is so pleasing to the mind.

In the *Śrī Caitanya-bhāgavata* (*Ādi-khaṇḍa* 10.90), the author, Śrīla Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura, when describing the meeting of Lord Caitanya with his future wife Lakṣmī-priyā, specifically refers to this time of *go-dhūli* as being most auspicious.

These and so many other wonderful benefits from the cows and the bulls are not known to the general populace nowadays, and unfortunately they are often unknown to many of our own devotees as well.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this 111th anniversary of your appearance, we would like to renew and rekindle our strong commitment to serving you by serving the cause of *varṇāśrama-dharma*. Without implementing the basic principles of cow protection and cultivation of land, we will never succeed in building a viable community where individuals and society can progress both materially and spiritually. You have shown us the way, and you have given us your valuable instructions. Kindly help us all become more serious and more determined about following in your footsteps. We beg your forgiveness for our mistakes and shortcomings. Please shower your mercy upon us, for without your mercy we cannot advance in devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at GLOVESCO.  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## International Society for Cow Protection (ISCOWP)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, our most beloved spiritual master!

You have given us the perfect philosophy for solving all the problems of our lives. Due to our imperfections, this perfect philosophy often remains only in our minds and our words. Integrating this philosophy into our everyday lives and making it the force behind our actions constitute the full implementation of your teachings.

For us imperfect disciples, you were personally present to touch our hearts, and your personal presence allowed us to hear your teachings and put them into action. Not only have you given us the perfect philosophy, you have given us your love and affection. How fortunate we have been to be near you and observe your pure presence! This experience has illuminated the quiet reflective moments that energize our souls in times of desperation.

How can the heart and not just the mind understand your teachings of cow protection? How can we move from speaking about cow protection in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class to actually brushing a cow, sitting with a cow in the pasture, milking a cow, working with an ox, or chanting to an ox in his last moments?

As your personal association touched our hearts, the personal association of other great souls helps us also. We have known such souls in cow bodies, and hearing of their pastimes touches our hearts and motivates us to follow your instructions for cow protection. Such a soul was Vraja, one of our oxen. The following are excerpts from our ISCOWP News memorial issue for Vraja, consisting largely of letters to and from members of ISCOWP.

Dear Vaninatha prabhu,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

It was a pleasure to read your letters, your history, and your realizations. We were so happy to have Vraja adopted by you. But there is some very sad news to tell you.

Yes, Vraja is the same ox you saw at the Washington, DC, Rathayatra in 1992. He and his brother Gita led many Rathayatra parades across the country. The potency of their preaching was beyond the preaching most of us devotees are able to offer to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Vraja was so extremely handsome and noble that he attracted so many people to him.

If you look at our blog (iscowp.blogspot.com), you will read how Vraja had arthritis for the last 2 years. Vraja being old for an ox (they rarely live 16 years), the arthritis was not a surprise. We treated it with some alternative medicines, as allopathic medicines really had nothing to offer. However, in the last month he was struggling to overcome it and was becoming weaker. We hoped he would make a comeback, as he had done so many times in the last 2 years.

We kept him separate from the herd because he had fallen and was having trouble keeping up with them. He was in the loafing shed (geriatric barn), with the gate open, and could roam the adjacent lawn. Radharani was also there with him as company. She is an old cow, 22 to 25 years old, but she is in good shape. The way the barns are laid out, Vraja had daily association with the rest of the herd.

A week ago, he lost his footing and fell flat on his side. Chaitanya Bhagavat, trained by Balabhadra, is taking care of the cows in Balabhadra's absence. (Since we do not take funds from ISCOWP for our personal income, Balabhadra is in a mall for the winter season, generating funds for ourselves. We do not collect enough to cover the cow projects and pay ourselves.) Chaitanya Bhagavat found him. Chaitanya talked to him and soon Vraja got up. However, Chaitanya noticed that he was walking less steadily than before.

When Vraja fell, all the cows came down the hill to see if he was okay. Ujala was moaning. Vraja got up and Ujala stopped moaning. The next day when Chaitanya went to feed Vraja, the herd came down the hill at a trot to see how he was feeling. They were stretching their necks over the fence to talk to him. He seemed to be assuring them that he was okay. Shelda Bloomingdale, a volunteer, was there and wrote about it. (Her story appeared in the ISCOWP News memorial issue and on iscowp.org ).

At this time, the weather was glorious, some cold nights but also some warm days. There were no flies or bugs, etc. Vraja was enjoying himself. He loved to be near the apple tree that was close to the house and ate almost all the apples. The night before last, Vraja must have fallen and could not get up. When we woke up in the morning, Chaitanya heard Ujala moaning and quickly got out of his cabin and ran in the direction of the moaning. Vraja was down near the apple tree. He was by the fence line and all the cows were around him. He had struggled in the night to get up, but could not. His face was looking up at the herd. He was very weak. Chaitanya chanted the Maha Mantra and two names of Krsna (Govinda, Gopala) in his ear. He then told Vraja he would be right back. Then he left to get me to see if there was something we could do. When we got back to him, he had left his body. The herd was still there. It was Sunday, 10/29/06.

We are very much missing him. He was such a noble soul. To his last days he remained majestic and noble, and we are happy that he did not have to linger in the barn for a long time. We are thankful that his last days were spent pasturing with the herd nearby. We are thankful that he heard the names of Krishna right before his death. We are also thankful that he obtained you as his adopter in his last days, that he wasn’t forgotten by his admiring public while in his retirement.

We are most sorry to give you this news, as we have been to experience it. We will eventually have a memorial to him on the ISCOWP web page, blog, and newsletter.

\* \* \*

10/30/06

Today we buried Vraja in the field. Ray tried to do it while the herd wasn’t looking, but near the end when Vraja was being covered with dirt, they figured it out and ran over to the spot, bellowing. That bright moon evening they returned to the spot where he was buried. Bhima, Nanda, Gita, Surabhi, Vishaka, and Kalki sat in a circle around the spot where Vraja was buried. They were mooing and crying in memory of Vraja. It was as if they were holding a memorial service. Chaitanya Bhagavat joined them, and the memorial service went on for several hours. The next morning Ujala was bellowing, and Chaitanya Bhagavat found her kicking the dirt at the highest point of Vraja’s mound and rubbing her face in the dirt. Then she looked up and joined the herd moving past Vraja’s burial place and onto the barn.

Vraja was their lead cow. He was like their father or grandfather. From the time they were babies he was the big protector and decided where the herd would go and who could do what. When anyone would come into the field, Vraja would approach first and analyze whether the person was friend or foe. If a stranger came into the field not accompanied by one of us, he would chase them out of the field. When another cow went down, he would be right there supervising our rescue efforts. He would stand over all of us (he was 7 feet to the tip of his horns), watching.

Now Gita has taken up the position as King of the Herd. In fact, he had already taken up this responsibility when Vraja was not with the herd. However, Vraja still had the respect of the herd. He was sure to maintain a proud and noble stance, holding his head up high even when arthritic and at the time of death.

The pain of his separation will take awhile to pass, after our having protected and loved him for the last 16 years. We welcome any remembrance writings about Vraja to include in his memorial tribute.

Your servant,  
Chayadevi

\* \* \*

My dear longtime friend Chayadevi and all others with the ISCOWP farm.

I am so deeply saddened about the passing away of grandfather Vraja. What a wonderful being he was! Such a wise and kindhearted soul. I’m glad that I was able to spend time with him just two years ago.

I will never forget when Vraja and Gita stopped by my house for a few days for some R & R between the Los Angeles and San Francisco Rathayatras. They were just young kids then and just as cute as could be. You had saved them from within one day, I believe, of being sold. I loved giving them my front yard as their home. How they caused quite a commotion when sighted by the cars that drove by! Many people came over to pet them, and they learned about your wonderful program of cow protection, a vegetarian diet, and how the oxen love to use their tremendous strength to work. That year I went to both Rathayatras, and I could just see that they enjoyed being in the parades and getting so much attention and admiration from the crowds.

I also remember the previous year, when you brought the fully-grown oxen Burfy and Lee (short for Lilananda) for an R & R stay. One evening after dark I was out sitting and talking with them and giving them big hugs and affection as they laid in my front yard, both of them over 1,000 pounds each. With Balabhadra’s encouragement, I laid on one of their sides. To this day, I can still feel his warmth and the sounds of his deep inhale and exhale with the up and down movement of his chest as he lay there in complete trust and contentment.

I don’t think people realize just how gentle, considerate, and intelligent these huge beasts are. They need our protection and our love, and in return they will give their very life, their total dedication, in service to us. Can anyone say that about a tractor? Does a tractor soften our hearts or teach us lessons of life? Can a tractor show us the interconnectedness of all living creatures on the deepest spiritual and emotional level? From these great animals we can learn all there is to know about relationships and our own false ego and hang-ups that keep us from surrendering fully to the Supreme Personality of Godhead and being in harmony with all that is around us.

Prabhujis, all glories to your most important service to the people of planet Earth and to the Earth itself. You have dedicated your lives so fully to bringing the importance of cow protection to the attention of the masses. I wish for you all facility for your expanding service. I know that your hearts are heavy with the loss of Vraja and that it will take time for both you and the animals to grieve his loss. My love and prayers go out to you.

Your friend and servant in the service of Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Urvasi Devi Dasi

\* \* \*

Dear Devotees,

Here is a remembrance I wrote of Vraja.

One day I was working in our garage when this pickup truck came flying out the lane from Balabhadra’s and stopped in a cloud of dust.

“Do you know who lives at that farm?” the guy asked me.

“Yes,” I replied.

And he said, “I just spent the last hour in a tree with my friend. A big bull chased us and we had to climb it to escape. He finally moved away a little and I was able to get down and make a run for the fence, but my friend is still up in the tree!”

I looked at him for a while, then asked, “Were you carrying a 5-gallon plastic bucket?”

His jaw dropped and he looked at me in surprise. “How did you know that?” he asked.

The reason I knew was that I had had similar experiences, and had guessed he had been carrying tools in a bucket. I forget why he was out in Balabhadra’s pasture, but it was some legitimate thing, working on a phone line or something, and he needed small hand tools.

While I was still healthy and doing Farmer’s Markets and Balabhadra was still getting established, we used to clean out his barn in exchange for keeping the manure. I would back down into the barn, and Tulasi and I would load 5-gallon buckets with the black gold. Having it in buckets made unloading it and applying it to the garden easier.

Vraja would come into the barn and be a real annoyance. He would get in the way. He would poke his horns in the buckets of manure and then toss his head. I tried to keep chasing him away, but he was very persistent. I actually scheduled runs to the barn at the times I knew he would be out in the pasture, where he couldn’t see us. It wasn’t that he was mean, but the chance he would step on your foot inadvertently or swing his head to chase flies and catch you with one of his long horns was a consideration. Mainly, though, he was just in the way.

Turns out it wasn’t that he necessarily liked hanging out with us; some curiosity was there, but mostly it was the buckets. Balabhadra fed him grain out of one, so he was conditioned to expect some goodies whenever he saw one and would crowd in so he didn’t miss anything. Hence, whenever he saw someone in the pasture with a bucket, he would naturally follow him, and his enthusiasm for an expected treat had been misinterpreted as aggression. I still laugh visualizing those guys up a tree with Vraja waiting patiently below.

Madhava Gosh

\* \* \*

Vraja touched the hearts of many devotees. More devotees wrote their remembrances, which are printed on the ISCOWP web page (iscowp.org).

We are eternally grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your instructions as well as your love and affection. Your love and affection touched our hearts, which allowed us to hear your teachings and put them into action

Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture.

## ISKCON Central Office of Child Protection

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

It has been many years since you left us to return to your pastimes with Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world. As the years pass, our feelings of separation from you increase and we lament the loss of your loving yet firm guidance both in our lives and in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You were that strong captain of the ship who always kept us on course. Now we are not always certain if we are truly heading in the correct direction, or whether our efforts will fully gain your favor. We recall the wonderfully secure feeling of just knowing that you were always there, watching out for your ISKCON society and the lives of all of your disciples. While many of us did not have the mercy of close personal association, we knew that if we ever really needed that instruction and encouragement it would be available. Your devotees also had the conviction that if we strayed from your instructions and desires, you would always swiftly pull us back in line and teach us the proper path. Now, in the absence of your physical presence, those direct instructions and corrections are not so apparent.

Yet as those years of separation increase, so has our conviction that you are ever with us. While your physical presence is now a cherished subject of our remembrances and realizations, your spiritual presence continues to manifest in ever-new and -vital ways in our lives. We have been forced to come to a higher level in our relationship with you, one in which we must perceive you in your spiritual manifestation rather than in your corporeal form.

Our greatest fortune is that your presence continues to be there for all time, due to the foresight and mercy of your disciples who painstakingly recorded all the moments of your twelve years with us. We will always be grateful for the love, intelligence, and service of these devotees who have helped preserve your legacy through compiling your writings, classes, words, pastimes, and pictures. We recently visited the Bhaktivedanta Archives, and felt great satisfaction in not only seeing the rare nectar from your earliest pastimes in the West, but how your disciples have painstakingly preserved your every word and instruction. These efforts of your faithful followers will help keep your memory alive for generations to come and increase our understanding of your unlimited qualities and amazing accomplishments.

But there is also a deeper level of realization of our spiritual connection with you — as our eternal spiritual master. You once confirmed the understanding of one disciple that you are ever situated in our heart, along with the Supersoul, if we desire you to be there. While Kṛṣṇa accompanies every living entity as he transmigrates through various species, the spiritual master also resides in the heart of the surrendered disciple.

To the degree that we hear from you, your knowledge will awaken within us.

To the degree that we are directed by you, your presence will pervade our lives.

To the degree that we know your desire, you will guide all our activities.

To the degree that we surrender to you, your love will manifest in our hearts.

To the degree that we serve your mission, you will empower our service.

To the degree that we know Kṛṣṇa through you, His dearmost servant, we will experience the love and mercy of the Supreme Lord.

Our work in the Child Protection Office is often difficult and burdensome, and sometimes it feels like a thankless task. But our growing faith is that when those difficulties are taken on your behalf, with the desire to serve and please you, then the full mercy of Lord Nityānanda, Lord Caitanya, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara may be attained through the mercy of Your Divine Grace. By our surrender to you, the difficult becomes a burden of love and the stress becomes an opportunity for greater surrender and realization. We have experienced that when we simply serve on your behalf, fully endeavoring yet striving for detachment from the result, wonderful things occur. We may participate in the unfolding of your mission and your pure service to Kṛṣṇa in ways that would be impossible for us to do if we were to rely on our own limited strength. For the disciple, there is no greater happiness than to feel oneself an instrument in the mission of the guru.

Our eternal, spiritual connection with you resides deep within the heart. Through this bond of love and service we may realize your desires, align our hearts and minds with those desires, and become empowered by you to render some small service to this powerful Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. May we always be granted the gift of service to your lotus feet.

Your eternal servants at the ISKCON Central Office of Child Protection.  
(written by Tamohara Dāsa)

## ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Although during your physical presence your ISKCON focused mostly on temple dynamics, and your followers were mostly temple residents, you left precious directions for the times (like now) when most of your followers would be based at home:

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is trying to elevate human society to the perfection of life by pursuing the method described by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His advice to the *brāhmaṇa* Kūrma. That is, one should stay at home, chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* and preach the instructions of Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* \[*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya-līlā* 7.128\]

Meditating upon and implementing this three-part instruction will generate a powerful wave of home-based practitioners and preachers, eagerly advancing in personal purification and enthusiastically spreading the message of Lord Caitanya.

We, your servants at the Congregational Development Ministry, look forward to seeing real vibrant synergy between all the devotees in building dynamic communities growing qualitatively and quantitatively in the shade of your lotus feet.

Your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry.  
(written by Kaunteya Dāsa)

## ISKCON Resolve

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are truly a genius! I have recently returned to my University studies after a twenty-seven-year gap. In all my courses on conflict resolution, I write about you and your ISKCON in hopes of rendering some service to your devotees, who are so dear to you. By learning conflict and management theory and mixing with people of other faiths, I have grown to appreciate your genius more.

In a course on negotiations, for example, I was in a mock debate about vegetarianism with a visiting scholar, a leader of the Orthodox Church in Egypt. I repeated your simple arguments that you present in your books and discussions. After a few exchanges the bishop said in front of the whole class: “I’m going to have to go back to the Bible and look at it again in light of your convincing arguments.” I smiled, knowing it was your simple logic that was so powerful.

In a class on family systems theory, we studied the characteristics of an effective leader. According to that theory, the main cure for poor leadership is for the leader to become “self-differentiated” and avoid “over-functioning,” avoid having the younger members overly reliant on the leader for micro-managing their lives. Instead, leaders need to offer followers challenges that help them grow. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you understood this perfectly, and your letter to Karāndhara Dāsa, written before most such theories were ever articulated, spells out these principles. There you wrote that devotees should be independently thoughtful, and that centers should be registered separately, without over-centralization, which leads to a beggar mentality. You also specifically say there that leaders should offer others fresh challenges.

Nearly all the courses I have taken emphasize listening to others as the key to developing rapport, helping others open up, and understanding one another. Again, you had the experts beat. Back in 1972 you wrote to Balavanta Dāsa:

Now I want that we shall recruit more and more of our men amongst the intelligent class of men. They, because they are little educated or they have got some wealth or fame or ability, so they will be sometimes little puffed-up, but that is all right, they deserve it. Now we shall have to learn the art how to approach such higher-class of men and attract them to apply themselves to this Krishna Consciousness process of self-realization. That requires much tact, and we shall have to expect to meet all challenges by sharp minds. But if we remain always absorbed in remembering Lord Caitanya, how He converted so many intelligent men, even sitting for three days and nights to hear them speak without Himself speaking anything, and if we remember how Krishna was so much patient to explain everything to Arjuna, even Arjuna was speaking like a fool — in this way, being always tolerant of others and appreciating their points-of-view, it will be easy matter for us to convince them gradually to join us. So you have got a very great opportunity for this kind of preaching work.

As I study other faith traditions from a management point of view, I am again struck by your expertise. The ISKCON you created was (and still is!) a tremendous challenge to lead and manage. You somehow transported a philosophy and culture all over the world, showing that it can apply in the most diverse settings and conditions. You transformed into saints those who had no interest in spirituality. You somehow kept us so indebted to you that we stuck together in your presence and continue to do so nearly thirty years later in your absence. No doubt there is practically unlimited room for improvement, but for today I just want to thank you for all you have given and all we have. It is all because of you. You are truly the genius and the captain of this ship. May we serve you selflessly and create a society that makes you proud of us.

Your servants at ISKCON Resolve.  
(written by Braja Bihārī Dāsa)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You instructed your disciples to put you on TV,  
An instruction that resonated loudly with me.  
The past three years we worked hard at ITV  
To display your *vāṇī*and *vapu* in the best quality.  
Madhudviṣa Prabhu worked diligently,  
Night and day he labored incessantly.  
Uddhava-gītā prabhu was sent by your mercy;  
He learned the programs and worked quickly.  
The images of you came out brilliantly,  
Enhanced by complex technology.  
Your voice sounds as if you are giving a live delivery.  
Additional footage completes the video library.  
Devotees around the world translated incessantly:  
Twenty languages have been subtitled abundantly.  
Hindi, Russian, and Chinese expand the availability,  
Spreading Mahāprabhu’s message as much as can be.  
The project could not have been done enthusiastically  
Without the support and conviction of Jitarati.  
Distribution in China will increase dramatically.  
Your *darśana* will transform theists’ enemies.  
In India the set is being produced domestically;  
For the first time it can be obtained duty-free.  
Your countrymen can take your *darśana* easily.  
Their eyes will carry their ears to your potency.  
So many devotees contributed graciously:  
The cameramen, writers, editors all contributed freely;  
Archivists, technicians, and those who gave philanthropically —  
Each made the impossible a possibility.  
Special thanks to Yadubara for his wizardry.  
He re-organized all the silent-footage imagery,  
Commencing from your early days with that “Māyāvādī”  
To the final moments of your corporal energy.  
We are forever grateful for the privilege of glorifying you.

Your humble servants at ISKCON TV.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda Dāsa)

## ISKCON Vedic Farm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most holy of all days, O beloved lord and master, we again feel so blessed to be able to glorify and worship your lotus feet.

By your divine grace we remain constantly happy and enlightened in your daily service. Your causeless mercy allows us to always plunge into the nectar of your written and spoken words and feel ever-increasingly spiritually refreshed and simultaneously fully protected from the relentless onslaught of the three modes of material nature.

O most holy of holies, those who are blind cannot recognize Your Divine Grace as the most empowered and spiritually advanced saint of this century, a position you occupy because no one is more dear to Oṁ Viṣṇu-pāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta SarasvatīGosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda then your lotus self, and no one is dearer to Lord Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and ŚrīmatīRādhārāṇī than he. Thus you are both unlimitedly kind reservoirs of loving grace. Not only do you give the highest of the most confidential teachings, as clearly revealed in your wonderfully sweet *Kṛṣṇa* book translations of the thirty-first chapter of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, but you especially give the ways and means by which one can genuinely attain to that most blessed of shelters. Your careful guidance teaches us how to proceed with the most sober reverence and devotion into that most august of assemblies. The proof is that Your Divine Grace cures us of *aśuddha-buddhi,* or having an intelligence polluted by lusty desires (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.10.9) and *asat-tṛṣṇā,* or again becoming thirsty to experience illusory gratification separate from Lord Kṛṣṇa.

We humbly bow down to your ever-merciful lotus feet and thank you for engaging us for another year in assisting you in your blissful service to the darlings of your heart, Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma. The more we are thus engaged in transcendental service, the more we realize how much we are eternally indebted to you, O most compassionate friend of us who are most fallen, and the more we feel happiness by basking in the soothing rays of your love-saturated glances.

We beg to remain  
Your humble servants at ISKCON’s Vedic Farm, Mauritius.

## The Krishna Center

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda: The Source and the Fruit of Our Fortune

In the scriptures there are many statements extolling the benefits of *sādhu-saṅga*, association with Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotees.

*sādhu-saṅge kṛṣṇa-bhaktye śraddhā yadi haya*  
*bhakti-phala ‘prema’ haya, saṁsāra yāya kṣaya*

**By associating with a devotee, one awakens his faith in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Because of devotional service, one’s dormant love for Kṛṣṇa awakens, and thus one’s material, conditioned existence comes to an end. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **22.49\]**

*‘sādhu-saṅga’, ‘sādhu-saṅga’ — sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya*

**The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that by even a moment’s association with a pure devotee, one can attain all success. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **22.54)**

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has stated that this invaluable association with pure devotees or a bona fide spiritual master is attained only through *bhāgya*, fortune.

*kona bhāgye kāro saṁsāra kṣayonmukha haya*  
*sādhu-saṅge tabe kṛṣṇe rati upajaya*

**By good fortune one becomes eligible to cross the ocean of nescience, and when one’s term of material existence decreases, one may get an opportunity to associate with pure devotees. By such association, one’s attraction to Kṛṣṇa is awakened. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **22.45)**

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **19.151\]**

In the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (1.4.15) it is indicated that this necessary fortune manifests in the form of *śraddhā*, initial faith.

*ādau śraddhā tataḥ sādhu- saṅgo ’tha bhajana-kriyā*

**In the beginning there must be faith. Then one becomes interested in associating with pure devotees. Thereafter one is initiated by the spiritual master and executes the regulative principles under his orders. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **23.14\]**

But where does this fortune come from? Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla Prabhupāda have explained:

Is this *bhāgya* (fortune) the result of an accident or something else? In the scriptures, devotional service and pious activity are considered fortunate. Pious activities can be divided into three categories: pious activities that awaken one’s dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness are called *bhakty-unmukhī sukṛti,* pious activities that bestow material opulence are called *bhogonmukhī sukṛti,* and pious activities that enable the living entity to merge into the existence of the Supreme are called *mokṣonmukhī sukṛti*. These last two awards of pious activity are not actually fortunate. Pious activities are fortunate when they help one become Kṛṣṇa conscious. The good fortune of *bhakty-unmukhī* is attainable only when one comes in contact with a devotee. By associating with a devotee willingly or unwillingly, one advances in devotional service, and thus one’s dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness is awakened. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya-līlā* 22.45, purport\]

For *bahirmukha*, materialistic persons, hearing about Kṛṣṇa occurs by chance, not because of applied *śraddhā*. However, such hearing is designated as *bhakty-unmukhī-sukṛti*, piety based on nascent devotional inclination, and if continued may eventually beget *śraddhā* in some later life. \[*Jaiva-dharma*\]

“*\[B\]hakty-unmukhī* involves faith in and appreciation of pure devotional service. Such exalted piety is achieved by unknowing performance of pure devotional service, without spiritual knowledge (*ajñāta-sukṛti*). *Bhakty-unmukhī* is the most important kind of *sukṛti,* and it leads the *jiva* to meet a pure, saintly devotee. By one’s associating with a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, the *jīva’s* faith becomes firm. Gradually he develops a taste for chanting the holy name, becomes compassionate toward all beings, and is fixed in devotional service. \[*Śrī Harināma-cintāmaṇi*\]

So this *sukṛti,* this fortune, there is a chance which is called *ajñāta-sukṛti*. *Ajñāta-sukṛti* means just like Kṛṣṇa’s representative is preaching all over the world, and if somebody comes in contact with that representative of Kṛṣṇa, and satisfies him, he acquires some *ajñāta-sukṛti*. This is called *ajñāta-sukṛti*. He does not know what spiritual benefit he’s achieving by serving the representative of Kṛṣṇa. He does not know. But he acquires it imperceptibly. This is called *ajñāta-sukṛti*. So when *ajñāta-sukṛti* is accumulated, then he becomes fortunate. That is called *kono bhāgyavān jīva*. . . . When \[*ajñāta-sukṛti* is\] accumulated, that makes the fortune of a person to associate with guru and Kṛṣṇa. *Ei rūpe brahmāṇḍa bhramite kono bhāgyavān jīva/ guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpāya pāya bhakti-latā-bīja.* \[*Nectar of Devotion* lecture in Vṛndāvana, 29 October 1972\]

The theory of chance can best be explained in the Vedic literature by the words *ajñāta-sukṛti,* which refer to pious activities performed without the actor’s knowledge. But these are also planned. For example, Kṛṣṇa comes like an ordinary human being, He comes as a devotee like Lord Caitanya, or He sends His representative, the spiritual master, or pure devotee. This is also the planned activity of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. They come to canvass and educate, and thus a person in the illusory energy of the Supreme Lord gets a chance to mix with them, talk with them, and take lessons from them, and somehow or other if a conditioned soul surrenders to such personalities and by intimate association with them chances to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, he is saved from the material conditions of life. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.21.27, purport\]

The conclusion is that the accumulation of many lifetimes of pious activities (*sukṛti*) rewards one with good fortune (*bhāgya*), which imbues the heart with faith (*śraddhā*) and respect for saintly souls and their association. This *śraddhā*, faith, gradually produces faith in devotional service and chanting, eradication of unwanted material desires in the heart, steadfastness in devotional service, taste for it, single-minded attachment, and, finally, attraction and love for Kṛṣṇa. In the lifetime when good fortune shines on the *jīva,* he is endowed with *śraddhā*, faith. Thus, *śraddhā* and saintly association are glorified as the root cause of all benedictions. \[*Daśa-mūla-tattva*\]

Kali-yuga is certainly an age bereft of fortune. But following in the footsteps of Lord Nityānanda and of his immediate spiritual predecessors, Śrīla Prabhupāda has carried the torchlight of fortune to this dark place. Śrīla Prabhupāda was not an armchair guru who simply did his *bhajana* and waited for the few rare, fortunate souls of this world to approach him. Rather, being the embodiment of the verse *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā/ amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*(*Śikṣāṣṭaka* 3), he compassionately went out to dispense fortune by canvassing the world, allowing unqualified persons to initially mix with him, talk with him, and take lessons from him. This indeed proved to be the formula for actualizing Lord Caitanya’s vision that His holy name would be chanted in every town and village on the globe.

Śrīla Prabhupāda requested his followers to similarly go out and make others fortunate.

There are many fortunate persons all over the world, and many unfortunate persons also. So those who are fortunate, they’re taking to this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, this ideal life, hopeful life, pleasant life, blissful life, life of knowledge. They’re taking to it. But it is the duty of Vaiṣṇava to go door to door to make them fortunate. Although they are unfortunate, but you have to go door to door to make them fortunate. That is your duty. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture in New York, 21 July 1971\]

Suppose a man accidentally comes in contact with our society and gives some service willingly or unwillingly, that is the beginning of his fortunate life. We go from door to door canvassing people to join Kṛṣṇa Consciousness movement means that we are trying to make them fortunate. \[Letter to Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa, 13 November 13, 1968\]

On this most holy day of the appearance anniversary of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, we pray that we may eternally be engaged in executing his order to make others fortunate by distributing his books, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and the holy name. Thus when the people’s *bhakty-unmukhī sukṛti* accumulates, they can happily become souls completely surrendered at His Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples and followers at The Krishna Center, in Urbana, Illinois, USA.  
(written by Dānakelī Devī Dāsī)

## Krishna Culture

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, savior of the most fallen!

How can we ever, ever repay you for all the mercy we have received over the years, along with millions of others as well? Our vision can hardly comprehend it all. It is only because of your inconceivable dedication, devotion, and purity of heart that ISKCON began to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s transcendental mission back in the sixties. By your transcendental potency a Golden Age of Satya-yuga will manifest during this dark Age of Kali, and your books will be the law books for the next ten thousand years.

Today there are many searching souls ready to take up spiritual life and are just waiting for devotees to find them. There is no doubt that there is a big increase in spiritual interest worldwide, but most people are misdirected in their search. Only your mercy can give light and life to these searching souls.
