Please let us be part of your transcendental rescue team, the *saṅkīrtana* mission, finding and directing devoted souls to Lord Caitanya’s movement. We humbly pray at your lotus feet that we can assist you in this wonderful service.

Your grateful servants at Krishna Culture, in Houston, Texas, USA.

## Māyāpur Institute

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on your auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā celebration. All glories to your transcendental books, which are bringing light to the people of this dark Age of Kali.

Since first opening its doors in February 2000, the Mayapur Institute for Higher Education and Training has made a good deal of progress:

- 361 students have received Bhakti-śāstrī degrees.
- 182 students have attended the professionally designed VTE Teachers Training Courses.
- Over 5,000 students have enjoyed yearly seminars and workshops, ranging from leadership and management training, Deity worship, and *Bhagavad-gītā* to fund-raising and Āyurveda.
- The MIHET currently serves over 2,000 students every year with courses ranging from four-day seminars to thirteen-week intensive śāstric degree-oriented courses.
- Many devotees taking Bhakti-śāstrī, Bhaktivaibhava, and Teacher Training courses at the Māyāpur Institute have gone back to their home temples and started Bhakti-śāstrī and Bhaktivaibhava courses.
- Śāstrically trained and spiritually empowered devotees who have graduated from our Bhakti-śāstrī courses are enthusiastically preaching throughout the world.

We ask for your blessings and mercy to continue to expand our educational offerings to ISKCON devotees from around the world and thus strengthen your movement.

Your servants at the Māyāpur Institute.

## Padayātrā Worldwide

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Recently, on the occasion of the Ardha Kumbha-melā held at Allahabad, ISKCON had a big pandal set up. Part of our Bullock Cart Padayātrā party was participating in the festivities and preaching there. While attending Kumbha-melā this year, I was reminded of your divine presence thirty years ago, in January 1977. I have fond memories of those days, especially of your interactions with our Bullock Cart Padayātrā Saṅkīrtana party.

Our bullock-cart party had our own tent compound. There was some space in the front where we had our cart parked. When you came to our bullock-cart traveling-*saṅkīrtana* campsite, you inspected our cart and bulls.

First you stood in front of the cart and looked up at the sign. With your eyes fixed on the sign, you put your head down on your left shoulder and then moved your head slowly in a circular motion as you read the sign out loud: “Bhaktivedanta Bullock Cart Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party.”

As you read the last word, your head was resting on your right shoulder. Then you said, “*Jaya!*”

You walked a few steps, picked a handful of grass, and began to feed the bulls. You patted them on the cheeks and then turned to us and said, “They won’t take any more births, because they are carrying Gaura-Nitāi from village to village.”

One day during your stay at the *melā* I had long conversation with you. Just the two of us were sitting together in your tent. You were anxious to hear how everything was progressing with the Bullock Cart Saṅkīrtana Party. Three months earlier in Vṛndāvana you had inaugurated the party, and now you wanted a full report.

As I was giving my report, you listened with great pleasure. You were responding as if there was nothing more important to you than listening to the Bullock Cart Saṅkīrtana report. You were encouraging me to say more by your expressions and gestures. You wanted me to tell you where we stayed, how we kept clean, what our morning program consisted of. I told you how we got up early in the morning and bathed — either by dipping into a nearby river or pond or by drawing water from the village well and throwing a few buckets over ourselves. We held *maṅgala-ārati* at four-thirty, and sometimes, just after *maṅgala-ārati,* we would go through the village chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and singing the song *Jīva Jāgo* (“O sleeping souls, wake up!”). I explained how, when we did this, both adults and children would come running straight from their beds. The sound of the drum, the cymbals, and the holy name reminded them of Kṛṣṇa and of their culture, and they were invariably happy. You were especially pleased to hear how we depended on Kṛṣṇa, on whatever the villagers provided us.

The more I spoke, the more enlivened you became. A big smile was radiating from your face. You liked the simplicity and dependence on Kṛṣṇa that the Bullock Cart Saṅkīrtana program displayed. If there is anything, even thirty years later, that gets me inspired to keep pushing on the Padayātrā Worldwide program, it is this memory of how pleased you were to hear the report about its beginning days.

The feeling I get from remembering your pleasure is something that has stayed with me over thirty years. I have a vivid image in my mind of you beaming with pleasure that day in the Kumbha-melā tent. It continues to give me fresh strength and inspiration.

Soon after that Kumbha-melā, in a letter dated 16 March 1977, you wrote to Nityānanda Dāsa:

Here in India our Lokanatha Maharaja has successfully organized such a program, and it is a great success. He has traveled all over India, and everywhere they distribute books, *prasadam* and perform *kirtana*, village to village. Each night they stop at a different village. We can introduce many millions of such carts all over the world.

You are known for your big thinking — “many millions of such carts all over the world.” It is inconceivable. However, as you have said so, it is our full conviction that it will be realized sooner or later.

On the occasion of your appearance day, we beg for spiritual strength and determination to march forward to realize your Padayātrā vision worldwide. We beg to remain your humble Padayātrīs.

Your servants at Padayātrā Worldwide.  
(written by Lokanātha Swami)

## The Pāṇḍavas’ Paradise

Dear grandfather, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please give me shelter under your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In these times of rapid change, we struggle to adapt and understand how to better present your teachings to this very troubled world. There are so many variables involved that we easily get confused. Yet our hearts burn with a desire to share with others this wonderful gift you have given us.

Do we safely repeat the methods of the past? Even as we see weak results? Do we dare come up with new and untried methods?

The great advantage of such times of change is that we quickly come to the conclusion that we need your shelter and guidance. Thus I beg you to please make your wisdom manifest in my heart, in my intelligence, and in my actions. Please guide me, since you have put me in such a position of leadership. Please help me and bless me.

Your servants at The Pāṇḍavas’ Paradise, Brazil.  
(written by Giridhārī Dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Please help us to feel your presence in your physical absence and give us the intelligence by which we can come to be with you eternally.

This year marks the thirtieth year we have been separated from your company. You left us a legacy of devotional practices and literature to sustain our fledgling spiritual lives. Some devotees have connected to you through your *vāṇī* and have made significant progress along the *bhakti* path. Others have strayed from your teachings and the practices you prescribed for curing our material afflictions. You told us not to be surprised at people who leave but rather to be surprised at the devotees who stay.

It is so rare to find a person who accepts that Kṛṣṇa is God, and rarer still to find someone who accepts Lord Caitanya as God. So having these two points in common with any person in this world is a very special connection. And even more extraordinary is to find those persons who follow in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda. How fortunate we are to live in a community, Prabhupāda Village, where the residents have these things in common! If we can focus on these points of commonality rather than our differences, we will flourish by your grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We don’t want to build a temple out of wood and stone that has no heart. The love and encouragement we show one another is the heart of the temple. When we please you by our service attitude, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our atrophied hearts can begin to feel spiritual emotions. Along with a heart, the temple and society need a brain. You taught us that philosophy and sentiment must both be there. While loving all the devotees, we need to be able to identify and expunge deviant philosophies that may subtly creep into our midst. This requires that we study your books very carefully and keep good company.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has given us the keys to spiritual advancement — to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa with focus and attention, and to practice *jīva doyā*, kindness toward all living entities. The more devotees that apply this formula, the more success we will witness not only in our small community but throughout the world.

The more we give to others, in a manner that is pleasing to you, the more we get. And what is that getting? We make progress on the *bhakti* path. Our material affections wane as our spiritual affinities increase. This is a barometer of our progress. We should be ever vigilant to insure we are going up rather than down in our devotional journey. You are there to help us ever step of the way.

Nothing glorifies you more, Śrīla Prabhupāda, than your disciples who have become spiritually successful. Someone once asked if you had performed any miracles. In response to this inquiry you pointed to your disciples. We were your miracles. By your spiritual potency you transformed hippies into happies. We are also your legacy for one another and for the next generation of devotees. *Evaṁ paramparā-prāptam:* this supreme science is received through the chain of disciplic succession. We thank you for the many inspirational disciples you left behind to help guide us in your absence, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your disciples and granddisciples at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.

## Radio Krishna Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. Even if totally unqualified, we have again, on this holiest day, been given the opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace through your Vyāsa-pūjā book.

Books are meant to be written in glorification of great personalities — the Supreme Lord and His devotees. Actually, any book that does not contain such glorification is useless and in fact deleterious.

This Vyāsa-pūjā book glorifies Your Divine Grace, a crest jewel among the pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa, a *mahā-bhāgavata,* an *uttama-adhikārī,* the *jagat-guru,* and *mahā-patita-pāvana.* Therefore this book is most beneficial for everyone to read. Even Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa glorifies His pure devotee above everyone else. Therefore no glorification of Your Divine Grace can be considered excessive.

Because your extensive transcendental literature is the expression of your pure devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, it is by no means different from Your Divine Grace. As you said, “I will never die; I will live forever in my books.” So, glorification of your books and of their distribution, above everything else, has a profound meaning and can never be considered excessive.

Just to give us a little hint, here are few excerpts from some of your letters:

My first concern is that my books shall be published and distributed profusely all over the world. Practically, books are the basis of our movement. Without our books our preaching will have no effect. \[To Maṇḍalībhadra 20 January 1972\]

Through the distribution of this literature, we can completely annihilate all the bogus yogi groups in the world. Of this fact I am sure. Therefore there is no need for us to directly attack these charlatans, but simply by this distribution of books the sunlight of Krishna consciousness will prevail. \[To Rāmeśvara, 14 February 1973\]

Practically our society means books, so if there are no books how can we preach? \[To Bali Mardan, 28 December 1971\]

These books are so much potent that anyone who reads them is sure to become Krishna Conscious. They are so nice. So we want to make propaganda in that way. And if you are enthusiastic and sincere in your efforts, then Krishna will give you all facility. \[To Prahlādānanda, 17 June 1971\]

We must become now very serious to save the people of the world from this very dangerous situation. So become very convinced yourself of our philosophy and then your preaching will act. And try to sell as many books as possible at all the schools and colleges. This is scholarly information, flawless science. Kindly assist me in this great work, and know it for certain, that by your sincerely working in this way you shall very soon go back to Home, back to Godhead. \[To Locan, 24 November 1971\]

The basis of ISKCON, your foremost concern,  
Your books’ publication, pure *bhakti* to yearn.  
Without it — your purpose — to teach is pretension,  
Just poor imitation with no comprehension.  
By books’ distribution the fakes are exposed,  
The truth soon prevails, real facts are disclosed.  
No need for attacking the charlatans’ group:  
Now Kṛṣṇa’s revealed and you’ve ruined their soup.  
In practice your ISKCON is founded on books.  
If our aim is to preach we cannot overlook.  
It is book distribution that’s needed the most,  
Above our short speech, and without it we’re lost.  
Your books are so potent — all have to concede —  
Make conscious of Kṛṣṇa whoever will read,  
Infused with *śakti,* this world will transcend.  
They’re your perfect *deṣṇa,* the soul will comprehend.  
These books’ propagation the whole world will save,  
Will please Prabhupāda. This Kṛṣṇa’s love wave  
Will bring everyone to our Lords’ place  
In Vṛndāvana-dhāma with His Divine Grace!

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, we wholeheartedly pray that the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and all His transcendental associates will bless us with the ability to become instrumental in increasing your book distribution. In this way Your Divine Grace will be pleased and bestow your causeless mercy upon us all. Your mercy is all we are made of.

Your menial servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi Dāsa)

## Tauranga

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We are so grateful to you. The fact is that we are indebted even beyond our comprehension. The things you have accomplished, and still continue to accomplish through your teachings and those dedicated to propagating those teachings, shine as your torchlight of knowledge to expose the criminal impersonalists who lurk in the darkness of ignorance, denying the attributes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa-candra like common thieves and standardizing such thievery for others to follow. You expose them all, in numerous ways.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, simply by describing the transcendental form of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His wonderfully attractive pastimes and your insights into Vedic culture, you educate the people of this world and free us all from the horrible, empty, faceless, formless calamity that is the impersonal conception of God.

In a letter to Satsvarūpa Mahārāja regarding a book he proposed to write, your secretary quoted you:

“You should go positively forward instead of attacking others. It will create a section of enemies. We may criticize with our mouth but not put it in writing. Let us prove by action that all others are failures. To criticize means to give some importance-a rival to Hare Kṛṣṇa. Our criticism is that as soon as we see there is no Kṛṣṇa consciousness, it is rejected. A rascal is beautiful so long he doesn’t speak. Just like rice — press one grain, and if it is not soft you can understand it is all uncooked. Therefore, one word, like ‘probably,’ means they are finished. There is a proverb in Bengali: ‘If you kill a skunk, your hand gets a bad smell.’ If you attack others, some bad smell will be there. Keep your position respectable. Why should I try to understand ̮̮̮̮’s philosophy? I know from the beginning it will be useless. I therefore say, ‘I do not know these.’ Let others present the philosophy of ̮̮̮̮, and then you smash it. You crush it by kicking. Otherwise, your hand will get a bad smell. In other words, when others bring up the name of ̮̮̮̮, you can ask, ‘What is his philosophy?’ After it is described, then you can smash it.” \[6 July 1977\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, people ask us, “From where did you learn all these things?” and with a mixture of pride in you and humility in our following you, we inform them it is you who are worthy of praise, not us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a true revolutionary, a freedom fighter in the true sense. You exposed the cheating so-called scientists’ ploy to replace God and become God. You exposed their elaborate and costly scams of moon-landing fame long before the conspiracy theorists of the world saw through the lies. You clearly stated that life comes from life and not from a big bang or a random mixing of chemicals, and you made a monkey out of Darwin by stating:

As stated in the *Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa*, there is a gradual evolutionary process, but it is not the body that is evolving. All the bodily forms are already there. It is the spiritual entity, or spiritual spark within the body, that is being promoted by the laws of nature under the supervision of superior authority. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.73\]

So many environmental issues you exposed: the rape and exploitation of the resources of Mother Earth through the greed for minerals and fossil fuels, the uncaring pollution of water, air, and earth, the wanton destruction of forests, the lungs of the earth, and the encroachment upon the animals ecosystems. You warned of it all — how the *ugra-karma* of industrialization caused social chaos, how animal slaughter caused continual wars, how exploitation and abuse of Mother Earth caused climate change and other calamities, and so on. Unlike the many so-called leaders of the world, you gave simple, sustainable positive alternatives through *daivī-varṇāśrama-dharma*, cow protection, propagation of vegetarianism, simple living and high thinking, and God-centered living.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this short homage to you there is no way we can glorify all your exploits, any more than Ananta Śeṣa, with His unlimited mouths, can fully glorify the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Still, it is our duty, out of appreciation for your amazing depth of compassion, to show what you are saving the world from, and what actually is the purport to *nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi* in your *praṇāma-mantra*, to give credit where credit is due and bring at least some of your glories to light.

We’ve heard of the devotee at the Kumbha-melā in Allahabad (circa 1971) who, using your philosophy, exposed the falsehood of the philosophy of exclusive oneness (*aneka-jīvada*) being taught there by a Māyāvādī. When the Māyāvādī saw that your disciple was dressed in a nice wool *chaddar,* he gestured that he should share that warmth. The empowered disciple pulled out one small thread from the chaddar and handed it to the Māyāvādī, declaring that if the part is equal to the whole, as he was claiming, then the Māyāvādī should be very happy and warm with that single thread.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you strongly addressed the various aspects of Māyāvāda philosophy, especially the misconception that Brahman is true and everything else is false. You would flex your philosophical muscles and smash this through disclosing the truth behind *brahma satyaṁ jagan mithyā*. You stated that the world is real but temporal, not false. In one swipe you smashed the misguided followers of Śaṅkarācārya. You corrected so nicely the boy who came forward at the end of one of your lectures claiming he was God. The Māyāvādīs say, “Ignorance is a powerful magician in the human mind. It makes invisible that which is most visible — that we are all God.” You showed a more powerful magic that awoke millions to the realization that the impersonalists are simply dyslexic: they think they are God, but actually they are dog, as pointed out to that boy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you address the so-called educational systems of the world today that are rife with the assumption that ideas formed on the basis of sense perception are paramount. Such educational systems therefore deny that the Vedic authority is supreme and conclusive. Instead, the loudest dog’s barking becomes the direction to follow, and the followers of such philosophies reserve the option of not accepting anything as true, claiming that “it’s all subjective.” You showed us that such misconceptions are simply a ploy to exploit the innocent, to be irresponsible and unaccountable to God, and that in the ultimate issue exponents of such ideas all fall into a ditch.

Until you came to the West, impersonalism had poisoned the world for generations, but you made it so easy to defeat it: we just repeated what you taught us. If we don’t know who our father is, we don’t have to go and ask every man on the planet, “Are you my father? Are you my father?” We seek out the authority of one who knows, our mother. You taught us to ask from one who actually knows and not just anyone; you taught us to inquire about the Absolute Truth from the *śāstra*.

The path of subjective hearing teaches that what is pleasing to the hearer is truth, that my perception of reality determines its existence or lack thereof, and that everything is relative and subject to debate. Thus the impersonalists arrogantly present that the reason we see things the way we do is because of a deluded mind; therefore nothing exists unless we think it does. To see through this illusion is salvation. They say this way to salvation alone is real, but you asked, “When you get salvation, then what?” Having rejected Kṛṣṇa, the all-attractive Personality of Godhead, they cannot enjoy the bliss of devotional service, and so instead of remaining on the impersonal Brahman platform, which is so dry, the try to do good deeds and open hospitals to relieve the suffering of others.

By your grace we had some fun with a proponent of *yathā mata tathā patha* —all paths lead to the same result. In support of their slogan, one of their *sannyāsīs* had read, as his elderly women followers mentioned, “Swamijīs *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*” and we humbly asked if he’d read the purports too.

He replied, “Oh no, I couldn’t, too one directional . . .”

We asked, “Mahārāja, if as you say all paths lead to the same result, then why are you neglecting the *bhakti* path?”

He said, “They are all one.”

We remembered the comment you had made in childlike innocence to a person who made a similar comment during question time at a program in Hyderabad in 1975. Your response to the notion of oneness was simple: “I disagree: therefore there are two!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the wisdom that you possess proves your worth as one of the great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*. All the *ācāryas* made their commentaries on *Vedānta-sūtra*. Your life, your purports, your lectures, your letters, your instructions, and so on are the *Bhaktivedānta-sūtra*, where all questions are answered.

Your personal dealings were so sharp that no one could pull the wool over your eyes. Some tried, but you, “the old Calcutta boy,” could deal with everything and everyone. Your disciples you educated and trained so nicely, while those like Mr. Nair you destroyed.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, underwent so many trials and tribulations, without which we would not be here today. You taught effectively how to do everything simply by keeping Kṛṣṇa in the center. He is the controlling department, we are the service department. Rather than trying to manipulate the material energy in an effort to overcome tribulation, we should all take shelter of Kṛṣṇa.

You taught that gratitude is an essential factor in personalism. Without it, life is not worth living. You showed this to your disciple Bhavānanda Mahārāja as he helped you across the drainage ditch embankment in Māyāpur. After he’d helped you reach the top, you threw his hand away, then took one step, turned, and explained that this is how the impersonalists treat the guru: they reject the one who has given them everything, thinking they have become God and no longer need him.

We clearly remember your last visit to Bhaktivedanta Manor in 1977. You gave yourself to us all, going round the room interacting with us one by one. You had come to save us and to imprint your love and compassion on our hearts. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are still doing this today, interacting with all the devotees in different ways according to their nature and service, and in so doing you enthuse all of us to serve you more and more. We are fortunate to have so many sources to draw memories of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — from our minuscule personal association, from those who had much personal association, and from those who serve you in separation, carrying your teachings and sharing realizations. In this way you have re-defined personalism, giving it deepest meaning possible based on interaction and love.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have given up all other *dharmas* and so-called important worldly goals, such as mundane education and economic development. We have sacrificed our youth, earning capacity, and time for you and have accepted your guidance above mundane expectation.

Where others failed in saving us, offering us the broken glass of illusory material things, you offered us the real diamond, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for the small price of sacrifice. This is the supremely successful way that you saved so many. We you are eternally grateful to you, whether we are disciples or granddisciples. Everything we find, everything we have, we bring to you, to your philosophy, the life-style you have given us, the template for validation or rejection.

Please continue to bless us all. Please never kick us away, even though we are so much lacking in realization and practical application of your teachings. Please empower us further so that we can do something truly wonderful, as you have done.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote in your memorable poetic Vyāsa-pūjā offering of 1935 to your beloved Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda:

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved,  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast removed.

Please allow us also to carry that banner into the battle and smash asunder the enemy, impersonalism.

Standing before you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel great humility, honestly understanding that we are lowly and from the fallen pool of humankind, having entered this world with a “bloop,” as a pebble enters a pool of water. Being reclaimed, we are yours. We follow your disciplines, your teachings, and your style. We are Prabhupādānugas, your children and grandchildren, totally dependent upon you and following your directives. We are like pearls strung on a thread, your thread. We are proud to be so and would consider ourselves most fortunate if you would continue engaging us in your devotional service until the final breath. Thank you from the core of our hearts. Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*! We beg to remain yours in the service of your devotees.

The servants of your servants at the Western Bay of Plenty community, Katikati-Tauranga, New Zealand.  
(written by Jaya Tīrtha Charan Dāsa)

## The Ultimate Self Realization Course

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to unto you, the most potent spiritual master in the history of the universe. You stand unique among all *ācāryas.* You have accomplished what no other *ācārya* ever accomplished. You successfully distributed Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Even Lord Caitanya, who could easily have done this, did not do so. He wanted you, His dearmost devotee, to get the credit for having inundated the entire world with this amazing tidal wave of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti.*

By your mercy, in December of 2002 I experimented with starting a Kṛṣṇa consciousness training program, an e-course, on the Internet. Since Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the ultimate self-realization system, I named the course the Ultimate Self Realization Course. By your mercy, the program has expanded like anything. There are now over six thousand people enrolled in over a hundred countries. In order to provide the best possible support for my readers, I am now traveling and preaching all the over the world practically full time to meet as many of them as possible, to encourage and help them fully surrender themselves at the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

I have never before tasted such wonderful, sweet happiness. This is all due to your mercy because you are engaging me constantly in propagating your mission. I have unlimited preaching work to do every day. I have weekly lessons and daily meditations to write. Plus every day I have a large volume of correspondence to answer, responding to the questions of my students from all over the world. While all of this writing is going on, I am also simultaneously traveling and preaching in an ever-expanding number of countries around the world. I usually give two lectures a day, morning and evening, and as I write this Vyāsa-pūjā homage I am en-route to Dortmund, Germany, to give an evening lecture for an ISKCON *nāma-haṭṭa* gathering.

When I remember how hard you worked, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for spreading this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, I become greatly inspired. You never retired from the battlefield, even when you were on your deathbed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I beg from you a benediction. Please bless me that I can always be purely absorbed in spreading your mission all over the world. Please bless me, if you so desire, that I can live to see the day when the entire world becomes Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Constantly falling in dust of your lotus feet and begging for your mercy, we remain  
Your unworthy but grateful eternal servants at The Ultimate Self Realization Course.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa Dāsa)

## Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E. (Counseling, Assistance, Resources, and Education for the Terminally Ill and Their Families)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, our beloved spiritual master, on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

On behalf of your disciples and granddisciples around the world who volunteer for Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E., we humbly thank you for your continued inspiration and inner guidance in caring for your devotees at the end of their lives. Three decades ago many of us were practically children when we sat before you, hearing you tell us how the so-called happiness in this world was nothing more than the absence of suffering. You often said that at the time of death we would each be “flying our own plane.” Being so young and immature in our spiritual lives, we barely understood the true meaning of your words. After all, our youthful bodies were strong and healthy, and we had our entire lives ahead of us. Now, as the years quickly slip by, we see more and more of your beloved disciples pass away from this world and how, on their death beds, each one is truly flying his or her own plane. Being caregivers to some of them, we now observe for ourselves what you tried hard to explain so many years ago, and as we ourselves age, your words mean so much more than they did when we were young.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are not even close to the age you were when you journeyed to America, yet we are often heard complaining to one another about minor aches and pains as if they were intolerable. Then we stop and remember you, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. We stop our complaining and speak in amazement, recounting your long voyage across the ocean on the *Jaladuta,* when you suffered two heart attacks. We think of how, in your later years, you circled the globe countless times while building this worldwide movement of temples and farm communities. We discuss how you went from lengthy, exhausting plane rides to giving arrival addresses in the temple room and then attending endless meetings with your disciples and guests. We remember how, through much of the night — every night — you tirelessly translated the essential Vedic literatures into English and wrote your invaluable purports to leave as your gifts to this world for innumerable years to come. Now, as our bodies age and wither, we remember these exemplary activities with great awe and humility, for only a transcendental visitor from Kṛṣṇaloka could have performed such miraculous achievements. In disbelief, we thank you for allowing us to be present while you softly yet powerfully walked upon this earth, and how, by your mercy, we were able to see you.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day, we pray to continue our service to your beloved devotees. We pray for your blessings so that each and every one of your disciples and granddisciples will forever remain safe in the cooling shade of your divine lotus feet.

The servants of your servants at Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E.  
(written by Saṅgītā Devī Dāsī)
